## T-020 — e2e WebSocket transport tests against a loopback server (queued by user)

- **Branch:** `feat/ws-transport-tests` (queued 2026-11-01, cut from develop @ `d0810d2`; worktree `zt-ws-transport-tests`)
- **Origin:** the transport (`WebSocketClient` + the adapter's `HandleWebSocketMessage`) has only ever been validated by runtime probes against the real stream host — that gap bit at T-013, when the combined-envelope framing bug survived every unit suite until a live probe caught it. This task closes it with in-test infrastructure: a real WebSocket server inside the test host, no network.
- **Scope:** `tests/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure.Tests` ONLY (a loopback server helper + two suites). Product code must not change. The test csproj gains a `Microsoft.AspNetCore.App` framework reference solely to host Kestrel in-process.
- **What:** run the whole transport path inside the test host — adapter → `WebSocketClient` → `ClientWebSocket` → loopback Kestrel server → server-pushed frames → envelope parsing → published observables — so reconnect and framing regressions fail in CI instead of in a manual probe.
- **Design (decided):**
  - `LoopbackWebSocketServer` helper: minimal `WebApplication` (Kestrel) bound to `127.0.0.1:0` — the dynamic port is read from the bound address after `StartAsync`, so binds never collide; `UseWebSockets` on the `/stream` path. Each accepted connection runs a fragment-aware receive loop (mirroring the client) that queues whole messages for the test; the test can push text frames, close the server side with a full closing handshake, kill the connection hard, and count sessions (reconnect evidence). Console logging providers are stripped — the server never writes to stdout (MCP rule territory).
  - Suite 1 — `WebSocketClientTests`, the reconnect/decode units; the client is built directly with a short `reconnectDelay` because the adapter hardcodes the production 5 s: `ConnectAsync` returns after the OPEN handshake with `ConnectionStateStream` true; server-pushed frames arrive verbatim on `MessageStream`; a server-side fragmented message (UTF-8 sequence split mid-codepoint) decodes as ONE message (pins the fragment accumulation); a graceful server close flips the state to false and a fresh session reconnects after the short delay — new frames flow again and the session count increments (the reconnect loop is exercised, not assumed); an unbound loopback port fails `ConnectAsync` with `InvalidOperationException` after the configured attempts (connection refused is immediate, no wall-clock sleeps); `SendAsync`/`SendJsonAsync` deliver to the server and inherit the not-connected guard.
  - Suite 2 — `BinanceAdapterWsE2eTests`, the T-013 regression on real sockets: the adapter is constructed with test endpoints (`ws://…/stream`); the first `SubscribeTicker(BTC/USDT)` drives the whole path — Kestrel handshake, the adapter's SUBSCRIBE frame lands on the server with `method`/`id`/the three `btcusdt@…` params, the server answers with `{"stream",…,"data"}` envelopes and the test asserts the published `Ticker` / `OrderBook` (bid/ask levels) / `MarketTrade` (`m`=maker-buy ⇒ `Side`=Sell, aggressor side) entities field by field in BOTH number encodings (numbers-as-strings and numbers-as-numbers — the `BinanceJsonNumbers` contract); a bare payload without the envelope publishes nothing (the raw-/ws frame drop stays pinned); the connection-state subscription keeps the adapter's `IsConnected` honest.
  - Reconnect pacing stays in suite 1; suite 2 never waits on the adapter's 5 s production defaults.
- **Acceptance:**
  - Everything runs against the in-process loopback server: no real hosts, no keys; every wait is bounded and polled (no flaky fixed sleeps).
  - gate.
- **Tests:** new `LoopbackWebSocketServer`; `WebSocketClientTests` (~6); `BinanceAdapterWsE2eTests` (~4).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commit `9599a59` is on develop (fast-forward merge). Outcome:
  - Shipped exactly the planned shape: `LoopbackWebSocketServer` (a minimal Kestrel `WebApplication`, console logging providers stripped — the host never writes to stdout) with a per-session-number waiter TCS, a fragment-aware receive loop mirroring the client's chunk accumulation, a `LoopbackConnection` handle for push/fragment/close, and the shared `TestPoll` bounded poll; suites `WebSocketClientTests` (6) and `BinanceAdapterWsE2eTests` (4).
  - Two design deltas settled while building: (1) the server does NOT read the port off Kestrel's reported bound address — the port is pre-reserved with a `TcpListener(:0)` and released before the bind, and `StartAsync` retries on 3 freshly reserved ports against `AddressAlreadyInUse`, failing hard (never silently) otherwise; (2) the refused-endpoint scenario uses the real server + a missing path (`/missing` answers plain HTTP, never an upgrade) instead of an unbound port — deterministic, no port-race arithmetic.
  - Pinned: verbatim in-order frames; mid-codepoint fragmented UTF-8 (the split lands inside the first 2-byte sequence) decodes as ONE message; graceful server close → states `[false,true,false,true]` with session #2 delivering frames again; the SUBSCRIBE frame shape (`method`/`id`/the three `btcusdt@…` params); ticker/book/trade entities field-by-field in BOTH number encodings; bare-frame and half-envelope drops with a valid tail probe — a silent leak shifts `Take(2)`'s first entry, so no sleeps.
  - Tests 207 → 217 (13 Core + 115 App + 58 Infra + 31 UI); the new suites ran green 3× straight (~0.5 s each run). GATE PASSED. Product code untouched; the test csproj gains `FrameworkReference Microsoft.AspNetCore.App` + `System.Reactive 7.0.0` for the in-process host.
  - No version bump (T-019 precedent: test-only tasks ship no user-facing change); alpha.16 stays queued for the next behavior-shipping task.

## T-021 — Cold-start polish: the first symbols apply lag (T-017 follow-up)

- **Branch:** `feat/cold-start-symbols` (queued 2026-11-02, cut from develop @ `7e217ed`; worktree `zt-cold-start-symbols`)
- **Origin:** the T-017 probe follow-up: the first symbols apply can lag ~4–10 s on an idle UI thread (attributed to a posted dispatcher job on a non-interacted window). This task measures the whole bootstrap chain and fixes the measured cause, not the first hypothesis. Cold start < 3 s is a standing performance target, so the combo must populate in that envelope.
- **What:** instrument and decide. The chain to timestamp on a real cold start (window show → combo populated): `AdapterChanges` first emission (the ChartViewModel ctor bootstrap runs during the walk AND again for the real window — both fire), the SymbolListProvider fetch start/finish (the apply posts only after the fetch), the `ObserveOnUiThread` Post submit vs execution, and the visible `Symbols loaded` log. The two candidate causes each have a distinct signature: (a) the post-executed-late signature blames the dispatcher (fix: apply inline on the UI thread or raise the apply priority); (b) the fetch-finished-late signature blames the network/parse — the adapter pulls the FULL unfiltered `/api/v3/exchangeInfo` (multi-MB JSON, status filtering happens after the whole body parses), so the fix is lighter/faster symbol data (e.g. the tiny `ticker/price`-style name list for the combo, keeping the Core `GetSymbolsAsync` contract, or a streaming parse), decided by measurement.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.App` (apply path, WeakSymbolBootstrap refinement) + potentially one small `BinanceAdapter` endpoint change (the `GetSymbolsAsync` CONTRACT does not move). Probe-only changes (temporary logging) never commit product; the probe's reported timings go into the Status block.
- **Acceptance:**
  - Probe: on a real cold start the combo is populated within the 3 s cold-start envelope (UIA-visible), the recorded timings in the Status block make the cause unambiguous.
  - Tests for whatever behavior ships (the fetch/apply split, a double-bootstrap dedup, the endpoint change if taken).
  - Branch tail: the `Directory.Build.props` suffix bump alpha.15 → alpha.16 + `CHANGELOG.md` (behavior-shipping: the combo arrives faster).
  - gate.
- **Tests:** new `SymbolListProviderTests`/`ChartViewModelTests` additions pin the shipped slice.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commit `20cbb5d` + the release tail `d1105f2` are on develop (fast-forward merge). Outcome:
  - Measured before fixing: the T-017 dispatcher hypothesis is disproven — the user's own 11-01 logs show the apply lands ~16 ms after the fetch completes (`ObserveOnUiThread` posts promptly even on a non-interacted window); the entire wait was the FETCH BODY itself. The App/apply path shipped untouched.
  - Shipped: `BinanceAdapter.GetSymbolsAsync` now draws the symbol catalog from `/api/v3/ticker/price` (~160 KB / 0.82 s raw) instead of the full unfiltered `/api/v3/exchangeInfo` (~17.8 MB / 7.8 s raw, 1.2–3.7 s in-app; the rejected middle option `exchangeInfo?permissions=SPOT&showPermissionSets=false` still measured 6.7 MB / 3.3 s). The Core `GetSymbolsAsync` contract does not move.
  - Name parsing: a Binance-LOCAL quote table `BinanceQuoteAssets` (sorted longest-first at static init via `SortBinanceQuoteAssets`) splits ticker names into base/quote; USD1, AED, KZT and the live one-letter `U` quote added after live probes (`SUIU` confirmed TRADING with quoteAsset "U"). Unrecognized quotes are DROPPED and counted in ONE aggregated warning (52 delisted remnants), never guessed — matching the old status-filter semantics (PAX/UST/IDRT/USDSOLD/EURI stay dropped).
  - Tests 217 → 220 (13 Core + 115 App + 61 Infra + 31 UI): 3 new tests in `BinanceAdapterParsingTests` pin the ticker/price endpoint (via the `StubHandler.LastRequest` capture), the name split and the unknown-quote drop. GATE PASSED; resx parity 95/95 (en/ru).
  - Probe (final build, run 4): window show → `Symbol list cached: 3693` (+0.49 s) → `Symbols loaded: 50` (+0.56 s) → chart bars delivered (+0.88 s) → WS connected (~1.5 s) — inside the < 3 s cold-start target (was 2.4–4.2 s).
  - Probe-artifact note: runs 1/3 (the same build that later flew in runs 2/4) froze 4–6 s after an HTTP fetch completed with ZERO further log lines — unreproduced, attributed to probe mechanics, not code; never pin behavior on a single frozen run.
  - Branch tail: `Directory.Build.props` alpha.15 → alpha.16; `CHANGELOG.md` gains the alpha.16 section (`### Changed (T-021 — the symbols catalog source)`). Correction made at T-022: this task's in-file comment was left stale by the tail (it still said "next is alpha.16") — the rewrite landed with the alpha.17 bump instead.

## T-022 — Order-dialog header names the active exchange (T-016 follow-up)

- **Branch:** `feat/order-dialog-exchange` (queued 2026-11-02, cut from develop @ `4200b46`; worktree `zt-order-dialog-exchange`)
- **Origin:** the leftover T-016 UX rule: the toolbar selector can switch the active exchange at runtime, but the order dialog never names the exchange in play — after a switch the dialog floats context-free (the only naming surface is the status bar chip).
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.App` only: the dialog VM + the XAML header; the DI registration is untouched (the transient's largest ctor auto-resolves the facade). No Core contracts, NO new resx keys (see What). Existing test ctor call sites updated.
- **What (decided in the code walk):** trading always places via `PaperTradingAdapter` today (registered over the facade by `AddPaperTrading`), while the dialog's context — the symbol suggestions and reference prices — rides the ACTIVE exchange; T-016's rule stands that the dialog header must name the active exchange. Shape, mirroring the status-bar precedent (`MainWindow.axaml` `<Run Text="{Binding ActiveExchangeName}" />`):
  - `NewOrderDialogViewModel` gains `ActiveExchangeName` — ctor-threaded `ActiveExchangeService`; a subscription over `AdapterChanges` sets the property (the behavioral stream emits the current adapter on subscribe, so the bootstrap value is free) and live-updates on every toolbar switch; lifecycle mirrors the StatusBar pattern (activation-scoped disposables; the dialog is transient per open, and window disposal releases it).
  - XAML: a header line at the top of the dialog body — `<Run Text="{loc:Localized NewOrder.Title}"/>` + separator + `<Run Text="{Binding ActiveExchangeName, Mode=OneWay}"/>`. The static part stays in resx (language switching needs zero extra keys), the dynamic part rebinds. The window `Title` stays the plain localized one.
  - Honesty note: the header names the price/context source; the paper surface stays disclosed by the confirmation dialog (untouched here) — when real exchange trading lands (future board task), the header keeps following the same property.
- **Acceptance:**
  - Probe: open the order dialog (Binance active) → the header reads "New order — Binance"; switch to Demo via the toolbar while the dialog is open → the header flips to Demo without reopening.
  - Tests: `NewOrderDialogViewModelTests` additions — (1) the property bootstraps from the current adapter name; (2) an `AdapterChanges` emission updates it live (bounded-poll assertions, never immediate post-event asserts).
  - Branch tail: version bump alpha.16 → alpha.17 + `CHANGELOG.md` (user-facing: the dialog names its context; next suffix alpha.18).
  - gate.
- **Tests:** new cases in `NewOrderDialogViewModelTests`; ctor call sites updated.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commit `122e35a` + the release tail `b3f6ffa` are on develop via merge `1f7648c` (no fast-forward — the queue docs commit `fe0e372` had advanced develop past the cut base `4200b46`). Outcome:
  - Shipped exactly the planned shape: `NewOrderDialogViewModel.ActiveExchangeName` — ctor-threaded `ActiveExchangeService`, bootstrapped by the facade behavioral stream's emission zero (the stream emits its current adapter on subscribe) and updated live on every toolbar switch while the dialog stays open. XAML header = the localized title `Run` + separator + the live-name `Run` (status-bar precedent) — NO new resx keys, so runtime language switching stays free; the window `Title` keeps the plain localized string.
  - The app's first transient VM with scoped subscriptions: the dialog VM is now `IDisposable` — the facade subscription parks in a `CompositeDisposable` and the window releases it on `Closed` (no per-dialog observer leak; the singleton VMs from T-016 keep their lifetime-long subscriptions by design). DI registration untouched — the transient's largest ctor auto-resolves the facade.
  - Tests 220 → 222 (13 Core + 117 App + 61 Infra + 31 UI): 2 new tests in `NewOrderDialogViewModelTests` pin the bootstrap from the current adapter name and the live switch; `CreateViewModel` gained an `exchanges:` parameter and a two-adapter facade helper. GATE PASSED; resx parity untouched (95/95 — no new keys, as designed). The new tests assert the synchronous emissions directly (no bounded polling — there is no cross-thread event in the tested path: the facade emits synchronously inside `Activate`).
  - Probe (UIA, real network, ru locale): the dialog opened via the Trading menu → the header read `Новый ордер — Binance`; the toolbar combo switched to Demo WHILE THE DIALOG STAYED OPEN (the same window element dumped before and after — no reopen) → the header read `Новый ордер — Demo`; the status bar chip flipped `Binance · Paper` → `Demo · Paper` and the version chip read `0.1.0-alpha.17` in the same dump. Probe-infra notes for reuse: the modal Avalonia dialog is INVISIBLE to the PId top-level query — bind it via hwnd enumeration + `AutomationElement.FromHandle`; the menu item's real name carries an ellipsis (`Новый ордер…`), so match by prefix, not equality; screenshots were skipped this time (a bad `[int]` cast on the DPI-scaled rect in the probe script) — the text dumps carry the evidence.

## T-023 — Real second adapter: Bybit (read-only market data)

- **Branch:** `feat/bybit-adapter` (queued 2026-11-02, cut from develop @ `a5c9bfa`; worktree `zt-bybit-adapter`)
- **Origin:** the exchange story is single-vendor: the registered catalog carries one production adapter (Binance) plus the canned Demo fixture, and only the teardown surface has been touched across nine UI/AI tasks since T-016. The plugin-first design — small `IMarketDataAdapter`, transport settings inside the adapter, the T-015 endpoint-registry seam, the T-016 selector, the T-017 workspace context — has never been stressed by a second real implementation. This is the structural test. READ-ONLY: `IMarketDataAdapter` only, paper trading stays the sole order surface.
- **Exchange choice, measured 2026-11-02 (public REST, no keys, same terms as T-021):** Bybit spot vs Kraken:
  - Bybit `instruments-info?category=spot&limit=1000`: 282 KB / ~0.95 s, 520 pairs, ONE page (empty `nextPageCursor`), and every item SERVES `baseCoin`/`quoteCoin`/`status` — no Binance-style quote table needed, the split is exact metadata (`status == "Trading"`), unlike the priced-list approximation T-021 adopted for Binance.
  - Kraken `AssetPairs`: 680 KB / ~1.24 s, 1464 entries including margin noise, with the `XBT`-vs-`BTC` canonicalization nuisance.
  - Bybit wins on payload, entry quality and target-market fit (Binance-shaped pairs); the choice is easily inverted at review time if the vendor flips.
- **What (decided by the live WS probe):** `BybitAdapter : ExchangeAdapterBase`, mirroring the Binance structure (`WebSocketClient` transport + a named HTTP client + endpoints from the registry snapshot). Wire facts from the probe:
  - REST catalog: `GET /v5/market/instruments-info?category=spot&limit=1000` → `new Symbol(baseCoin, quoteCoin, Crypto)` per `status == "Trading"` entry (the Core `Symbol` registry already speaks the Bybit concatenated exchange format — `GetExchangeFormat("bybit")` ships today). A non-empty `nextPageCursor` follows with a bounded shipped page cap (ONE aggregated warning when the cap bites); malformed entries skip singly, never guess.
  - REST bars: `GET /v5/market/kline?category=spot&symbol={BASE}{QUOTE}&interval={map}&start&end&limit` → `[[startMs,"o","h","l","c","vol","turnover"],...]`, newest-first, ms as strings (shared `BinanceJsonNumbers` helper); interval map M1/5/15/30 → `1,5,15,30`, H1/H4 → `60,240`, D/W/MN1 → `D,W,M`; reversed to ascending (Binance's bar contract).
  - WS `wss://stream.bybit.com/v5/public/spot`, one shared socket; per-symbol subscribes ride ONE frame `{op:"subscribe",args:["tickers.X","orderbook.50.X","publicTrade.X"]}`; the ack frame (`"success":false`) logs loudly; dispatch by `topic` prefix with `{"topic","type":"snapshot|delta","ts","data"}`. Spot tickers are SNAPSHOT-typed (live-verified — no state merge), `publicTrade.X` is an event array (aggressor `S=Buy|Sell`, `T` ms epoch).
  - Order book: `orderbook.50` arrives snapshot then deltas with monotonic `u`; qty `"0"` == delete level (live-verified). `BybitOrderBookAssembler` (the genuinely new component): snapshot resets, deltas apply only when `u` == prev+1, a gap discards state and re-subscribes the topic for a fresh snapshot; publishes the FULL rebuilt depth each frame, capped at 50.
  - Spot tickers carry NO bid/ask (linear-only fields, live-verified): the mapped ticker keeps the honest absent-value (0), price = lastPrice — no guessed substitutes (the chart consumes price only; implications checked at build).
  - Keepalive: the infrastructure has NO app-level ping today; Bybit sockets dislike silence, so the adapter ships a small adapter-lifetime heartbeat (`op:"ping"` on a `PeriodicTimer`, owned by `DisposeAsync` — never a subscription token). A soak on the final build verifies it holds; if the stream proves self-warming, the keepalive stays as belt-and-braces.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure` (new `Bybit*` files; `ExchangeEndpointRegistry` gains the Bybit descriptor with the T-015 override semantics; `AddBybitAdapter()` in the transport aggregate), `src/ZettaTrade.App` (the active-exchange catalog array gains Bybit — one line), tests. Core contracts untouched. Demo adapter STAYS (cold-start fallback + test fixture; removal is not this task).
- **Acceptance:**
  - Probe: a real cold start shows THREE choices in the picker; switching to Bybit restores the chart/book context (the T-017 seams hold); Bybit ETH/BTC bars load and the WS ticker/trades/book flow live.
  - Tests pin the shipped slice: catalog parse (`status` filter, page-cap warning, malformed-entry skip), kline mapping, `BybitOrderBookAssembler` snapshot/delta/gap sequences, and the Bybit protocol shape e2e via the T-020 loopback server.
  - Branch tail: version bump alpha.17 → alpha.18 + `CHANGELOG.md` (behavior-shipping: a second real exchange).
  - gate.
- **Tests:** new `BybitAdapter*Tests` suites (reusing the T-020 `LoopbackWebSocketServer` and `StubHandler` helpers).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commits `0c799f4` (adapter) + `68559f1` (catalog) and the release tail `387a1cb` (alpha.18) are on develop via merge `07c476f` (`origin/feat/bybit-adapter`); develop is back in sync with `origin/develop`. Outcome:
  - Shipped exactly the planned shape: `BybitAdapter : ExchangeAdapterBase` — WebSocketClient transport + named HTTP client + endpoints from the T-015 registry snapshot; ONE multiplexed socket with all tracked symbols in one subscribe frame (`{op:"subscribe",args:["tickers.X","orderbook.50.X","publicTrade.X"]}`); reconnect re-subscribes the tracked set; a depth gap discards state and re-subscribes (`Subject<Symbol>` + `FromAsync().Catch().Switch()` — no fire-and-forget); the 20 s `op:"ping"` keepalive lives on a `PeriodicTimer` owned by `DisposeAsync` (never a subscription token); `BybitOrderBookAssembler` runs the snapshot + monotonic-`u` deltas with `"0"`-qty deletes and republishes the full rebuilt depth capped at 50.
  - Honesty preserved on the wire gaps: spot tickers are snapshot-typed with NO bid/ask (live-verified) — the mapped ticker keeps 0 for the absent fields, prices from `lastPrice` and carries `price24hPcnt × 100` as the 24 h percent; no guessed substitutes.
  - Catalog: `ExchangeEndpointRegistry` gains the Bybit descriptor (`BybitKey`, per-vendor override reads); `AddBybitAdapter()` in the transport aggregate; the App active-exchange catalog becomes `[Binance, Bybit, Demo]` — one line, the T-021 cold-start default stays Demo.
  - Tests 222 → 252 (13 Core + 117 App + 91 Infra + 31 UI): 40 targeted tests in `BybitAdapterParsingTests`, `BybitOrderBookAssemblerTests`, `BybitAdapterWsE2eTests` and the endpoint-registry suite (the T-020 loopback server). Pins: `status=="Trading"` filter, the single-page fast path, cursor paging capped at 5 pages with ONE aggregated warning, malformed entries skipped singly, `retCode != 0` throws, kline strings reversed to the ascending contract. The new suites caught a latent `BinanceJsonNumbers` defect on arrival — it now returns `Clone()`ed elements and gained `ParseInt64`. GATE PASSED; resx parity untouched (95/95 — no new UI strings, as designed).
  - Probe (UIA, real network): the picker shows THREE choices (`Binance | Bybit | Demo`); switching to Bybit live — real `api.bybit.com` kline 200 → 169 candles, 520 instruments, WS `Connected` to `wss://stream.bybit.com/v5/public/spot` with live book/ticker/trades; a persisted-`bybit` cold start restored end to end (catalog → WS → bars, ~2 s); a 150 s keepalive soak held — ONE `Connected`, no reconnects, no ping failures. Probe-infra note for reuse: the settings file lives in ROAMING (`AppData\Roaming\ZettaTrade\config\appsettings.json`) — the switch persisted `bybit` into the real settings (the probe's LOCALAPPDATA backup never fired, its path was wrong; restored and re-verified manually).

## T-024 — Fix the demo seeded-walk underflow (demo bar loads overflow)

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (discovered during the T-023 acceptance probe)
- **Origin:** the T-023 probe's cold start loads the persisted workspace symbol (BTC/USDT) on the persisted exchange (Demo — the T-021 default). The demo bars request died with `[ERR] ZettaTrade.App.ViewModels.ChartViewModel Failed to load chart data for BTC/USDT` + `System.OverflowException` (a `Decimal.op_Multiply` inside `DemoMarketDataAdapter.BuildBars`).
- **Root cause (proven by an instrumented reproduction):** `BarStep` computes `(Next(ref seed) % 2001 - 1000) / 100000m`; the subtraction happens in UINT semantics and UNDERFLOWS whenever `Next % 2001 < 1000` (wraps to ≈ +4 294 965 x), so ~25% of the steps come out as ≈ **+42 949 (≈ +42949% per bar)** instead of the documented ±1 %. The multiplicative walk explodes and dies on `Decimal` range; every demo symbol x full chart range (H1, 7 d lookback, limit 200) overflows: BTC/USDT, ETH/BTC, ETH/USDT, SOL/USDT all reproduce.
- **Pre-existing, not T-023:** the same instrumented reproduction overflows on a DETACHED `develop@a5c9bfa` build (`ZettaTrade.Infrastructure.dll` byte-same source, zero Bybit involvement). No `OverflowException` line exists in any Serilog log from Oct 18 to Nov 1 — earlier probes and workspace choices simply never drove a full demo-bars load until today.
- **What:** fix the step arithmetic (evaluate the modulo/subtraction in `long` semantics — the intended ±1 % step) and pin it: a regression test that loads a full chart-range of demo bars for every demo symbol (H1, limit 200) asserts a finite last close bounded by the documented walk; fix-shipping → alpha.19.
- **Scope:** `DemoMarketDataAdapter` + its test suite only. No Core, no Bybit regressions.
- **Acceptance:** the reproduction no longer overflows; the app cold-starts on the persisted BTC/USDT/Demo and delivers demo candles (the error line gone from the Serilog chronology); gate.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the fix commit `3f8f333` + the release tail `ce8f732` sit directly on develop — fast-forward (no merge commit; develop had only the T-023 docs commit `900740d` past the branch's cut base). Outcome:
  - The fix, scoped to `DemoMarketDataAdapter`: BOTH sites of the `% N - k` unsigned-wrap class now evaluate in `long` semantics — `BarStep` (the walk exploded full-range demo bar loads) and, discovered in the same sweep, `PriceChange24h` (the ticker's 24 h change: 4 of the 6 demo symbols — BTC/USDT, ETH/USDT, SOL/USDT, ETH/BTC — permanently displayed ≈ +429 496 729 %). The healthy path is untouched: the seeded sequences equal the wrapped build wherever the modulo lands ≥ the subtractand (same request ⇒ same bars preserved).
  - Tests 252 → 254 (13 Core + 117 App + 93 Infra + 31 UI): a full chart range (H1, 7 d, limit 200 → 169 candles) loads for EVERY demo symbol with every close inside the documented-walk bounds (`bars[0].Open × (0.99…1.01)^n`), and every ticker's 24 h change is pinned to the documented (−1 %, +1 %) band. GATE PASSED; resx parity untouched (95/95 — UI strings not involved).
  - Regression harness (the T-023 scratch repro, repointed at the fixed build): all six symbols walk 169 H1 bars with 78–95 wrapped steps each (the ~50 % wrap rate as diagnosed) — finite closes everywhere; the fixed adapter loads 169 bars per symbol without exception.
  - Probe (UIA + Serilog, persisted `ExchangeId: "demo"`): the cold start restored Demo, cached the 6-instrument catalog, served the whole demo universe in the chart combo, read the version chip `0.1.0-alpha.19`, and the exact former killer line flowed clean — `Chart bars load requested for BTC/USDT on Demo` → `Chart bars delivered: 168 candles`. Fresh log window: no `[WRN]`/`[ERR]`, no `OverflowException`, no `Failed to load chart data`.
  - Probe-infra note: the T-023 settings-path lesson is baked into the reusable probe (`t024-probe1.ps1`: Roaming `AppData\Roaming\ZettaTrade\config\appsettings.json`, offset-scoped log reading so only the probe's own lines count).

## T-025 — Click a book level to prefill the order dialog

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (worktree name `book-click` when cut)
- **Origin:** the T-011 book (60 fps) is read-only: to act on the level in front of their eyes the trader opens the dialog, retypes the symbol, flips the side and rekeys the price. The T-017 workspace context already binds the book to the dialog's exchange; a click closes the loop.
- **What:** pointer-down on a level hit-tests the widget's own row geometry (the Skia layout math, no re-layout) and raises `LevelClicked(Symbol, OrderSide, Price)` — bids are the side to BUY at that level, asks SELL; the routed request opens the SAME New Order dialog the menu uses with the VM prefilled BEFORE Show: symbol, side, price, quantity EMPTY (never assume size). The dialog stays fully editable; placement keeps riding IRiskManager + IConfirmationService untouched.
- **Scope:** `OrderBookWidget` (pointer + hit-test), the event route through the panel/`MainViewModel` (same plumbing as `NewOrderRequested`), the dialog prefill surface (the transient's largest ctor keeps auto-resolving; a prefill carrier type is added at App level), tests.
- **Acceptance:** probe: with BTC/USDT live, click a mid-book bid — the dialog opens prefilled (symbol/side/price, quantity empty), Cancel closes with nothing placed; placing follows the normal confirmation. The prefill always reflects the CURRENT exchange's context (a level click right after a toolbar switch names the new exchange), and a second click while the dialog is open re-opens the transient with the fresh prefill.
- **Tests:** hit-test index math (pure helper), the routing+prefill values, existing placement/confirmation suites untouched and green.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the four commits (`78762f2`, `01d4619`, `f73a90f`, `a94c342`) sit directly on develop — fast-forward (no merge commit; develop had only the queue docs commit `1da448d` past the branch's cut base). Outcome:
  - The click resolve ships as a pure layout helper (`OrderBookPageLayout.ResolveLevel`): asks = ceiling index, bids = floor, half-open bands toward the mid neighbor — the press resolves the side the trader would trade AT that level. The prefill carries `OrderPrefill(Symbol, Side, Price)` into the SAME dialog path the menu drives (dialog creation + `ShowDialog` stay View-owned per the window-opening convention); quantity is NEVER auto-filled; IRiskManager + IConfirmationService untouched.
  - Route shape (the async-void review fork): `LevelClicked` publishes as a hot `IObservable<OrderBookLevelClickedEventArgs>` (Subject-backed, fed on the UI thread); MainWindow subscribes through the house `SubscribeAsync` extension (task per press, one error callback logging `"Book level click stream failed"`, disposed with `Closed`) — zero `async void` at the seam. Placement was reviewed and KEPT View-side: the stream's source is the widget and the observer's effect needs the owner window; the VM gains nothing today (no decision logic to own) — revisit via `Interaction` if the VM ever must decide what a click means.
  - Hit-surface lesson: a plain Avalonia `Control` rendering only a custom draw op is NOT hit-tested — the first live clicks died silently. Fixed with a transparent child `Rectangle` bound to `Bounds` (events bubble to the widget); same defect class as the T-022 canvas-invisibility note.
  - Tests 254 → 268 (13 Core + 120 App + 42 UI + 93 Infra): the resolve matrix (8 layout tests), 3 `Prefill` facts on the dialog VM, 3 `SubscribeAsync` facts (per-press ordering, error routing, hot-source tolerance). GATE PASSED ×2; resx parity untouched (95/95 — no new strings).
  - Probe (UIA + Serilog + pixel math, toolbar → Demo, BTC/USDT): the book column measured from the theme palette at 125 % DPI (ask bar `(73,26,30)`, bid bar `(16,61,37)`, navy midline `(18,25,45)`, pitch 17 px = 14 DIP). Bid row 0 click → «Новый ордер» opens prefilled BTC/USDT / «Купить» / `66993.30`, quantity EMPTY; ask row 0 → «Продать» / `67006.70`; Cancel closes each; the fresh log window contains no `Order dialog: placed`.
  - Probe-infra notes: UIA `SetFocus` throws on Avalonia peers → Win32 `SetForegroundWindow` on the process window instead; the dialog title is «Новый ордер» and the side combo items are «Купить»/«Продать» — both from the ONE resx pair there is (`ZettaTrade.UI/Resources/Strings` en+ru; the earlier "App resx vs UI resx" split and the «Покупка»/«Продажа» claim were a mojibake misread of the ru file — corrected in T-025b; trust the peer dump, not memory).

## T-025b — Fix the prefilled-dialog defects from the live review

- **Branch:** `fix/dialog-prefill`, reported 2026-11-02 by the user right after the T-025 merge (two screenshots: the symbol AutoCompleteBox popup hanging open over the just-prefilled dialog; pressing Place on the empty quantity shows «Количество не является числом: » with nothing after the colon).
- **Origin:** the T-025 probe asserted the prefill VALUES but not the dialog's post-open state. The symbol load handler deliberately opens the dropdown while ANY text sits in the field (the slow-network, mid-typing affordance) — and a click prefill IS such text, so the popup echoed the clicked level over the layout. The empty-quantity refusal fell into the "not a number" parse branch, whose message dangles on a blank argument.
- **What:** a prefill is not a typist's intent — while the field holds its untouched click payload the suggestions stay empty (the dropdown cannot open) and the FIRST editing change releases the hold; empty/whitespace quantity and limit price name the REQUIREMENT with dedicated resx keys instead of the dangling wording; unparsable input keeps the "not a number" message with the argument trimmed.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the fix commit `1a6f18f` sits directly on develop — fast-forward (no merge commit; develop had only the T-025 docs commit `2f148fc` past the branch's cut base). Outcome:
  - `NewOrderDialogViewModel`: `Prefill` parks the `_prefilledSymbol` hold BEFORE assigning `SymbolInput`; the setter clears the hold on any differing edit; `RefreshSymbolSuggestions` returns the empty list while the hold is active (the invariant is documented and guarded by the setter); the `IsSymbolSuggestionsOpen` doc notes a prefill can never open the gate.
  - `TryBuildRequest`: trimmed required-branches for the quantity and the limit price → new keys `NewOrder.QuantityRequired` («Количество обязательно») / `NewOrder.PriceRequired` («Лимитная цена обязательна») added to BOTH files of the single resx pair (`ZettaTrade.UI/Resources/Strings`); unparsable input keeps the NotANumber wording.
  - Tests 268 → 271 (13 Core + 123 App + 42 UI + 93 Infra): the empty-quantity prefill repro, a whitespace-quantity fact covering the trim, and the Limit-missing-price fact reworded to the required contract. GATE PASSED.
  - Probe (the T-025 probe skeleton + new asserts, exit=0): bid0/ask0 clicks open the dialog on attempt 1; zero ListItems outside the combos and exactly 2 visible top-level windows (no popup); the prefill is exact (`BTC/USDT` / `66993.30` Buy, `67006.70` Sell), quantity EMPTY; Place on the empty quantity refuses with «Количество обязательно» while the fresh Serilog window has no `Order dialog: placed` and no `risk manager rejected`; Cancel closes.
  - Review note: the gray `0.01` in the quantity field is the TextBox placeholder, not a value — a click never assumes a size (the T-025 contract stands; the trader types the quantity). Confirmed correct with the user after an initial misread.

  - Header UX + persistence: localized `Chart.TimeFrame` label and the TF combo in the chart header; `UI.ChartTimeFrame` via `UiSettingsKeys`; the restore runs in `IAsyncInitializable.InitializeAsync` (startup-walk parity with `ActiveExchangeService` — applied before the first load, no H1 flash); user flips persist live; a restore is a read and never re-persists (suppression flag, test-pinned); an unrecognized stored value keeps H1 and logs a warning — never a silent substitute.

GATE PASSED.

## T-027 — Hide/show the AI assistant panel

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (grounding: the chat panel is a fixed 300 px right column in `MainWindow.axaml`; `Menu.OpenAiChatCommand` (Ctrl+Shift+A) only focuses the input — nothing hides the panel)
- **What:** `IsAiChatVisible` property (binding lives where the toggle is owned — the menu VM mirrors it as a checkable item); the hotkey semantics become toggle: hidden → show + focus the input, visible → hide. The right column collapses and the rest of the layout reflows. Persisted as `UI.ShowAiPanel` (UiSettingsKeys; default TRUE — today's layout stays the default view) and restored BEFORE the first layout (no flash of the hidden or shown state).
- **Scope:** menu VM + the property owner VM, `MainWindow.axaml` (column/IsVisible + the checkable item), `UiSettingsKeys`, the settings ViewModel section, tests. resx: `Menu.AI.Chat` is reused; a new `Ai.*` key ONLY if a visible toolbar toggle button ships too.

- **Tests:** the toggle command semantics (hidden/visible branches), the persisted restore, the default when the key is missing.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commit `007bb85` is on develop (the user merged `feat/ai-panel-toggle` as a fast-forward). Outcome:
  - Shipped: `ChatViewModel` owns the persisted visibility (`IsAiChatVisible`; ctor `(ISettingsService, ILogger<ChatViewModel>)`, `IAsyncInitializable`): `UI.ShowAiPanel` (default TRUE) is restored by the startup walk BEFORE any window opens (no first-frame flash); every flip persists live behind a real-difference cache set BEFORE the property change (the restore and the changes-stream echo never rewrite); external writers apply live through the settings changes stream.
  - Menu discipline: `MenuViewModel` now takes the chat VM; `OpenAiChatCommand` → `ToggleAiChatCommand` (Ctrl+Shift+A), the interaction renamed `FocusAiChatRequested` and fired ONLY when the toggle SHOWS the panel; the menu item is checkable (`ToggleType="CheckBox"`, OneWay mirror driven by the chat VM — no write-back loop). The right column became `Auto`-width, so a hidden panel releases its 300 px to the workspace.
  - Focus honesty (the user's review flag): `FocusChatAsync` is genuinely async — it awaits `Dispatcher.UIThread.InvokeAsync(() => ChatInput.Focus(), DispatcherPriority.Background)` per attempt (below the render priority, i.e. after the pending layout pass), bounded by `MaxChatFocusFrames = 8`, and completes the interaction only when the focus lands — no fire-and-forget posting.
  - Tests 381 → 389 (13 Core + 166 App + 117 UI + 93 Infra): the toggle/persist facts, restore-without-rewrite, the live external change with a silent echo, the menu toggle/focus discipline (focus only on show), the mirror continuity across a late startup restore. No resx changes (`Menu.AI.Chat` reused; no toolbar/settings-dialog row — the acceptance names only the menu + hotkey path).
  - Probe `PROBE PASSED` (`t027-probe.ps1`, UIA + SendKeys with a UIA menu-invoke fallback, original settings bytes restored): staged-visible start (ChatInput at X=1791) → the toggle hides (input absent, workspace reflow +375 px measured on the TF combo's right edge); a relaunch stays hidden; the toggle shows AND the input takes the keyboard focus (X=1791); a relaunch restores shown — both states round-trip. Probe lessons: SendKeys' foreground delivery can race a maximized-window relaunch (the menu-invoke fallback re-drives the SAME command); the focused-edit zone check must be `winRight − 450`, not −340 — the input's legit X=1791 against a window right edge misses the tighter zone.
  - Ops: the 60-fps order book perf test and the status-bar notice-expiry test flaked once more under the gate's parallel builds — green in isolation and on the clean rerun (per the T-037 ops note). Worktree `zt-ai-panel` and branch removed post-merge.

## T-028 — Candle OHLC + crosshair price on chart hover

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (grounding: `CandlestickChart` handles zoom wheel + focus only — `OnPointerMoved` does not exist; the canvas is UIA-invisible per the T-022 probe lesson)
- **Origin:** reading a candle's price today means eyeballing the axis. The chart already owns the price↔pixel mapping for axes/grid — hovering is the same math reversed.
- **What:** `OnPointerMoved` tracks the cursor and requests a redraw on each move (the chart already owns its redraw pipeline): dashed crosshair (theme colors), a price-axis label at the cursor's price and a compact O/H/L/C (+V) readout of the HOVERED candle — the honest pair the user asked: cursor price on the axis, candle values in the readout. Hidden when the pointer leaves; dead code paths when no Bars. The x/y ↔ price/time mapping is extracted into internal static pure helpers (the same geometry the axes already use) so tests can pin it without pixels.
- **Scope:** `CandlestickChart.cs` ONLY (+ the pure helpers), the UI test suite. No resx (numbers reuse the axis formatting), no VM changes.
- **Acceptance:** screenshot probe (the canvas prints no UIA nodes): px-diff the chart region before/after a hover move — crosshair present; move over two distinct candles → the readout changes to the second candle's OHLC. The known-broken DPI-scaled-rect screenshot helper (T-022 note) is fixed as probe infra.
- **Tests:** mapping helpers (top/bottom padding, zero bars, one bar), hovered-bar selection math, no-throw when the cursor is outside the bar area.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commit `75fd153` is on develop (the user merged `feat/chart-hover` as a fast-forward). Outcome:
  - Shipped: `OnPointerMoved` tracks the cursor into `_hoverPosition` (UI-thread state, copied into the immutable snapshot like the visible bars; `OnPointerExited` clears, same-position move events skip the invalidation). On hover the draw pass renders LAST (over candles and axis labels): 1 px dashed rails on the Grid token (`[4,4]` px dash) clipped per-axis to the plot, a hovered **price pill** backgrounded in the right gutter at the cursor's row, and the hovered candle's compact **O/H/L/C + V readout** in the plot's top-left. Dead paths stay silent: no hover, a cursor in a gutter, or a cursor outside every bar's half-pitch band draw LESS of the layer — never a throw, never a substitute.
  - Geometry extraction (the board's ask): the padded spread and the price↔pixel mapping moved out of the draw op into the pure `ChartPriceScale` (`PaddedSpread` preserves the flat-window unit-range substitution; `PriceToY` stays bit-identical for normal windows and clamps a plot-sliver's usable span instead of inverting the scale; `YToPrice` is the linearly-extrapolating inverse — the pill prints an honest outside-band price, never clamps, never throws). The hovered-bar selection is `ChartBarLayout.SlotIndexForX`: nearest-center half-pitch bands, the boundary tie rounds UP, outside the slot space (the capped window's empty left zone, the half-open right band end) is −1 — never a wrapped or clamped bar.
  - Tests 389 → 411 (13 Core + 166 App + 139 UI + 93 Infra; +22 UI facts): the padded spread (ratio, flat window, one-bar window), the headroom anchors + top-down ordering, mapping round-trips across the band, outside-band extrapolation without throwing, the degenerate plot sliver staying alive and ordered, slot-selection bands (centers, the boundary tie, the empty left zone, degenerate inputs).
  - Probe `PROBE PASSED` (`t028-probe.ps1`, real cursor via `SetCursorPos` + rounded-band diffs, settings untouched): idle jitter measured FIRST (0/0) and thresholds lifted on top of it, so a live feed can never read as a hover; the crosshair band diff **2814** vs baseline, pill ink **1304**, the readout appears **1154**; moving ~4 candle pitches RIGHT flips the readout to the second candle (**454**; crops show `O 0,02927 … V 16,14370` vs `O 0,02937 … V 51,91450`); on leave the plot returns to the baseline pixels exactly (**0** — the layer fully hides). Screenshot infra (the T-022 note fixed as the board required): a DPI-aware process + physical-px UIA rects + boundary rounding with clamping. New probe lesson: `SetCursorPos` needs SCREEN coordinates — the first run fed window-local ones and the cursor never lined up with the pill band.
  - No version bump: alpha.20 stays deferred (batch-completion decision, per T-037/T-027). Worktree `zt-chart-hover` and branch removed post-merge.

## T-029 — Persist the workspace symbol

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (grounding: settings persist ONLY `Workspace.ExchangeId` — the T-023/T-024 probes cold-started on BTC/USDT because it is the demo universe's first entry, the Preselect default)
- **Origin:** the T-017 one-symbol workspace context survives exchanges at runtime but not restarts: everything (chart/book/dialog) re-opens on the first-entry symbol after a relaunch.
- **What:** `Workspace.SymbolId` setting; every confirmed `SymbolSelections` emission saves it (the settings service already debounces); at bootstrap the Preselect rule gains a persisted-priority branch: persisted → preserved-current → first entry → empty; the persisted value is a PREFERENCE, not a substitute — an unknown/unsupported symbol falls through and is logged (trading-safety: never substitute defaults silently).
- **Scope:** `ChartViewModel` (Preselect + a save hook), the settings keys, tests. Core untouched.
- **Acceptance:** probe: select ETH/BTC while Binance is active → restart → the chart/book/dialog reopen on ETH/BTC; switch exchange to one that lacks it → logged fallback to the first entry.
- **Tests:** the Preselect priority table (persisted/current/first/empty/fallback), save-on-change timing, restore behavior.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commit `c9c77d5` is on develop (the user merged `feat/persist-symbol` as a fast-forward). Outcome:
  - Shipped: `Workspace.SymbolId` (`UiSettingsKeys.WorkspaceSection`/`WorkspaceSymbolId`); the constructor persists every confirmed selection (`WhenAnyValue(SelectedSymbol)` → `Skip(1)` eats the ctor echo → the bootstrap-application gate → non-null → `FromAsync(SetAsync(...))`, errors logged), and every symbols bootstrap reads the preference FRESH inside its load (`LoadBootstrapAsync` — deliberately not `InitializeAsync`, which would race the constructor bootstrap). The decision stays pinned pure: `Preselect` prefers persisted → preserved-current → first entry → empty and returns the LIST's own instance (identity uplift, the combo's `SelectedItem` stays of its source); `TryParseStoredSymbol` never throws; an unsupported value only warns (no substitute), an unlisted one warns `is not listed on <Exchange>`. A bootstrap application never rewrites the preference (the restore echo would be a duplicate write; persisting the fall-through substitute would destroy the preference it deferred); a cleared selection never erases it.
  - Tests 411 → 424 (13 Core + 179 App + 139 UI + 93 Infra; +13 App facts): the persisted-priority and fall-through tables, the identity uplift (`BeSameAs`), the restore-parse matrix (any case/trim/blank/`/USDT` → null), save-on-change, the identity echo writes once, null writes nothing; the `Preselect` call sites updated to the 3-arg signature.
  - Probe `PROBE PASSED` (`t029-probe.ps1`, UIA + a real book-level click, settings bytes restored from the backup): a no-preference cold start on Binance opens its first listed entry ETH/BTC (combo and book agree); picking BTC/USDT persists it; the relaunch reopens chart, book and a book-click-prefilled order dialog on BTC/USDT; staging `Demo` + `XLM/USDT` logs `Persisted workspace symbol 'XLM/USDT' is not listed on Demo; starting on 'BTC/USDT'`, the combo falls to the demo first entry and the settings file keeps `XLM/USDT` + `ExchangeId=demo`. Canon fact the probe established: the persisted exchange is `binance` and Binance's trimmed listing's first entry is **ETH/BTC** (the demo universe's own first entry is BTC/USDT — this task's grounding dated from the demo runs). Probe lesson: a `,$`-wrapped array returned into a PowerShell PIPELINE arrives as one object, so `Where-Object` matched the whole fresh-log window as a single blob and masked real matches — bare `return` enumerates per-line; Phase 4's 0/1 timeouts were this harness artifact, never app behavior.
  - No version bump: alpha.20 stays deferred (batch-completion decision, per T-037/T-027/T-028). Worktree `zt-persist-symbol` and branch removed post-merge.

## T-031 — Cancel a working order from the UI

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (grounding: open orders are listed (T-009 panel, T-010 persistence) but the UI has no cancel; the MCP `cancel_order` tool already wraps the adapter cancel surface)

- **What:** a Cancel action on the open-orders rows (and positions-adjacent orders if one panel hosts both): routes through the SAME ITradingAdapter cancel the MCP tool uses; the SYMBOL always accompanies the request (safety: no symbol-less cancel); the high-risk gate is `IConfirmationService` (the composed confirmation dialog); the journal records it; failures log + surface honestly (no silent no-op); the row/list and history update from the adapter's own events (no optimistic mutation).
- **Scope:** the panel VM + row action, the dialog composition reuse, tests. No Core changes (the cancel surface exists for MCP).
- **Acceptance:** place a paper limit, cancel from the panel → confirmation dialog → the order ends Cancelled, history persists it, the panel refreshes from the adapter events; the user-declined confirmation changes nothing.
- **Tests:** the VM flow (success/adapter-fail/user-declined), the symbol-always-present rule, no optimistic list mutations.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the merge commit `6ec82a5` is on develop (the user merged `origin/feat/cancel-order`, branch tip `4cecb96`). Probe `PROBE PASSED` (`t031-cancel-probe.ps1`, UIA + log needles, fresh `orders-history.db` with a bytes backup): a non-crossing paper BUY rests as Pending (the "Paper rest" log line, no fill), the panel lists the resting row with a Cancel button, the CONFIRMED cancel drives the same adapter path the MCP tool uses (the journal + history show Cancelled from the adapter events, no optimistic mutation), and the DECLINED confirmation leaves the row untouched with a logged decline.

- **Status:** done. Canon: the merge commit `d2144c8` is on develop (the user merged `origin/feat/ai-chat-local`, branch tip `61a7a5b`). The send command runs the local Ollama completion first (`AI` settings section keys); the workspace context (exchange, symbol, last price, time frame) rides the system prompt; an unreachable model answers an honest in-chat error line, never silence; the T-012 focus behavior rides unchanged; the loopback-stub e2e (timeout / HTTP 500 / malformed JSON) pins the error paths.

## T-033 — Chart pan + older-history paging

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (grounding: the chart zooms — `_visibleBars` on wheel — but cannot pan; `GetBarsAsync` serves one from-anchored window per request, naturally paginated by `start/end`)
- **Origin:** a 200-candle chart cannot look further back; deep history is unreachable in the UI even though both real adapters page klines server-side.
- **What:** pointer-drag pans horizontally; crossing the oldest visible bar fetches the PREVIOUS window (same limit) from the adapter and prepends (times stay strictly ASCENDING — the T-023 contract; an overlap is deduped by opening time, a failed fetch leaves the view honest). Appends ride the LAST loaded (symbol, TF, exchange) pair only — a selection/TF/exchange change rebases the whole series (the existing Switch machinery owns rebasing). Hover/zoom continue over the extended series.
- **Scope:** `CandlestickChart` (pan + the fetch threshold), `ChartViewModel` (an append path beside the full reload), tests.
- **Acceptance:** probe: pan left past the first candle → older candles appear (an appends log line distinct from the reload line); the ascending invariant holds visually and in the VM collection; a TF flip mid-pan rebases cleanly.
- **Tests:** the window-merge math (overlap, dedupe, non-ascending guard), the append-vs-rebase ownership, no-throw under cancellation.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the merge commit `2744444` is on develop (the user merged `origin/feat/chart-pan`, branch tip `ee04058`). The left-drag pans in whole bar slots and reaching the loaded series' head pages the previous window, prepended in place so the pan state survives every append (pure pan/merge math: `ChartPanWindow`, `BarSeriesMerge`); probe `PROBE PASSED` (`t033-chart-pan-probe.ps1`, UIA + Serilog needles) — the wheel-out edge pins the window on the series head with no loads fired, H1 pan appends ×15 with exactly ONE delivered line (no rebase), the M5 flip produces its own request+delivery pair, M5 pans append under the new frame, and the delivered count never grows from panning.

## T-035 — Fix the clipped chart axis labels + adaptive axis labels on zoom (user-reported)

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (reported the same day by the user with screenshots of the chart on ETH/BTC: scale numbers clip at the canvas edges and the wheel zoom visibly changes nothing). Independent files from `feat/timeframe` (T-026) — rebase onto develop after that merge.
- **Origin (proven against `CandlestickChart.DrawAxes`, develop@e4bbf49):** price ticks draw right-aligned with a `y + MinAxisLabelPaddingPx` baseline spanning the FULL canvas height — the bottom-most tick's baseline lands below the canvas (invisible), the top-most glyph crosses it; the first time label is `SKTextAlign.Center` at `x = Left` (left half clipped) and the last one sits centered on the right canvas edge (right half clipped). `price.ToString("F2")` collapses close prices (ETH/BTC renders one repeated «0,03»); time ticks are per-bar-index `"HH:mm"` — never re-bucketed on zoom (and every D1/W1/MN1 bar renders `00:00`).
- **What (user-approved scope):** axis gutters so labels never clip — the plot rect insets right/bottom by the MEASURED label extents and the price mapping gains vertical headroom (shared by candles, grid and ticks so the scale stays honest); the price ladder keeps even ticks with the smallest decimal count (≥ 2) that keeps adjacent labels distinct; time ticks re-bucket from a step ladder over the VISIBLE time span (`HH:mm` ↔ month-day ↔ year as the window widens; ticks aligned to the step grid and horizontally clamped inside the canvas). DATA/TIMEFRAME STAYS MANUAL (the T-026 picker): zoom keeps moving the visible window only; "see more history" stays T-033; auto-TF-on-zoom was explicitly rejected.
- **Scope:** `CandlestickChart.cs` + one pure axis-formatting class in `src/ZettaTrade.UI/Charting`, tests in `tests/ZettaTrade.UI.Tests/Charting`. No resx (dates/numbers format through CultureInfo, which `LocalizationManager.SetLanguage` already resets), no VM/XAML changes, no Core.
- **Acceptance:** screenshot probe (the canvas prints no UIA nodes): labels present inside both gutters, no ink in the label bands' outermost edge rows/columns; a zoom-out visibly re-buckets the axes (axis-band pixel diff between two zoom states).
- **Tests:** the time-step ladder, both formatters, the tick alignment, and the decimal-precision rule — including the ETH/BTC distinctness repro and D1/MN1-span windows.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commit `852b81e` is on develop (the user merged it as a fast-forward). Outcome: measured price gutter + headroom, epoch-anchored time ladder (`ChartAxisFormatting`, 27 new tests; UI 42 → 69; gate 316 total), and a pixel probe `PROBE PASSED` over three chart states (default full window / 8×IN to the 20-bar floor / 8×OUT back): label ink in both gutters (~2994/~1372 px at s=1.25), zero ink at the outermost edge rows/columns, jitter 0, zoom gutter diffs TG 593 / PG 3835 per state change. Probe facts live in the temp dir (`t035-probe.ps1`, `t035-*.png`); known follow-ups from the user's live review queued as T-036 (chart history depth + candle-width cap) and T-037 (denser time ladder rungs).

## T-036 — Chart history depth + candle-size cap (user-reported)

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (user review of the merged T-035: the weekly and monthly charts render ONE candle stretched across the whole plot).
- **Origin (proven against develop@c4afc77):** `ChartViewModel.LoadBars` asks for a 7-day window (`ChartLookbackDays = 7`, `InitialBarCount = 200`) — the request yields per interval: H4 42 bars, W1 1–2, MN1 1; and the draw pass stretches whatever arrived: `candleWidth = plotRect.Width / bars.Count` paints one bar full-width. The same window also dates the M1 "latest" edge ~6.9 days back (the API returns the OLDEST `limit` bars of the window).
- **What (user-approved, exchange-standard):** every time frame gets the same INITIAL bar count (~200 is the web-terminal norm): `from = now − InitialBarCount × tfDuration` from a TF-duration map (W1 → 200 weeks, MN1 → all months since the listing, M1 → a fresh 200 minutes). The chart never stretches bars: the bar pitch is capped at a max logical pitch, and when fewer bars than the visible window are loaded they keep that pitch anchored to the plot's RIGHT edge (the terminal right-offset; the empty space grows left). Zoom semantics unchanged (the T-035 machine).
- **Scope:** `ChartViewModel.cs` (+ the TF-duration history-start math as a testable pure piece), `CandlestickChart.cs` bar layout + time mapping for the capped case, tests (App.Tests history-start facts; UI.Tests bar-layout facts). No Core, no resx.
- **Acceptance:** pixel probe (the T-035 harness): W1/MN1 show the exchange picture — many candles and NO full-width candle; the H4 state keeps the T-035 verdict (label bands inked, zero edge ink).
- **Tests:** the TF→duration history-start math for every enum member, the pitch cap + right-anchor mapping (pure facts), the snapshot/zoom window unchanged.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commit `2c4952e` is on develop (merge `727d7a2`, non-FF — develop had the T-037 docs commits past the branch's cut base). Outcome: per-TF history window (`ChartViewModel.HistoryStart`, MN1→30d…M1→1m switch; the load limit holds one over the bar count so the exchange's oldest-first answer keeps the fresh edge), capped pitch + right-anchored slots in a new pure `ChartBarLayout` (21 UI tests: cap/uncap, legacy-equivalence, slot snapping, gap/one-bar/empty edges), App.Tests +11 (per-TF 200-bar walk-back theory, unknown frame → error). Gate: `GATE PASSED`, 348 tests (13 Core + 152 App + 90 UI + 93 Infra). Pixel probe `PROBE PASSED` (harness `t036-probe.ps1`, TF switching via UIA + delivered-log gating): delivered M1=200/H4=200/W1=201/MN1=104 (was 7-day window: H4 42, W1 1–2, MN1 1), max candle paint run ≤ 44·s px on every state (no full-width walls), H4 keeps the T-035 verdict incl. zoom re-bucketing (TG diffs 3534/3874 over jitter), the persisted TF was restored after the probe. The user's follow-up review (W1/D1 axis labels: backward-drifting day-of-month, no years, off-month day grids) grew T-037's scope on develop (`9aab302`).

## T-037 — Exchange-grade time axis: density, calendar anchors, year labels (user-reported)

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-02 (from the same user review: at the 20-bar zoom floor the M1 chart grids every 5 minutes, M5 → 10 min, M15 → 30 min — the time step always lands one ladder rung above the bar step). Scope EXTENDED 2026-11-03 from the user's live review of the T-036 build: the COARSE end of the axis fails the same class of way.
- **Origin (all proven against the current ladder):** (a) the time axis uses a FIXED 10-tick budget (`ChartAxisFormatting.TimeTickBudget`) and a ladder without fine rungs: a ~20-bar window then always picks one rung above (1m→5m, 5m→10m, 15m→30m); (b) between 28 d and 730 d the ladder holds only uniform epoch multiples (91/182/365/730 d), so a ~3.5-year W1 window picks the 182-day rung: the labels alternate февр./авг. while the day-of-month DRIFTS BACKWARD each half-year cycle (февр. 6 → февр. 5 → февр. 3 → февр. 2 — reads like time running in reverse (the user asked "it's like it goes backwards?" — quote translated from the original), the "MMM d" format prints NO year so four "февр." labels across three years are indistinguishable, and the epoch-anchored lines slice arbitrary weekdays BETWEEN the Monday-opened weekly candles; (c) a D1 window's 28-day grid lands on random calendar dates (апр. 27, мая 25, июн. 22 — no month starts), while terminals anchor calendar events: a daily chart grids month starts, a weekly chart grids months, a monthly chart grids years.
- **What:**
  1. the tick budget becomes measured from the label font (max label width + gap, clamped to a sane band), and the intraday ladder gains 2m/3m/2h/4h;
  2. the day-and-coarser scale switches from uniform epoch multiples to CALENDAR ticks: steps land on month starts (1/2/3/6 months) and year starts (1/2/5/10 years), so a W1 window grids month boundaries, an MN1 window grids January-only, a D1 window never shows "апр. 27";
  3. the label format becomes span-aware: intraday → "HH:mm" with midnight→date (unchanged); spans inside one year → "MMM d"; multi-year spans → month + year ("MMM 'yy"); year-scale steps → "yyyy" — no more yearless February triplets.
- **Scope:** `ChartAxisFormatting.cs` (ladder, anchor policy, format policy), the draw pass consuming the measured budget in `CandlestickChart.cs`, tests. No Core, no resx.
- **Acceptance:** pixel probe (the T-035/T-036 harness): the W1 repro reads as month-start labels WITH years; the D1 repro grids month starts; at the zoom floor M1 grids below 5 minutes; wide windows re-bucket exactly as before (the diff-based re-bucket assertions hold); no glyph clipping (edge asserts stay 0).
- **Tests:** the rung picker bands for every TF×window pairing from the reports, the calendar anchor policy (month/year starts, leap years, the Feb-day-backward repro pinned as a pure fact), the format policy (the span thresholds "MMM d" vs "MMM 'yy" vs "yyyy"), the measured-budget calibration against the on-screen font.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the feature commits `2a7257c` (the measured tick budget + the fine 2m/3m/2h/4h rungs; CALENDAR day-and-coarser anchors — W1 grids month starts, MN1 January-only, D1 grids month starts; span-aware formats "MMM d" / "MMM 'yy" / bare "yyyy") and `b5d3afa` are on develop (the user merged `feat/time-grid` as a fast-forward; the branch also carried the user-approved `0657eb9` — the slnx now groups ONLY the test projects under a `/tests/` solution folder, src stays at the root). `b5d3afa` ships the user's pre-merge review asks: the axis label culture resolves on the UI thread into the immutable per-redraw snapshot (`ChartTickCulture.Resolve()`: the applied UI language first, the OS UI culture as the ungated fallback) because the Skia draw pass runs on Avalonia's render thread where the ambient culture still holds the process default — a live language switch re-inks months AND price separators (ru comma/space vs en dot); and the persisted `UI.ChartUse12HourClock` (default false = the 24-hour view the axis always printed; the settings window gains a third localized time-format row, `Chart.TimeFormat*` en+ru) applied through `ChartViewModel` (startup-walk restore + live `Changes`) without a restart — 12h labels are a plain culture-mapped "h:mm tt" (.NET 10's ICU data ships AM/PM designators for every installed culture, so the Latin-fallback idea was dead code and was deleted). Pixel probes `PROBE PASSED`: t038 (settings-file A/B over cold starts, original bytes restored, crops eyeballed) — M1 ru+24h vs en+12h → time-gutter diff 2524 / price-gutter 48 (crops "07:10/08:20" vs "7:10 AM/8:20 AM"), language-only D1 A/B → 1335 (ru «авг. 3 …» vs en "Aug 3 …"), edge ink 0 everywhere; the t037 regression harness re-passed after the culture change (label clusters W1=8/D1out=13/M1floor=10/MN1=8, zoom diffs 739/1096/966 over jitter 0). Gate: `GATE PASSED`, 381 tests (13 Core + 158 App + 117 UI + 93 Infra). Ops note: two distinct tests flaked once each under the gate's parallel build load (the 60-fps order book perf test; one subscription timing test) — green in isolation and on the clean rerun. Worktree `zt-time-grid` and branch removed post-merge.

- **Branch:** feat/layout-splitters (user-reported 2026-11-03 right after merging T-027, quote translated from the original: "I'd like to be able to resize (shift) the chart / order book / AI panel" — the user wants to resize/shift the chart, the order book and the AI assistant panel columns). The UIA geometry probe (drag → reflow, the hidden splitter absent from the UIA tree) is left for the merge review; the identical mechanics are verified headless against Avalonia 12.1.4: a TwoWay `GridLength` binding on `ColumnDefinition.Width` writes back through a real `GridSplitter` resize, the star workspace stays one and the pixel column resizes alone, `MinWidth` clamps the drag, programmatic pushes still reflow after a splitter left a local value behind, and the drag persists once per settled burst (the probe used the keyboard route, which runs the same `MoveSplitter` path a mouse drag does). ## T-039 — Symbol favorites (star badge, pinned on top) + hiding pairs (user idea)

The marks live in the settings service (a `Workspace.*` JSON map or two keys, same reactive pipeline as the workspace symbol) and the symbols bootstrap applies them on every exchange switch: favorites first, hidden filtered (or grouped under the reveal toggle when it is on). A hidden-but-selected symbol stays selectable (no silent rebase of the live series).

- **Acceptance:** probe: star a pair → restart → it heads the list with the badge visible; hide a pair → it leaves the default list and returns under the reveal toggle; marks survive a time frame / symbol change without losing the current selection.
- **Tests:** the sort/filter/mark facts, the persistence round-trip (restore-without-rewrite discipline), the garbage-marked-values fail-safe (unknown/malformed marks drop to none, never crash the picker).
