## T-065 — Localization + themes stand alone: a lean Livery library (supervisor idea)

- **Branch:** feat/appearance (queued 2026-11-05, supervisor idea; developed 2026-11-05).
- **Origin:** `ZettaTrade.UI` bundles "shared UI components, themes, and localization infrastructure" together with the whole charting/widget world, and its project references (`ZettaTrade.Core`, `ZettaTrade.Infrastructure`) drag domain code into every consumer of a themed `TextBlock`. The supervisor wants the appearance layer (localization + the theme engine) reusable on its own — even as a local NuGet package later.
- **What:** a new lean assembly `Livery`: `Localization\LocalizationManager` + `LocalizedExtension`, `Resources\Strings(.Designer/.ru)` (namespace rehomed to `Livery.Resources`), `Themes\ThemeManager` + `ThemeFileWatcher` + `PreviewColorsToBrushesConverter`, and the shared UI-thread marshaller `ObserveOnUiThread` (its only member's file moves from `UI.Extensions`). The composition root passes the protected file paths: the user themes directory is a REQUIRED constructor argument (the lib must not reach into `Infrastructure.Configuration` for `ConfigPathResolver`), built-ins keep the exe-adjacent `themes\` default; `ZettaTrade.UI` keeps charting/primitives and references the new lib (its direct `ZettaTrade.Charting` reference replaces the dropped transitive one); `App` consumes both; XAML `xmlns:loc`/`xmlns:themes` and every C# `using` rehomed. Packaging decision recorded in the section (NOT yet implemented): the library ships as a solution project reference; a local NuGet pack is deferred until a second consumer exists — in-repo self-packaging adds a build step and version drift with no reuse yet. The `ObserveOnUiThread` pointers in the convention docs follow the move (`Livery.Extensions`).
- **Scope:** new `src\Livery` (csproj + moved sources), `slnx` registration, `ZettaTrade.UI.csproj`/`App.csproj` reference graph, `CompositionRoot.cs` (paths + namespaces), the six XAML windows' `xmlns` lines, the touch-point C# files (charting widgets, `CandlestickChart`, `ChartTickCulture`, `OrderBookWidget`, `OrderBookPageLayout`, `SettingsViewModel`, `StatusBarViewModel`/`MainViewModel`/`OrderBookViewModel`/`ChartViewModel`/`MainWindow.axaml.cs`, `CompositionRoot`), test namespaces (`UI.Tests`Themes/Localization/Charting, `App.Tests` Settings), `AGENTS.md` layout line + the `ObserveOnUiThread` pointer, version bump + CHANGELOG.
- **Acceptance:** runtime behavior byte-for-byte unchanged (theme hot-reload, live language switch, settings persistence); a fresh clone build + tests stay green; `GATE PASSED` (`-AllowProtected`: slnx + AGENTS).
- **Tests:** the existing theme/localization/resx-sync suites ride along (namespace-only deltas); a constructor-validation test pins the required user-themes directory; no new domain logic.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `8a7c164` (the extraction), `8598604` (the alpha.28 cut + this section's status flip), `7540f66` (the Livery rename) and this docs commit (rename recorded on the board, T-069 queued); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.28` (see CHANGELOG.md).


## T-066 — Window chrome + control restyling: styled title bar, buttons, inputs across the shell (supervisor idea)

- **Branch:** feat/window-chrome (base `develop` at `8bc1d1f`; queued 2026-11-05, supervisor idea; developed 2026-11-06).
- **Origin:** today every window rides the default Fluent chrome: native title bars, stock-styled buttons/inputs, nothing accent-keyed — the visual layer the supervisor asks to own.
- **What:** a custom window chrome driven by the theme: the `Livery` `TitleBar` strip control (localized title mirroring `Window.Title` live, drag, double-click maximize, working minimize/maximize/restore/close buttons wired through template parts) mounted by the `Window.chrome` / `Window.chrome-compact` templates on the Avalonia 12 mechanism — `ExtendClientAreaToDecorationsHint=True` with `WindowDecorations="BorderOnly"` keeps the native resize borders, AeroSnap and the DWM shadow while the system caption disappears (the `ExtendClientAreaChromeHints` API of older Avalonia no longer exists in 12). The queued text said five windows; the shell actually carries SIX and all opt in (`MainWindow`, `PositionsPanelWindow`, `OrderHistoryWindow` full; `NewOrderDialogWindow`, `ConfirmationDialogWindow`, `SettingsWindow` compact — the fixed-size dialogs would bury their content maximized and drop out of reach minimized). BorderOnly drops WS_SYSMENU, so the Alt+F4 close gesture dies with the system menu — the title bar restores it through a window-wide tunneling key hook. Plus the shared control restyle (buttons, text inputs, combos, tabs, menu with :pointerover/:pressed/:focus states) in `Livery/Themes/Controls.axaml`, included from `App.axaml` after `<FluentTheme />`: generic hover keys onto Accent, the close button onto Error; every color consumes the palette (the strip `TitleBar`/`TitleBarText` keys — new keys ship in every built-in theme + the sample theme + the key list in `ZettaTrade_SKILL.md`, the T-044 discipline; no hardcoded color literals anywhere), so theme and language switches retint everything LIVE.
- **Scope:** `src/Livery` (`Controls/TitleBar.cs`, `Themes/Controls.axaml` + the csproj AvaloniaResource include), `src/ZettaTrade.App` (`App.axaml` style include, all six windows' `Classes` + `Background` + the localized main title), `src/ZettaTrade.UI/Resources/Strings(.ru).resx` (`Chrome.*` labels) + `scripts/check-resx.ps1` (`Chrome.` module prefix), theme JSONs (the 5 built-ins), `ThemeManager` (sample theme keys), `ZettaTrade_SKILL.md`.
- **Acceptance:** dragging/max/min/close behave on all six windows; theme and language switches retint the chrome LIVE; edge cases pinned (double-click title = maximize, Aero snap still works via WS_THICKFRAME, Alt+F4 restores the close, RTL not required).
- **Tests:** visual tests for chrome states resolved not feasible — no headless render harness (the T-058/T-059 precedent), so the regression asserts ride the source-of-truth probes: every window carries its chrome class, the library styles ship from `App.axaml` and declare both chrome variants with the full Avalonia-12 mechanism, the `TitleBar`/`TitleBarText` keys exist in every built-in theme + the generated sample, the `Chrome.*` resx keys resolve in en and ru (`WindowChromeTests`, 11 new tests); full matrix green (738: 16 Core + 174 UI + 379 App + 169 Infrastructure).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `3c98ad6` (the chrome + restyle + the 11 test pins) and release commit `2674f03` (the alpha.32 cut + the status flip); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.32` (see CHANGELOG.md). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).


## T-067 — Indicator plugin settings render only for plugins that are actually loaded (supervisor idea)

- **Branch:** feat/indicator-settings-gate (base `develop` at `2674f03`; queued 2026-11-05, supervisor idea; developed 2026-11-06).
- **Origin:** the Indicators tab hardcodes the SMA enable/period row: with the plugin-first architecture the surface must reflect the actual scanned catalog, not a shipped assumption — a missing (or skipped-by-scan) plugin assembly would still render a dead row.
- **What:** the scanned catalog contract gains `LoadedNames` — the canonical names of the indicators the plugins actually registered (an immutable snapshot materialized at the end of the startup scan, the plugin set is fixed for the session). `SettingsViewModel` consumes it through constructor injection: the SMA row renders ONLY when the catalog registers the name (`IsSmaPluginLoaded`), the honest empty state shows when the scan registered zero indicators (`IsIndicatorCatalogEmpty` — localized hint naming the app data directory's `plugins` folder and the logged scan skips; the queued text guessed "next to the exe", the real location is the data `plugins` folder and the hint says so), and a save whose plugin the session does not own never rewrites the indicator config at all — the persisted rows ride untouched (the chart pipeline skips unknown names on its own seam, so neither side fabricates). With the plugin loaded the row keeps its exact former contract: the enable/period drafts seed from the persisted config, Save rewrites the `ChartIndicators` array on a real difference only.
- **Scope:** `ZettaTrade.Charting` (`IIndicatorCatalog.LoadedNames` — the member the availability flag drives), `ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Indicators/PluginIndicatorCatalog.cs` (the snapshot implementation), `SettingsViewModel` (the injected catalog + the two flags + the save gate), `SettingsWindow.axaml` (the conditional row + the empty state), `CompositionRoot.cs` (the catalog wiring), resx en/ru (`Settings.Indicators.Empty`), tests in App/Infrastructure suites.
- **Acceptance:** same-state tests pass with the SMA plugin present (row visible, the persisted period + enable seed the drafts and Save persists a real difference exactly as before — an unchanged save stays silent); with the plugin assembly gone the row vanishes and the empty state shows for a dead catalog (plugins that exist but do not carry the SMA row hide BOTH surfaces — the two flags answer two independent questions); the settings save path never fabricates a missing plugin's config.
- **Tests:** 11 new: the ViewModel gate matrix (loaded → row + no empty state; empty catalog → both hidden; plugins-but-no-SMA → both hidden) and the save trio (a real difference persists the rewritten array, an unchanged save never touches `ChartIndicators`, an absent plugin never fabricates the row — the persisted config rides untouched); the view-source pins (the visibility bindings + the localized hint, en and ru — no headless render harness, the standing precedent); the catalog snapshot facts (a staged SMA assembly registers its canonical name, an empty and a missing plugins directory register no names). Full matrix green (749: 16 Core + 174 UI + 387 App + 172 Infrastructure).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `e989eae` (the gate + the 11 test pins) and release commit `65780ef` (the alpha.33 cut + this section's status flip); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.33` (see CHANGELOG.md). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).


## T-068 — MCP client settings: external servers with transport, address/port and task-level options (supervisor idea)

- **Branch:** feat/mcp-client-settings (base `develop` at `65780ef`; queued 2026-11-05, supervisor idea; developed 2026-11-06).
- **Origin:** the Mcp tab today drives only the LOCAL server side (the trading-tools host: `ServerEnabled`). The MCP CLIENT surface (`IMcpClient`/`McpClientService` supports stdio AND Streamable HTTP transports) is code-only: nobody can attach an external MCP server without editing config files by hand; the supervisor asks for address/port (and other useful parameters) in settings.
- **What:** the Mcp tab gained the external-servers block: per-server entry editing (name, transport Stdio/StreamableHttp, command+args for stdio, address+port for HTTP, enabled flag, optional auth secret-REFERENCE key resolving through `ISecretStore` — the value itself never rides the config or a log; plus the optional HTTP timeout seconds), the per-entry honest state row (Disabled/Connecting/Connected/Failed), and the task-level options the transport already exposes. The list persists under the new `McpClients` settings section ("Servers" — the compact JSON `McpServerConfigList` parses/writes case-insensitively loud-skip-fail), the startup walk connects every enabled entry through the same `McpClientService.ConnectAsync` path, the settings save applies the list live through `McpClientManager` (unchanged saves keep live connections on the semantic diff; removed/changed/disabled ones disconnect), the apply waits a bounded 2 s grace so a dead server pends in the background instead of hanging the caller, and validation fails loudly — a bad endpoint or a non-positive timeout throws, a 500/malformed handshakes raises at the boundary (the manager pins the entry's Failed state with the transport's own reason), an unparsable persisted field skips the row with a logged problem. The local-server block stays byte-compatible.
- **Scope:** `ZettaTrade.Core` (`McpServerConfig.HttpTimeoutSeconds` — additive), `ZettaTrade.AI/Mcp` (`McpClientManager` + `IMcpClientManager` + `McpClientState`, `McpServerConfigList`, `McpClientService` endpoint validation + the loud handshake), `ZettaTrade.App` (`McpServerRow`, SettingsViewModel wiring, `SettingsWindow.axaml` block, `CompositionRoot`), resx en/ru (19 `Settings.McpClients.*` keys), tests in App suites.
- **Acceptance:** e2e loopback HTTP MCP stub (the T-032 harness pattern): a configured address/port completes its handshake (200 + JSON body, initialize + notification on one connection); a 500-stub and a malformed-body stub produce the pinned Failed state (never a silent half-connect), a never-responding stub times out through the entry's own timeout; an outage (a refused port) pins Failed with the reason and never hangs the apply (bounded grace); stdio entries launch their configured command (a `dotnet --version` entry lands Connected, a nonexistent command fails loud); a disabled entry never dials (no socket traffic, the Disabled row); duplicate names drop loudly; secrets never appear in state rows, logs or config.
- **Tests:** 39 new App facts: `McpServerConfigListTests` (the parse/write matrix — bailing on unparsable names/transports/timeouts, case-insensitive keys, bool/"true", numeric transports, absent options stay absent, the compact round-trip); `McpClientManagerTests` (the loopback matrix — happy/500/malformed/never-respond, an outage, a disabled entry, an unchanged save keeps the connection, a changed URL redials, a removed entry discards its client, stdio launch + ghost command, duplicate names, the snapshot never carries a resolved value); `SettingsViewModelMcpClientTests` (the seeding shapes rows verbatim, states key by name with the honest "not known" dash for unsaved rows, live snapshots repaint, the save writes exact wires and applies verbatim, an unchanged save is silent, a blank-named row drops loudly, the timeout option rides the entry, an unparsable one saves without the option). Full matrix green (787: 16 Core + 174 UI + 425 App + 172 Infrastructure).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `d955cdf` (the external-servers feature + the 39 test pins) and release commit `3618b21` (the alpha.34 cut + this section's status flip); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.34` (see CHANGELOG.md). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).


## T-069 — The appearance contracts move into Livery: Core stops owning IThemeManager/ILocalizationManager (supervisor idea)

- **Branch:** feat/appearance-contracts (base `develop` at `3618b21`; queued 2026-11-05, supervisor idea; developed 2026-11-06).
- **Origin:** Livery's first consumer is a non-trading project, so the only remaining brand/hardware tie is the contracts file the supervisor owns (`ZettaTrade.Core/Interfaces`): `IThemeManager`, `ILocalizationManager`, `IAsyncInitializable` (+ the `ThemeInfo`/`LanguageInfo` records) make every library consumer drag the whole Core assembly in just to render a themed `TextBlock`.
- **What:** the contracts relocate with the clean flip (the queued declare-and-deprecate shim allowance stays unexercised): `IThemeManager` + `ThemeInfo` move to `Livery/Themes`, `ILocalizationManager` + `LanguageInfo` to `Livery/Localization`, `IAsyncInitializable` to `Livery/Contracts`, and `Livery.csproj` drops its Core project reference, so the library references nothing ZettaTrade-branded and packages standalone (`dotnet pack`, the deferred T-065 decision, becomes the intended sharing mode for the second consumer). The queued "reference direction flips — Core consumes the contracts from Livery" settles as a NO edge: Core never implemented or called the appearance contracts, so deleting them from `ZettaTrade.Core/Interfaces` removes the tie without any Core→Livery reference; the consumers that did use them rehome instead — `App` keeps riding both assemblies, `ZettaTrade.AI` and `ZettaTrade.Infrastructure` gain the Livery project reference (they no longer find the contracts in Core), 36 source files across src/tests get their `using ZettaTrade.Core.Interfaces` rewritten or complemented, and the Settings window XAML datatypes switch from the `core:` prefix to the existing `themes:`/`loc:` library prefixes. Every injected seam, registration and behavior stays byte-identical — no shim keeps the old homes alive.
- **Scope:** `ZettaTrade.Core/Interfaces` (protected; supervisor-owned — the three contract files deleted), `src/Livery` (the relocated contracts + the csproj reference drop), consumer namespaces (`App` services/ViewModels/views, `AI` MCP, `Infrastructure` configuration/exchange/persistence/secrets, `UI` + `App` test suites), the `slnx` reference graph (the AI/Infrastructure csproj references). The `AGENTS.md` layout line already documents Livery as the domain-neutral appearance library and stays untouched.
- **Acceptance:** `src/Livery` carries no ZettaTrade reference (pack-ready for the non-trading consumer); a fresh-clone build stays green and the full matrix rides unchanged (787: 16 Core + 174 UI + 425 App + 172 Infrastructure); `GATE PASSED` (`-AllowProtected` for Core/Interfaces + board docs).
- **Tests:** the existing theme/localization suites ride along (using + reference deltas only — no new domain logic, the moved contracts keep their shape and XML docs byte-compatible); the build graph pins the direction (Livery compiles without Core while AI/Infrastructure compile with it).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `352dfab` (the contract move + the consumer rehome) and this release commit (the alpha.35 cut + this section's status flip); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.35` (see CHANGELOG.md). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).

## T-071 — Window chrome follow-ups: fix the black content pane + caption strip polish (user-reported)

- **Branch:** `feat/window-chrome-black` (worktree `zt-window-chrome-black`, base `develop` @ `2674f03`; reported 2026-11-06, developed the same day).
- **Origin:** with T-066 shipped the main window rendered as a black void — no charts, no menu; the user pinned the new chrome style as the culprit and was right.
- **What:**
  - The black pane: Avalonia 12 presents a template `ContentControl`'s content only through explicit bindings, and the fresh `Window.chrome`/`Window.chrome-compact` templates' `PART_ContentPresenter` carried none. `Content`, `ContentTemplate`, the `Padding` margin and both alignment bindings now complete both templates in `src/Livery/Themes/Controls.axaml` (`0d81f7d`), pinned by the `EveryChromeTemplate_PresentsTheWindowContent` regression in `WindowChromeTests`.
  - The strip polish: the title centers on the strip at 13px; the caption glyphs center in their 44×32 buttons at a 1.5 stroke (min/max/restore redrawn to a ~10×10 design box); hover feedback rides the ICON — Avalonia's Fluent paints button state fills on the template's `ContentPresenter`, so the presenter is suppressed part-wise, the button opacity carries the states (0.85 rest / 1.0 hover / 0.8 press), and the close glyph retints to the palette `Error` over the logical descendants — `ControlTheme` nesting forbids combinator-led styles and `/template/` never reaches button content (`defa610`).
- **Scope:** `src/Livery/Themes/Controls.axaml`, `tests/ZettaTrade.App.Tests/Views/WindowChromeTests.cs`.
- **Acceptance (achieved):** content renders in every chrome window again; live screenshots with pixel checks — the strip stays uniform under hover (`17,17,28`), the hovered close glyph alone takes the palette danger color (`239,68,68`), the rest glyphs dimmed per the Windows convention; `GATE PASSED`.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `0d81f7d` (the content-presentation fix) + `defa610` (the strip polish) plus the develop-sync bubble merge `308b2ab`; shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.36` (see CHANGELOG.md).


## T-072 — Order book empty under Bybit: the facade resolves the displayed depth against the served one (user-reported)

- **Branch:** `feat/order-book-depth` (worktree `zt-order-book-depth`, base `develop` @ `2674f03`; reported 2026-11-06, developed the same day).
- **Origin:** since the BUG-032 depth guard (`7ce1e0a`) the Bybit order book widget silently showed nothing: the widget rides the interface default of 20 levels while Bybit serves only `orderbook.1`/`orderbook.50` — the adapter rejects the request (an error by contract, never a silent default) and the guarded stream error emptied the widget (the rejection has been in the daily logs since 2026-10-18).
- **What:** the resolution moves to the request side while the adapter guard keeps rejecting: `ExchangeAdapterBase` gains the public `ServedBookDepths` face over its wire declaration and the pure `ResolveBookDepth(served, requested)` — the smallest served depth at least as deep as the request wins, else the deepest served one; a null/empty declaration (serve-anything adapters, the demo) passes the request through for the adapter to own. The `ActiveExchangeService` facade pre-resolves every book subscription against the ACTIVE adapter inside its switch (Bybit: 20 → 50, logged at Debug; Binance's stepped set unaffected).
- **Scope:** `ExchangeAdapterBase.cs` (the HIGH-RISK shared base — human-reviewed at the merge), `ActiveExchangeService.cs`, the resolution contract tests (`ExchangeAdapterBaseResolveBookDepthTests`) and the facade wiring tests (`ActiveExchangeServiceTests` gains a base-derived depthed stub adapter).
- **Tests:** 5 resolution cases (the exact serve stays, the nearest deep-enough wins, the deepest fallback, the between-served midpoint, the undeclared passthrough) + 2 facade cases (the default 20 resolves to 50 on a `{1,50}` adapter; a declared 25 rides unchanged).
- **Acceptance (achieved):** a live Bybit run loses its daily-log depth rejection and the widget renders the live ETH/USDT book (asks/bids with spread, dozens of levels — the 20→50 resolution visible); Binance/demo requests unchanged; `GATE PASSED`.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `e1332f2` (the resolution + its tests) and the develop-sync bubble merge `9da6989`; shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.37` (see CHANGELOG.md).

## T-073 — Flaky concurrency test: GetString_AFactoryParkedAcrossASwitch fails intermittently under parallel-suite load (queued from the T-071/T-072 landing)

- **Branch:** feat/flaky-localization (base `develop` at `8eeb5cc`; queued 2026-11-06; developed 2026-11-06).
- **Origin:** `LocalizationManagerTests.GetString_AFactoryParkedAcrossASwitch_ReturnsTheSnapshotCultureString` (the T-060/BUG-051 pin) failed twice during the T-071/T-072 landing verification — once inside the full parallel-solution run and once in a back-to-back solo rerun, both immediately after heavy builds — and passed every other time (solo ×7 including 3 runs at the pre-merge `1b9a717` base, full-suite ×1; both branch gates green). The failing assertion speaks the pre-BUG-051 symptom: the parked lookup returns a wrong-culture string.
- **What:** the root cause is the test harness, not the manager — the reproduction captured BOTH flake limbs of one family. (1) The parked fact's `"any lookup parked"` witness was class-wide, while the fact's `Task.Run` caller can stay queued past the switch under parallel-suite schedule pressure (the captured window carries 33 sibling tests; the 60-frame perf fact overlapped it) — a foreign lookup resolving the same process-wide `LocalizationManager.Instance` through production code (the order book layout's `Build` path, which parks in the same stamping fake) opened the witness for it, so the switch committed over a call that had never reached its snapshot; that call read the fresh culture and returned `'ru:Menu.File'` — EXACTLY the manager's documented snapshot-consistency promise ("a racing caller may still receive either the pre-switch or the post-switch string, but always the one consistent with its own snapshot"), so the manager is correct and the handshake was not. (2) The same instance coupling let parallel `Build` facts resolve the localization suites' stamping fake template ("en:OrderBook.SpreadFormat" — the captured `SpreadText` failure) and a DISPOSED manager keep `_instance` set, so `IsInitialized` stayed true for the whole process after the first creation. The fix: the handshake waits for the fact's OWN thread id at a per-name register (the snapshot is sealed before the switch may park), the `OrderBookPageLayoutTests` join the localization singleton collection (no foreign resolution during `Build`), and `LocalizationManager.Dispose` clears the static when it owns the slot. The `GetString` snapshot path itself stays byte-identical — the race was never in it.
- **Scope:** `tests/ZettaTrade.UI.Tests/Localization/LocalizationManagerTests.cs` (the per-name/thread handshake + the class remarks), `tests/ZettaTrade.UI.Tests/Charting/OrderBookPageLayoutTests.cs` (the collection join), `src/Livery/Localization/LocalizationManager.cs` (the Dispose self-clear), `Directory.Build.props` + `CHANGELOG.md` + board docs.
- **Acceptance:** (verified reality) the localization family holds zero recurrences post-fix — UI.Tests-only runs ×12 green and 21 full-solution runs at the tip/merged tree, where every CAPTURED failure belongs to the unrelated `BinanceAdapterWsE2eTests` mock-server 10 s timeout (two more such failures in the pre-capture post-fix loop are identity-unknown — PowerShell 5.1 `2>&1` swallowed the message bodies; the timeout family is the only residual discovered and is explicitly queued as T-116). The pre-fix reproduction: solution runs ×7 and UI.Tests-only runs ×10 flapped 3 times (failure texts + trx timing captured). The original ≥20-consecutive-green solution-run bar is superseded by the supervisor's merge instruction; the race explanation is written into the test headers.
- **Tests:** no new domain logic — the deterministic shape lives in the already-shipped parked-fact pin, whose handshake is now interference-proof; the reproducibility was established by rerunning the exact landing contexts (full solution / UI.Tests-only / heavy-build-then-solo) from a fresh worktree.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `1bf6506` (the handshake seal, the layout suite's collection join, the Dispose self-clear) and this release commit (the alpha.38 cut + this section's status flip); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.38` (see CHANGELOG.md). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file). The residual full-solution failures are the unrelated WS E2E `EventWait` family — queued as T-116.

## T-077 — Position actions: close, reverse, partial close (paper-first)

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-06 (supervisor idea at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut).

- **Scope:** positions panel VM + a service, confirmation wiring, adapter mapping, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** close-on-empty is rejected; reverse math is two validated orders; double-click while in flight is a no-op (idempotent disable).
- **Tests:** close/reduce/reverse goldens on paper, rejection paths, the in-flight disable case.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the position-actions pipeline (`6485f80`), the row action surface (`e309636`), the goldens/races/in-flight test pack (`8452cb9`) and this section's status flip; shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.46` (see CHANGELOG.md). No Core changes were needed - the actions compose validated orders through the existing trading seam (`OrderRequest.Validate` - `IRiskManager` - `IConfirmationService` - `ITradingAdapter`); the reverse is two risk-checked legs under ONE confirmation, an outside fill wins and is reported, and an exit that leaves the symbol non-flat aborts the re-entry with a localized report. ## T-078 - Order ticket confirmation: the pre-submit summary

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-06 (supervisor idea at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut).
- **Origin:** `IRiskManager`/`IConfirmationService` exist (T-057) but the gate is invisible; traders want one look at the order economics before submit.
- **What:** a modal confirm listing side/qty/type/limit/exchange, the paper-live tag prominently, estimated notional vs the hard cap headroom and an estimated fee line; defaults ON for live, OFF in paper; a settings key to skip below a configurable notional; market orders always confirm in live.
- **Scope:** a confirm dialog + ticket VM wiring, `Settings.*` keys + reactive settings change, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** the confirm composes once from the final ticket state at submit — no per-keystroke re-valuation, no double-Enter double submit.
- **Tests:** pass/block matrix, the settings-driven skip, double-Enter submit protection.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the submit-consultation lane — the `OrderConfirmPolicy` settings gate, the notional anchor helpers and the detailed composer summary with the resx en+ru `Trading.Confirm*` keys (`27c6267`), shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.53` (see CHANGELOG.md). Scope decisions: the policy reads the settings fresh at submit time instead of subscribing to changes (an external reload delivers a boxed `JsonElement` — typed handlers would silently miss the flip; one read per submit is one final-state consultation, exactly the acceptance), the anchor follows the stop family (stop-limit/trailing stop anchor on the stop price, the captured reference close substitutes only where no ticket price exists), risk-mandated confirmation is ORed in by the caller and never relaxable, and the double-Enter guard is one `SemaphoreSlim` held across the whole submit handler (build → risk → confirmation → placement) — a parked dialog can neither be re-confirmed nor re-placed. Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).

## T-085 — Local candle cache in SQLite (write-through, offline start)

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-06 (supervisor idea at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut).
- **Origin:** every start re-requests the history; EF Core + SQLite are in place since T-010 and the upsert-race lesson is T-061.
- **What:** a write-through candle cache keyed (adapter, symbol, interval), upserted by open time; startup serves from cache, then refreshes over the feeds; an eviction cap and a `Caching.*` settings section; EF migrations per canon.
- **Scope:** Infrastructure persistence + the chart VM's bootstrap path, `Caching.*` settings keys, tests.
- **Acceptance:** a fresh start renders instantly from cache with the refresh riding behind; overlap conflates, never duplicates.
- **Tests:** upsert idempotency across restarts, range-overlap golden, eviction cap, the check-then-act race pinned.
- **Status:** done. Chosen cut: the `ICandleCache` seam lives in Infrastructure next to the other persistence services (Core untouched, 0 diff). The store (`SqliteCandleCacheStore`) rides the T-061 lesson exactly: per-operation contexts over singleton `UseSqlite` options, ONE raw upsert statement per bar inside one committed transaction — `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (AdapterId, Symbol, TimeFrame, OpenTime) DO UPDATE` with values pre-converted through the shared persistence converters (`OrderHistoryConversions`) so the stored TEXT stays byte-consistent with the entity pipeline — so the check-then-act race is impossible by shape and an overlap conflates instead of duplicating; the eviction keeps each series' newest `Caching.MaxBarsPerSeries` bars (default 100 000, the chart target's depth; `Caching.Enabled` switches the whole cache off) in the same transaction, with the policy swapped live on settings changes (the `FeeScheduleService` reactive pattern, snapshot probe capped the polling fact). `InitialCandleCache` rides the shared `Persistence\Migrations` folder via the context's design-time factory; the store initializes during the composition root's startup walk (`AddCandleCache()` after `AddPnlServices`). The chart's bootstrap is `Concat(cached page, fetch)` — the newest cached window (depth+1 bars) delivers first, the network refresh lands behind it and every adapter-served set persists write-through BEFORE its page delivers (`LoadBars` and the older-page fetch both ride it); a cache fault logs and the fetch page still delivers, an offline start keeps the cached bars: the cache is bookkeeping, never a source of truth. Tests: 11 store facts (OHLCV round-trip, restart idempotency, range-overlap golden, the two-store concurrent-writer race pin, the eviction cap with per-series budgets, series isolation, the disabled no-op double-verified, the live policy reload) + 3 chart facts (cache-first ordering with write-through-before-delivery, the failing-fetch offline world, the faulting-cache world). Canon: the branch carries `7d09192` (the board claim), `c691702` (the store + migration + wiring + both test suites), `688e840` (the `alpha.51` cut + the CHANGELOG section), `28de3cb` (the gate's rule round — the fake's fault-arm without the sync wrap, zero behavior change); this docs flip lands as its own commit; shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.51` (see CHANGELOG.md). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).

## T-086 — Deep-history backfill and gap repair

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-06 (supervisor idea at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut). **Blocker:** T-085.

- **What:** on demand past cached ranges, fetch older segments and stitch without overlap or dup; a calendar-aware gap detector (session/timeframe calendar) with a repair pass on subscription; backpressure rides the existing `Channel<T>` legs; per-adapter depth ability surfaced (some feeds cap history — display the limit, never fabricate).
- **Scope:** the fetcher service + cache write-through (T-085), chart VM integration, tests.
- **Acceptance:** after an outage of hours, the chart heals itself on restart/subscription.
- **Tests:** stitch golden, gap detection over calendars, depth-limited adapter behavior, batching against T-106 later.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the repair lane (`4ecf25a`) — the venue calendar + the gap detector (`CandleGap.IsTruncated` bound semantics), the bounded-segment fetcher with write-before-emit (`CandleBackfillService`) and the bounded `Channel<T>` backpressure leg, the chart-side repair chain (an `Interlocked`-claimed epoch per delivery, marshalled merges, the stale-stamp drop, the localized depth note) and the additive `ICandleCache.GetOlderThanAsync` probe; shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.54` (see CHANGELOG.md). Scope decisions: repair and detection ride the App-owned seams and the T-085 cache (`ICandleCache` grows in Infrastructure next to the store — Core untouched, 0 diff); the feed's depth limit surfaces per series as the localized `Chart.HistoryLimit` note (an explicit boundary, never a fabricated continuation); a hole wider than one bounded pass's 1200-open enumerate stays the paging machinery's own subject (batching against the T-106 limiter), while a hole exactly at the bound is one whole fetch (the detector's truncation boundary is pinned by the twin facts); contiguous cached coverage only extends the detection context behind the head, never the displayed depth (the cache is context, not a source). The T-085 blocker was already satisfied on develop (alpha.51). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).

## T-089 — Chart types: line, area, bars, Heikin-Ashi

- **Branch:** feat/chart-types (base `develop` at `1b9fcf4`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-08).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the chart-types lane (`ba4937d`, claim `2bb1183`) — the rendering vocabulary (`ZettaTrade.Charting.ChartSeriesType`: Candlesticks keeps its pre-selector status as the restore default, Line/Area/Bars/HeikinAshi as renderer variants off the SAME candle source — only the renderer differs, never a second data path), the line/area close polyline (translucent fill down to the plot bottom under the Area variant, the theme text color so the price line and the VWAP/SMA overlay accents never blend), the OHLC-bar stems with open/close ticks at half the candle body width (the same bullish/bearish paints as the candles), the client-side Heikin-Ashi transform (`ZettaTrade.Charting.HeikinAshi`: left-to-right chain — first bar seeded (O+C)/2, thereafter (prevHA-O + prevHA-C)/2, HA-C the four-price mean, HA-H/HA-L the envelope extrema; the transform runs ONCE per delivered series, never per draw pass — every HA open depends on the previous HA bar, so a window-local chain would shift the first visible bar on every pan) published aligned with `Bars` by index through the overlays' raw-then-marshal split (a stale publish races into a raw-candlestick frame, never a silently shifted series; the times and volumes pass through so slots, the volume-at-price histogram and the hover readout keep their semantics); the overlays always consume the RAW window — the VWAP band, the profile histogram, the indicator lines and the no-volume note never follow the renderer variant; the padded price spread rides the per-type `ChartSeriesSpread` (candles/bars/HA spread over the full low–high of the DRAWN bars, line/area over closes only — the wicks are not drawn geometry, so nothing a frame paints can clip the plot). The selector persistence is the per-symbol JSON map `UI.ChartSymbolTypes` (`ZettaTrade.App.Services.SymbolChartTypeMap`: a fail-safe parse — blank/malformed/non-object documents, non-string values, blank keys and unrecognized type names each cost at most their own entry, never the parse; the STRICT-NAME contract — the value must name a defined `ChartSeriesType` and numeric strings never ride `Enum.TryParse`'s integral path, per the no-substitution trading-safety rule; serialization the exact inverse in normalized ordinal order, `<c>{}</c>` for an empty map; `WithEntry` writes exactly the addressed symbol and persists even a pick equal to the default — the user chose it), the flip pipeline lifts the cache at issue time in issuance order so a rapid burst never drops its second write and re-serializes the whole map under the workspace symbol, an addressed flip with no symbol stands transient (no context, no document), every restore path (the construction seed, a symbol switch, a live settings-service change) applies through the `_isApplyingPersistedChartType` gate so a restore is a read, never a rewrite (the BUG-015 shape the overlay choices keep), and a symbol without an entry keeps the standing type — a preference map substitutes nothing; the combo wears the `SelectedChartTypeIndex` two-way shim (the `SelectedThemeIndex` precedent: static localized labels never live on ViewModel properties). Shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.64` (see CHANGELOG.md). Scope decisions: the type-flip streamline recomputes the HA series against the CURRENT `Bars` immediately (the pure transform over the series' own copy); the HA golden stays a Charting-level fact — the VM facts pin only the publish discipline (alignment + the null-off contract), never re-deriving the math. Shipped tests: the HA math goldens (exact first-bar seed, chain pooling, empty in/out), purity/determinism (the input never mutated), the per-bar envelope containment + the window spread superset, the per-type ChartSeriesSpread boundary universe, the map's storage matrix (fail-safe garbage → empty, individual entry drops with the tolerable-key remainder, stable ordinal serialization, the round trip, the WithEntry isolation), and the five ViewModel facts (the shim flip persists the whole map both through the index shim and the canonical property, the persisted restore without a rewrite across the entry / no-entry / return walk, the no-context transient until the first address, the Heikin-Ashi publish alignment + the non-type nulls, the live settings change re-address). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (`Directory.Build.props` alpha.64 is the supervisor-only protected file, the rule-6 alpha duty). Hazards: the known parallel-load App.Tests timing family flared once during the session (a lone PositionsPanel timeout that retried clean solo, recorded not fixed in-flight).
- **Origin:** candles only; line/area are the intraday standard, HA smooths the noise.
- **What:** renderer variants off the existing candle source; Heikin-Ashi computed client-side from OHLC; per-symbol persistence of the choice alongside the workspace symbol (the T-029/T-055 restore discipline).
- **Scope:** Charting renderers + the chart VM switcher, tests.
- **Acceptance:** every variant keeps the axis-bucket invariants (T-035/T-037 canon) and the pan/paging behaviors.
- **Tests:** HA derivation golden, per-type axis invariants, choice persistence restore.
- **Shipped tests** (beyond the board's three): the shim-vs-canonical flip surfaces, the restore-gate no-write proof across the three address walks, the no-context transient, the publish-alignment and off-type-null contract, the map's garbage/round-trip matrix, the transform purity and the window-spread superset.

## T-090 — Y-axis scale modes: log and percent

- **Branch:** feat/chart-scales, worktree zt-chart-scales, based on develop@9fc27da (claimed 2026-11-09; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut; taken autonomously by the supervisor session after the T-138/T-104 double landing, per the user's standing "take the next tasks" directive).
- **Origin:** log is mandatory for long ranges; % change is the relative read; both absent.
- **What:** a log price mode with a strict positive-domain guard (invalid candles clamp visibly, never default), and a percent-change mode anchored to the first visible close; axis labels/formatting follow the T-037 time-grid counterpart on the price side; per-symbol persistence.
- **Scope:** Charting scale transforms within the existing axis pipeline, tests.
- **Acceptance:** wheel zoom stays stable across modes; switching never loses the visible window.
- **Tests:** transform goldens, domain edge cases, the mode-switch window guarantee.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch lands `9ce1de5` (single feat commit: code + tests + the alpha.81 bump + the CHANGELOG section). The design is the TRANSFORMED-DOMAIN shape: the pixel pipeline stays linear and maps axis values — `ZettaTrade.UI.Charting.ChartPriceTransform` (`ToAxisValue`/`ToPrice`, the base-10 log and the anchored percent read) owns the mode's semantics, the spread transforms the drawn extremes before `ChartPriceScale.PaddedSpread`, and every raw-price mapping site wraps its argument in the draw operation's `AxisOf` (candles, wicks, OHLC bars, the close line, indicator lines, the VWAP center/upper/lower bands, signal and fill markers, the working orders, the position entry line and PnL tag, the volume-at-price buckets) — the grid and price-axis tick sites stay unwrapped because their values are already axis-domain. The strict positive-domain guard clamps any price outside the mode's domain VISIBLY onto the drawn geometry's smallest positive candidate (`PositiveFloor`, the `LogFallbackFloor = 1m` keeps an all-corrupt window total): the candle draws at the clamped position, the mode never silently falls back to linear, nothing throws and nothing drops. The percent mode anchors on the window's first visible RAW close; the acceptance's window guarantee holds by construction — the transforms are stateless functions of the window's data, the pan window is mode-foreign state, so a mode switch re-maps the same bars in place (the transform-fact `ModeSwitch_IsAStatelessRemapOfTheSameWindow` pins it). Axis labels per mode: the log mode prints prices (the log tick unwinds through `ToPrice`, with the RAW-price decimals — the log-range decimals are log-unit digits, nonsense on a price axis); the percent mode prints the signed read (`FormatPercentTick`: a leading `+` on positives, a percent tail); the hover pill follows the axis semantics, while the OHLC readout keeps quoting raw prices with raw decimals. The mode selector rides the chart toolbar next to the rendering type (three static localized items bound by index — the `SelectedChartTypeIndex` shim precedent, resx `Chart.ScaleMode*` en+ru) and persists PER SYMBOL as `UI.ChartSymbolScales` (`ZettaTrade.App.Services.SymbolScaleModeMap`, the T-089 map's fail-safe clone: garbage costs entries, never the parse; strict enum-name restore, whole-map writes, the cache-leads pipeline under the `_isApplyingPersistedScaleMode` restore gate). GATE: run 1 FAILED on the [FORMAT] lint only (the formatter roots a switch expression's braces at the closer's indent when the parameter list is multi-line — the two class-level switches in `ChartPriceTransform` shifted +4; build and tests had passed), run 2 GATE PASSED. Verdicts: the solution build green, UI.Tests 289/289 (the 12 transform facts: log goldens on powers of ten, the visible-clamp edges, the anchored percent goldens, the inverses, the identity, the window guarantee), App.Tests 1232/1232 (the map facts + the three VM facts: the flip persists the whole map, the restore re-asserts without rewriting, the settings change re-addresses live). Shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.81` after the launch smoke; merged into develop by fast-forward.

## T-092 — Standard indicator pack (bundled plugins)

- **Branch:** feat/indicator-pack (base `develop` at `086579b`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-07).
- **Origin:** only the seam + an SMA sample (T-034) exist; the standard set is missing while the plugin machinery is ready (T-054, T-067, T-068).
- **What:** EMA, WMA, RSI, MACD, ATR and Bollinger as separate bundled plugin assemblies (plugin-first canon), registered into the existing catalog, parameter editors through the loaded-plugins gate, reference-formula tests over fixed series.
- **Scope:** the new plugin assemblies + catalog registration + resx for names, tests.
- **Acceptance:** cold start pays no cost for unloaded plugins; formulas match reference vectors.
- **Tests:** formula goldens, scan tolerance, settings roundtrip per plugin.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the pack (`165ea40`) — six separate plugin assemblies in one consistent pattern (the parameterless constructor falls back to each family's classic period, the catalog-facing `ctor(int)` carries the row's period knob, an out-of-range or collapsed construction throws — the catalog skips the overlay row then, nothing defaults silently): `EmaIndicator` (seed = the plain mean of the first period closes, then one alpha = 2/(N+1) step per bar), `WmaIndicator` (the linear ladder weights the newest close heaviest — weights N..1 over the weight sum N*(N+1)/2), `RsiIndicator` (Wilder's conventions: the seeded first gain/loss averages, the (prev*(N-1)+move)/N smoothing, the zero-loss shortcut pinning 100 instead of dividing by zero), `MacdIndicator` (the fast/slow seeded-EMA spread, the canonical 26/9 slow/signal pinned behind the row's fast knob, a fast length at or beyond the slow one throws), `AtrIndicator` (Wilder's smoothed true ranges, the opening bar's plain range standing in for the missing prior close), `BollingerIndicator` (the upper band is the window SMA + k population-deviation sigmas with the classic k = 2 — the middle is exactly the SMA sample's line, the lower band mirrors); the registration seam decision: NO new catalog surface and NO composition edits — the pack registers exactly the way the SMA sample has always registered (the unchanged `PluginIndicatorCatalog` scan: load-from-plugins-directory, the ctor-probe name read, first-registration-wins on duplicates, the tolerant skip of broken neighbors), the settings gate keys every pack row on the catalog's `LoadedNames` snapshot through the T-067 loaded-plugins gate (a row renders only for a plugin the session actually owns, a save without a plugin never fabricates a config row for it, and the shared `TryPersistIndicatorRow` helper generalizes the SMA row's draft contract so the tab still lands as one settings write), and the row hints ride new `Chart.Indicators.*Tip` keys in en and ru (the checkbox labels stay the plugins' canonical product names); the numeric-fact discipline: every formula pinned by hand-computed golden vectors over fixed closes/bars chosen so expectations land whole or re-derive with the same decimal division (repeated decimals exact rather than rounded stand-ins, the Bollinger irrational windows riding the shared decimal-to-double-to-decimal root pipeline byte-exact), plus ctor-boundary facts (each rejected construction throws instead of substituting a default), the pack-registration facts (the seven-name scan, the per-name create roundtrips, the row's period reaching the plugin, rejected-period warnings, drive-by neighbor tolerance and the cold-start charter: production assemblies reference ZERO plugin assemblies, pinned by reflection with the test assembly as the negative control) and the settings facts (the per-name row derivation, the roundtrip per name, the no-op silence, the unreadable-period skip, the hidden-row gate and the foreign-row ride); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.60` (see CHANGELOG.md). Scope decisions: ONE period knob per row — MACD's slow/signal lengths and Bollinger's deviation count stay canonical constants (constructable in full for embeddings and the tests; the settings seam's single integer is T-067's contract, not extended); the Bollinger row renders the UPPER band through the single-series seam (recorded gap: a fuller band trio joins when the seam grows past one series per indicator); the plugin assemblies join the solution build (the slnx lists them, the test project compiles them once and stages copies into per-fact scan-fixture directories at runtime — production never references them). Hazards: the plugins folder is a user-managed deployment surface — the pack reaches a session only when its assemblies are copied under the data plugins directory, the cold-start charter pins that an unloaded pack costs nothing; two timing-flaky suites flaked during verification and both passed on rerun (the App panel in-flight family and the T-116 Binance resubscribe family, both outside this task's lanes). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).

## T-093 — VWAP + volume profile overlay

- **Branch:** feat/vol-vwap (base `develop` at `c2b1443`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, claimed and developed 2026-11-08 — two sessions, the second finishing the dead one's uncommitted rendering draft).
- **Origin:** the intraday VWAP and volume-at-price histogram are standard reads; both absent.
- **What:** session-anchored VWAP (optional ±1σ bands) and a right-margin volume-at-price histogram over the visible window; active only when candles carry volume — an explicit "no volume data" state otherwise (never zero-fabricated).
- **Scope:** Charting overlays + the indicator seam reuse, tests.
- **Acceptance:** session anchors handle day boundaries of the T-037 calendar correctly.
- **Tests:** VWAP boundary math golden, histogram bucket alignment, the no-volume state.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the compute (`4b1dd1a`) — `VwapSessionAnchor` (the UTC calendar-day break rule: crypto venues trade around the clock, so the UTC day is the only venue-honest anchor their series carries; the stock-style venues' weekday sessions break on the same rule because their quiet days carry no bars at all, and a series opening mid-session anchors the accumulation at its own first bar), `VwapOverlay` (the per-session accumulation of the typical price (H+L+C)/3 with the bar volumes as weights, flanked by the ±1σ volume-weighted standard-deviation bands; exact-decimal goldens, a zero-share bar carries the standing value, an unweighted session holds nulls, `Slice` re-slices by index and rebases the session starts against the visible window, the honest `NoVolumeData` state) and `VolumeProfile` (the even shared-edge bucket ladder over the window's spread — each bucket's high IS the next bucket's low, the last closes at the window's high; the typical price owns exactly one bucket, the volume conserves exactly, the flat spread stays a one-bucket edge, a sub-one count throws); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.62` (see CHANGELOG.md). The integration (`bf0004b`) finished the dead session's draft: the chart slices the published overlay with the SAME [start, end) window as the bars (the indicator-lines alignment rule — a stale publish is skipped wholesale) and draws the center line solid in the theme indicator color over the dashed translucent ±1σ bands, every session break in the window cutting all three polylines and a null value breaking the pen, while the histogram recomputes the pure `VolumeProfile.Compute` over the visible snapshot every pass — exactly what the lens shows — with the bars hugging the plot's right edge, translated from the heaviest bucket onto the shared price scale (never its own axis); the honest no-volume note (`Chart.NoVolumeData`, en+ru) prints centered when the published overlay reports the series carries no volume at all or the toggled histogram's window holds none — never a fabricated zero or a spread shape; the toolbar toggles sit beside the time-frame picker (`Chart.Vwap` / `Chart.VolumeProfile` labels + `Chart.Vwap.Tip` / `Chart.VolumeProfile.Tip` tooltips, en+ru, static strings via `{loc:Localized}` — never ViewModel properties; the on state rides the `Classes.<flag>` pattern), the settings keys are `UI.ChartShowVwap` / `UI.ChartShowVolumeProfile` (both default on through `UiSettingsKeys`), and the ViewModel wiring reuses the house shapes: the BUG-015-class every-path-first restore seeds the persisted caches before the early returns, the BUG-053 cache-leads persistence pipeline takes the toggles (the write serializes behind the issued value in issuance order, a read never rewrites), the publishes ride the raw `VwapOverlayUpdates` stream with only the property assignment marshalled (the delivered/prepend/repair/toggle cadence, exactly the indicator lines' publish sites), and the settings-notify stream re-applies an external writer live. Scope decisions: the overlay computation rides the existing compute cadence with no new services or composition edits, and the histogram deliberately recomputes per draw pass instead of publishing — a pan must never see a profile stale against the lens, and its cost is bounded by the visible window, never the loaded series. Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).

## T-094 — Chart export: PNG snapshot + CSV candles

- **Branch:** feat/chart-export, worktree zt-chart-export, based on develop@67dfbdf (claimed 2026-11-09; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut; taken autonomously by the supervisor session right after the T-090 landing, per the user's standing "take the next tasks" directive).

- **Scope:** Charting + a menu item, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** the export matches the on-screen frame (same lens, same annotations state).
- **Tests:** pixel-diff smoke on a seeded chart, watermark presence, path/error handling.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch lands `8194d59` (single feat commit: code + tests + the alpha.82 bump + the CHANGELOG section). The PNG snapshot renders the CURRENT frame — the acceptance holds by construction: the export's render seam (`CandlestickChart.RenderSnapshotPngBytes`, internal; the public `ExportSnapshotPngAsync` wraps it with the pure path guard and the file write) builds the same `CreateSnapshot()` and paints through the SAME nested `CandlestickDrawOperation` the on-screen render runs, at the fixed `ExportScale = 2` supersampling on a raster `SKSurface`, then stamps the translucent watermark (alpha 140, the `PngWatermark` "symbol · timeframe" composition — data, not prose, no resx) bottom-right. The suggested file name (`PngFileName`: the symbol made file-safe via `Path.GetInvalidFileNameChars`, the time frame, the UTC timestamp to the second, culture-invariant) pre-fills the generalized `SaveFilePicker` (the CSV-only picker generalized to an extension/label pair; the three CSV call sites keep their shape). The visible-range CSV hands the control's new public `VisibleBarsSlice` (the pan window resolved by the shared `CopyVisibleWindow` `CreateSnapshot` also consumes — no duplicated span math) through the view interaction (`VisibleCsvHandoff(path, bars)`) into the shared `ICsvExportService.ExportCandlesAsync` — never the whole loaded series by accident. The ViewModel owns the outcomes only: `ExportPngCommand`/`ExportPngRequested` and `ExportVisibleCandlesCommand`/`ExportVisibleCsvRequested` interactions, a no-symbol pick = a quiet skip (the transient-pick rule), a cancelled dialog = quiet nothing, a render or write fault = the localized `Chart.ExportFailed` error line (the candles-CSV flow's clone). GATE: run 1 GATE PASSED (the [PACKAGES] warning is the UI csproj's `InternalsVisibleTo` for the test project — no new package; the HIGH-RISK flag on OrderHistoryWindow.axaml.cs is the mechanical picker rename, three call sites, the same call shape). Verdicts: the solution build green, App.Tests 1246/1246 (14 new facts: the VM flows — the request shape, the quiet cancels, the localized faults, the no-symbol skip, the exact-slice hand-through, the two builder goldens; the control facts — the visible slice of a tiny/empty series, the byte-determinism pixel-diff smoke, the watermark-ink differ, the PNG signature + the 640×400 supersampled dims + the full-sweep distinct-colors non-blank probe, the public guard), UI.Tests 289/289. Test-harness canon: the control facts marshal through the pump-owned dispatcher (`Dispatcher.UIThread.Post` + the T-138 pump's `DrainAsync` + a `TaskCompletionSource` completed INSIDE the job — `InvokeAsync`'s returned task never completes under `RunJobs` and hangs); the render needs `Measure`+`Arrange` to size `Bounds`; without an `Application` the theme colors fall back to the sane dark constants, and the honest non-blank probe is a full every-3rd-pixel sweep (a sparse 5×5 sample grid lands in the candles' gaps). Shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.82` after the launch smoke; merged into develop by fast-forward.

## T-095 — Global symbol palette (Ctrl+K)

- **Branch:** feat/symbol-palette (base `develop` at `086579b`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-07).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the palette's match rules (`FuzzySymbolMatcher`, one pure culture-free scorer) — the adapters' catalogs are prepared ONCE per snapshot as uppercase `MatchKey`s (`Symbol.Normalized.ToUpperInvariant()` fixed at the source, never per query; the scans stay ordinal), the rank is tiered so a better shape always outranks a better bonus inside a worse tier (exact leading prefix > word-start contiguous hit > anywhere-contiguous > in-order subsequence > one-typo-tolerant subsequence — exactly one skipped query character, beyond it the instrument noise reads as misses) and inside a tier the score prefers the query's coverage (flat per covered character), a first match character on a word boundary (the key's head or right after `/ . - _`), fewer characters skipped BETWEEN two matched characters and the typo escape's own cost; ties keep the input order, a blank query pages the natural order (the ViewModel's blank page leads with the session's recents, then the favorites, then the registration order, every page capped at 24). Keyboard route: the window's `Ctrl+K` binding toggles the palette (`Palette.ToggleCommand` — closed opens, open closes), the overlay is a `Grid` sibling riding ON TOP of the workspace so its key route can never mix with the workspace's own key surface; the row navigation lives in the code-behind route over the overlay subtree alone, added with `handledEventsToo: true` so the query box's caret handling of the arrows cannot eat the row keys; the route carries Up/Down/PageUp/PageDown (bounds checked before every dispatch so a keystroke never executes a gated command), Enter (apply the highlighted row, or swallow when no row), Esc (close) and the palette never dispatches a command other than through the view's own logged error-handled execute; the focus enters and leaves through the two interactions (the window stashes the pre-open focused element as the return point, retried under render priority like the T-012 chat focus) and a press on the scrim closes; the rows list is pointer-only (`Focusable=False` on the list and items) so a click keeps the query holding the keyboard. Adapter-source decisions (`SymbolPaletteSource`): ONE epoch-guarded behavioral snapshot over ALL registered adapters (the palette is workspace-level, unlike the autocomplete riding the active adapter), warmed through the startup walk (`IAsyncInitializable`, non-blocking — a failed warm-up leaves the honest loading state), every adapter-change emission kicks a re-fetch and an epoch bump drops the superseded composition's late publish; a faulting adapter contributes zero rows and logs — nothing fabricated (the no-default rule), the healthy adapters' rows still serve; per-adapter duplicate normalized pairs stay two rows (the exchange is part of the pair's identity); a same-exchange apply writes `ChartViewModel.SelectedSymbol` directly while a cross-exchange apply FIRST carries the pair into the same `Workspace.SymbolId` preference the chart's setter maintains and THEN switches — the switch-kicked pending bootstrap reads the applied pair as the preference and delivers it with the exchange already current, so the chart's synchronous rows-rebuild rebase (discovered hazard: the shown rows still belonged to the previous exchange while the target bootstrap was pending, so a plain post-switch write was rebased to the old exchange's head AND re-persisted over the applied choice — `AppCrossExchange` probe facts) can never replace the applied choice; shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.61` (see CHANGELOG.md). Hazards: the worktree inherited diagnostic probe scaffolding from a dead sibling session — dropped (the reveals had served their purpose); the branch base `086579b` predates develop's alpha.59/60 landings, so the `Directory.Build.props` bump (alpha.58→alpha.61 per the assigned release) and the CHANGELOG section head conflict trivially at the landing merge (resolve to alpha.61 / the section on top); one `PositionsPanelViewModelTests` flake tripped under the sibling WS-E2E load series (the documented T-116 story) and passed on the solo retry.
- **Origin:** T-014's autocomplete lives inside the ticket only; a Ctrl+K palette is the terminal norm.
- **What:** a fuzzy palette over all adapters' instruments with recents and the existing favorites store as inputs (read-only — the favorites area is parked task A's turf), keyboard-first, Enter applies the workspace symbol context (T-017 discipline); backdrop styling via the chrome canon.
- **Scope:** palette VM/view, instrument source wiring, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** opens under 100 ms with thousands of instruments; esc/dismiss returns focus cleanly.
- **Tests:** fuzzy ranking golden, per-adapter empty states, hotkey conflict check with existing bindings.

## T-096 — Price and percent-move alerts with a manager

- **Branch:** feat/price-alerts (base `develop` at `a2acdeb`, rebased onto `c527dee` at landing; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-08 supervisor-solo).
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.71 (feat `76a1e44`). The engine (a session singleton) is the roster's SOLE writer — create, edit re-arm, snooze and delete ride one single-flight gate and it alone authors the `Alerts / Definitions` settings key; it deliberately takes no `Settings.Changes` subscription (the sole-writer canon makes an external payload edit out of contract, and the drain would deadlock the gate). The evaluation is purely stream-driven: one ticker subscription per evaluatable symbol over the active-exchange facade, every arriving observation routed through an unbounded channel into one serial consumer — roster work and tick work never interleave; a per-observation fault is logged and swallowed (the route lives), a malformed observation is dropped, nothing is polled and there are no wake timers. Edge semantics exact in `AlertEdge` (the ONE decision place): Above is strictly greater, Below strictly less, At is exact decimal equality (no tolerance either direction), an unseen trigger is an argument fault — never a default; the percent move evaluates the OLDEST observation the engine still holds inside the alert's own window by exact-or-greater (the equality boundary belongs to the alert), the cleared history means the window is not covered yet and the alert waits, and the per-symbol history is time-pruned to the roster's widest active window with a pathological-rate count backstop. The fire is persistent: the definition becomes Fired with the qualifying tick's own stamp, the roster persists, a Fired alert is terminal and stays in the manager as the honest record (a reload can never re-fire it), the beep rides the `IAlertSoundPlayer` degrade lane — kernel32 `Beep` on Windows (a classic `[DllImport]`, no unsafe), `afplay`/Ping on macOS, the paplay→aplay ladder on Linux — and a failed beep is a logged warning, never a broken evaluation or a crash. The snooze pauses evaluation until the window expires and re-arms on the NEXT arriving tick (the re-arm tick itself evaluates nothing, the next one does — tested stream-side). Drafts are validated before the engine ever sees them: `AlertDraft.Validate` demands a parsable spelling under the module's own vocabulary (`IsAParsableSpelling`, because `Symbol.Parse` itself is lenient — a one-part spelling is a legitimate stock ticker: at most two non-empty letter/digit/dot/dash tokens), a strictly positive target or percent+window, an unseen kind is a fault; a refused draft never joins the roster and never persists. Persistence: one versioned JSON payload through `AlertCodec` — the root is all-or-nothing (malformed JSON and a wrong shape throw the same uniform `ArgumentException`; a broken store is the caller's logged fault, never a fabricated empty roster), while each entry is exact-or-skipped with a counted skip (an entry's stored facts stay untouched in the key; no half-decoded roster is substituted); the failed write logs loud while the live roster carries on. The manager window (modal, opened via the menu's `ShowAlertsRequested` interaction route like the other windows): a transient `AlertsViewModel` per open over the singleton engine, the attach handle owns the event + language subscriptions so a dead window never lingers; rows re-render live from the engine's events and re-resolve on language switches (the picker option labels recompose too, the selection survives); the editor loads the selected row, every draft field parses explicitly with its own localized refusal line, and the row commands gate on the selection. resx `Alert.*` en+ru 33 keys + `Menu.Trading.Alerts`, registered in `check-resx` (chore `304a436` rides the branch). Tests: 46 new — the edge matrix (strict above/below, exact-at, the percent boundary and argument faults), the codec's round-trip/exact-or-skip persistence facts, the engine's end-to-end stream facts over a fake adapter (no-fire-then-fire pairs proven through the FIFO route, the tick-stamped fire, side-window snooze re-arm, reload never re-fires, the sound degrade, refused garbage never persists, unknown-id refusals), and the manager VM (localized refusals, valid drafts, the editor load, the language re-render with the surviving selection, and the attach handle's dispose). Scope notes recorded honestly: the alert→order leg is out of scope (no auto-trading without `IConfirmationService`) and the toast leg rides T-097's stack when it may. Gate: run 1 failed ONLY on the scanner's two `[LOCK]` hits (the test fixture's `lock (census)` census — converted to a `ConcurrentQueue` ladder); run 2 green, and the post-rebase supervisor re-run green on its first pass. Known-flake record: the `App.Tests` full-suite tallies under load stayed in the family (1 fail, then a 35-fail tally, then 1043/1043 green in consecutive manual runs) — recorded, never widened scope to fix in-flight.

## T-097 — Notification center: toasts, history, DND

- **Branch:** feat/notif-center (base `develop` at `c2b1443`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-08).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the center (`f292e0c` + `7198c77`, claim `8678944`, resx grant `87ec50b`) — the vocabulary (`Notification` record + `NotificationKind`) with the factored factories (`ForJournalOutcome`, `ForAdapterState`, `ForDoNotDisturbSummary`, `ComposeDigest`) never carrying display text (rows compose it at arrival from the localization catalog), the click vocabulary (`NotificationRoute` + the static `NotificationRouteMap.Map` as the one tested answer per kind: fill → trade statistics, cancel/reject → order journal, adapter state → NO panel (the health strip is always visible, the click only dismisses), the DND summary → the notification history itself, an unnamed kind faults instead of defaulting), the publish surface (`NotificationCenter`: one `Publish` over the serialized inbound feed; every published event appends the history ring UNCONDITIONALLY before the display pipeline decides toastability — a mute silences the popup, never the journal; `PublishTradeOutcome` announces only terminal outcomes and drops a zero-filled Filled row exactly as the trade statistics scan does), the display pipeline per subscription (mute seeds + the DND flag seeded ONCE per subscription from the reactive settings store and re-applied live through the change stream — an external settings-file edit lands without a restart; the muted-kinds filter FIRST so muted events never toast and never count in a DND summary; per-event Do-Not-Disturb classification at arrival: candidate or missed, exactly once; the missed events fold per active span into EXACTLY one system summary counting the span's missed (not muted!) events, emitted the moment the closing flip lands), the journal tap (`NotifyingOrderHistoryStore` over `AddOrderHistory`'s SQLite artifacts — the last `IOrderHistoryStore` binding wins so every flow and the MCP lane ride the tap; the inner store's migrations ride the startup walk once through the tap's delegating `IAsyncInitializable`; every announcement happens only AFTER the journal write succeeded; a cancellation's symbol recovers best-effort from the journal of record and a row the journal never had still announces the adapter-confirmed cancel EMPTY-symbol — the symbol is never invented), the adapter tap (`AdapterStateNotificationSource` over the T-098 health fold: the first snapshot per adapter is the bootstrap state and does NOT announce (a startup never shouts disconnected), repeats don't either, `Unsupervised` clears the last-known entry so a re-bound adapter re-announces its first real state), the toast host (`NotificationToastHostViewModel`, an app-lifetime singleton lane like the status bar's streams: newest-on-top writes behind `ObserveOnUiThread`, every timing fact lives on the publishing threads — the overlay never blocks the user-interface thread; a stack of five visible with the tail trims, one 5 s expiry timer per row torn down with its removal, the burst badge on digests), the click routing (rows pose their route through the host's `Interaction` to the window's single handler — the T-012 window-plumbing precedent: the ViewModel never opens windows; a no-route click only dismisses; the main window's switch faults an unnamed route through the log, never a silently swallowed click), the notification history window (a transient view model per open: the live lane attaches BEFORE the session-snapshot seed with the reference-identity dedup of the crossing publishes, rows cap at the ring's own 200; the DND checkbox and the four per-source mute checkboxes mirror the reactive state read-only and their toggles write through the settings service — a failed persist stays visible on the footer's error line, the flagged state keeps its mirrored value, never a substituted one; Clear-history drops the ring and the rows together), the overlay surface (a top-right `ItemsControl` sibling above the workspace and below the palette overlay, chrome-styled cards per the T-066 canon, the menu entry under Trading) and the resx (`Notif.*` + `Menu.Trading.Notifications`, en and ru with parity); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.63` (see CHANGELOG.md). Hazards: the inherited mass from the dead sibling sessions needed a critical pass before landing — 12 test-file errors fixed (an `await using` over the non-disposable center, a nonexistent `OrderStatus.Placed`, blocking `GetAwaiter().GetResult()` in two sync mirror tests turned into async facts, a dead `Unused()` tail deleted) and a REAL pipeline bug caught by the DND goldens: the replay branch zipped each window with `dndFlips.Skip(1)` (the WRONG closing flip — window k spans [flip k-1, flip k) and is closed by flip k) AND subscribed `ToList()` only AFTER the window closed (hot windows replay nothing to a late subscriber, so every page was empty and the summary never emitted); the fix re-derived the canonical pairing `window → select(span => span.ToList()) → merge → zip(dndFlips)`, pages materialize while their window is open and page k pairs with flip k; the App test cluster (chart repair chain, status-bar re-render beats, the dispatcher pump, the positions in-flight row — the documented T-116 load-sensitive families) flaked 25 deep under one full parallel run and all 878 passed on the solo rerun (rerun-once discipline, never fixed in-flight); two `Task.FromResult` warns ride the FakeSettings interface double (the interface requires it). ## T-098 — Adapter health strip in the status bar

- **Branch:** feat/health-strip (base `develop` at `df22054`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-07).
