- **Acceptance:** state flips render within a beat; the strip adds no polling loops (observables only).
- **Tests:** state mapping from transport events, tooltip composition.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the strip (`81fcc4e`) — the `AdapterHealthService` fold (an App-side observable composition: the active-exchange facade's fixed adapter catalog folded with the central `ReconnectSupervisor`'s hot per-adapter state stream; the supervisor's `Snapshot` fetched at subscribe time inside `Observable.Defer`, so a late subscriber bootstraps with the supervisor's current states instead of waiting for the next transport report — the subscription keeps its own `Scan` fold state) mapping every supervised transport phase into the strip's vocabulary (an open session connected; the initial connect loop, the backoff wait and the watchdog force-cycle reconnecting; the disconnected phase down; a never-bound adapter — the demo's canned-data lane — unsupervised instead of a dishonest down; an out-of-vocabulary phase errors the fold per the standing no-default rule); the status bar VM renders one dot per adapter with the palette fills as `DynamicResource` style selectors picked through the Avalonia `Classes.<flag>` bindings (`CandleUp` connected / `Highlight` reconnecting / `Error` down / `TextSecondary` unsupervised — a theme switch or hot reload retints live), every write behind `ObserveOnUiThread` so a flip renders within one posted beat (pinned by pump facts: pre-drain reads are frozen, the drain re-renders), and the localized tooltip composed per state through `Status.Health.Tooltip` (name, state label, the last event clock in the tape's `HH:mm:ss` invariant precedent, the supervisor's consecutive-failure counter) or `Status.Health.TooltipUnsupervised`, the connected/disconnected labels reusing the connection chip's existing keys; shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.58` (see CHANGELOG.md). Scope decisions: the supervisor state surface proved SUFFICIENT for the strip (per-adapter `ConsecutiveFailures` + `UpdatedUtc` ride `AdapterConnectionState` — recorded gap is only its read-then-subscribe shape: a report published between the snapshot fetch and the stream subscription is seen with the adapter's next report, the window being the subscription's own microseconds and every state flip healing its row, the canonical next-report heal); the market-closed state is NOT rendered — the T-086 `TimeFrameCalendar` is bar-cadence arithmetic (next-open scheduling), not a deterministic per-adapter venue-session query, so nothing can produce the state yet (it joins when that work lands); the click → log-pane hook stays unwired for T-101's synergy; a language switch re-composes the rows from the stored snapshot (only the texts change, rows never flicker); the strip's no-polling acceptance is pinned as a reflection shape fact (no timer/thread/period fiber ever exists for the fold). Core untouched (0 diff), Infrastructure untouched; `Directory.Build.props` is the only supervisor-only file touched (the alpha bump). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected`; the first full gate run needed one flaky retry each for `ZettaTrade.App.Tests` (the fallback-notice timing family under parallel-suite load) and `ZettaTrade.Infrastructure.Tests` (the known T-116 WS E2E load flake) — both passed on retry, the second run clean save the Infrastructure flake.

## T-099 — First-run wizard (theme, adapter, symbol)

- **Branch:** feat/first-run-wizard (base `develop` at `fb95b81`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-08).
- **Origin:** a cold start lands on a bare workspace; onboarding is nothing.
- **What:** an opt-in three-step wizard — theme picker, adapter pick (testnet/demo defaults, keys stay in `ISecretStore` via the stdin path, never argv), first symbol pick; stored through the reactive settings service; skippable and re-runnable from settings; demo is the default lane so nothing real is touched without explicit choice.
- **Scope:** wizard VM/views, settings keys, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** skipping leaves a working demo configured app.
- **Tests:** step navigation golden, settings-write integration, the skip path.
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.66 (feat `9c61975` after `104820c` taught the gate's check-resx the `Wizard.` prefix — the wave map's number order ceded to the landing order, the alpha.66 heading proven at landing). The wizard stays view-contract edge work: `FirstRunWizardState` owns the persisted announcement flag and marks it exactly once on ANY exit — finishing, skipping or a plain window close — while a settings re-run never touches it; `FirstRunWizardViewModel` holds the three drafts (seeded from the applied theme and the active adapter, an unresolvable seed stays null rather than substituting a fabricated default, surviving back-and-forth) and the finish validates ALL three picks BEFORE the first write — an invalid pick is a refusal with the pre-wizard state untouched and a failed finish keeps the wizard open with the drafts intact. The launch shows the wizard after the disclaimer only when the announcement flag is unset (a fresh install or an externally reset settings file); a failure to show is logged and non-fatal — the flag stays unset and the next launch retries instead of booting half-configured. Secrets are never collected: the adapter step's note says so in the wizard itself, keys stay on the per-machine `ISecretStore` path and never argv. Behavioral change hazard recorded: the active-exchange facade's registration now LEADS with the canned-data demo adapter so a fresh install works on a simulator, while an existing install restores its own persisted exchange regardless of order; skipping is a plain window close — no write at all, the demo lane stays standing and the wizard still marks its once-flag. The settings window gains the re-run entry (appearance tab): it closes the settings window and shows the wizard modally over the main window. Tests: 16 new (13 VM facts incl. the walk clamps and the failed-finish shape + 3 state goldens for the flag semantics) plus the `WindowChromeTests` row adaptation; gate green (the first run tripped the PositionsPanel load-timing family — the test passed in isolation, 266 ms — and the full re-run passed clean, no retries).

## T-100 — Settings import/export + factory reset

- **Branch:** feat/settings-port (base `develop` at `1b9fcf4`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-08).
- **Origin:** no portability of a tuned setup; settings live only in the local store.
- **What:** export the user-facing settings subset (themes are files already; secrets are NEVER exported) as a versioned JSON; import with schema validation and a dry-run diff preview; factory reset behind a typed confirmation; changes re-apply live through the reactive settings change stream (T-053 canon).
- **Scope:** a settings port service, settings-window tab wiring, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** round-trips byte-identical; no restart needed after import.
- **Tests:** roundtrip golden, invalid-schema rejection, the live reapply cases.
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.68 (feat `dcec186` after `bf9f330` taught the gate's check-resx the `Port.` prefix — the wave's last landing, so the number the worker's landing-order calculus inherited stood). `PortSettingsRegistry` declares the portable subset: registry-owned keys only, both levels sorted, a fixed root shape — two exports of one state are byte-identical and a restore round-trips byte-stable; the export takes NO secret-store dependency, so a credential structurally cannot reach the file. The import lane rejects a foreign file whole (the port's own marker, the exactly-shipped version, the key allowlist and a stored-shape kind per key — nothing coerced, nothing defaulted) and a passed draft becomes the dry-run diff preview before the apply unlocks. The apply rides one locked, single-write batch on `SettingsService` (`SnapshotAsync` + `SettingsMutation` records; the publish happens strictly after the save lock's release, so a re-entering subscriber never faces a held lock) and every real difference publishes exactly one reactive change (the T-053 canon) — every subscriber that already reacts re-applies live, no restart after import. The factory reset stamps every registry-owned key back to its shipped fresh-install default behind the typed human confirmation — a decline writes nothing; the secrets lane and foreign tuning keys always survive. Landing-review verdicts: the two out-of-scope touches are behavior-neutral access widenings (`ActiveExchangeService.ExchangeIdSettingKey`, `SqliteCandleCacheStore`'s shipped-default consts went private→public so the registry references the shipped constants instead of duplicating magic numbers); the settings window gains the port tab with the picker in code-behind (async-void event handlers fully try/caught, coexisting with the T-099 wizard re-run route in the same window). Localization: the `Port.*` keys ship in en and ru (the check-resx chore registered the prefix right after T-099's `Wizard.`). Tests: the port's App.Tests suites (the kit-hosted roundtrip golden, invalid-schema rejection, the typed-confirmation reset lane, the live re-apply lane, the service and VM port facts) plus 11 new `SettingsServiceBatchTests` facts in Infrastructure. Gate facts: two landing runs tripped the pre-existing PositionsPanel load-timing family (each time singleton-green, 266-278 ms) and the re-run passed on the in-gate flaky retry — recorded as the wave's standing hazard for the human's stabilization pass.

## T-101 — Log viewer window + global crash guard

- **Branch:** feat/log-viewer (base `develop` at `a2acdeb`, rebased onto `cb00400` at landing; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-08 supervisor-solo).
- **Origin:** Serilog writes files only (the stdout rule); users cannot inspect without an editor, and T-056 flagged silent death paths.
- **What:** an in-app viewer (live tail, level/source filter, export a bounded excerpt) with secrets-shaped strings redacted; a global unhandled exception handler that shows a crash dialog with the log path instead of dying silently.
- **Scope:** a log viewer VM/window (virtualized tail), a crash guard host hook, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** tens of thousands of lines tail smoothly; the redaction never leaks shaped patterns.
- **Tests:** virtualization perf, redaction golden, the unhandled-handler path.
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.72 (feat `2c05545` after `98bfd12` taught the gate's check-resx the `Log.` prefix; the worktree's alpha.68-era docs ceded to the landing order — the CHANGELOG/props union took the newest-first shape at the rebase). The `LogTailService` is the only production seam: every byte chunk of file growth rides the `LogRedactor` gate BEFORE parsing, so no consumer (ring, surface, export) can ever hold a raw credential shape, and the parser keeps Serilog's exact header grammar (offset-bearing moments, continuations inheriting their header's fields, a heading-less malformed line rendering with `LogLevel.None` and no invented defaults). The ingest pipeline is redact → parse → bounded channel (drop-oldest backpressure, the flood's memory stays fixed) → history ring of `MaxHistoryLines` 32 768, with evictions riding the `Slices` lane openly; the seed window reads only the newest 1 MB tail of the newest `zettatrade-*.log` file (a partial head line never claims a record). The viewer (`LogViewerViewModel`, transient per open) attaches the live slice lane first, seeds from the ring's `HistorySnapshotAsync` behind it through a value-identity dedupe (a `LogLine` record's equality collapses identical content strings on purpose — seeds stay per-line-distinct), and the mirror replays into a `LogViewerRowList` applying ONE multi-element collection event per drained slice; a disposed lane writes nothing and the ring's evictions mirror onto the surface's head exactly as handed out. Level/source filters rebuild the surface synchronously out of the mirror — never a disk read — and the bounded excerpt export writes the matching slice through the file picker (a cancelled pick exports nothing; a failed write surfaces on the error line and never throws away the window). The `CrashGuard` is a last-inform hook pair (`AppDomain.UnhandledException` + `Dispatcher.UIThread.UnhandledException`): log FIRST, compose once (type name, a 300-char newline-free message run, the literal log path both raw and inside the localized sentence), present the dialog once (the first report wins), and RETURN — it never marks anything handled, never exits, touches neither settings nor secrets, and can therefore never fail a trading write. Everything stays file-sunk: nothing new writes to stdout. Tests: 42 new facts (12 redactor matrix, 8 tail facts, 4 excerpt-writer, 8 crash-guard/composer, 9 viewer VM facts inside the `AvaloniaDispatcherSerial` collection + the menu routing fact on `ShowLogViewerRequested`); the targeted slice ran 65/65 green and the six pump classes' serial membership 141/141 at landing. Gate history: the first run tripped the HTML-entities rule (`&amp;` in the redactor's XML docs — rewritten in words), the second the Avalonia `TextBox.Watermark` obsoletion (`PlaceholderText`), the third tripped the known full-suite tally flake and passed on the gate's own flaky retry, and the post-rebase run passed clean — all fixes amended into the single feat commit, GATE PASSED with `-AllowProtected`. Recorded (not fixed in flight): the full-suite tally flake reproduced during this task — with the identical binary the suite produced 37 fails, then an all-green 1054/1054, then 38 fails within minutes; the serial-collection slice (six pump classes, `AvaloniaDispatcherPumpTests`, chart/status/palette/viewer VMs) is green in isolation, and the first casualty of a failing tally is the pump's own `DrainAsync_ExecutesPostedJobsExhaustively` (run first by class order, before any of this task's tests post anything) — the cross-collection dispatcher-post interference family, a standing candidate for a follow-up board row.

## T-102 — Hotkey manager + cheat sheet overlay

- **Branch:** feat/hotkey-manager, worktree zt-hotkey-manager, based on develop@0b3a5b7 (claimed 2026-11-09; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut; taken autonomously by the supervisor session right after the T-108 landing, per the user's standing "take the next tasks" directive).
- **Origin:** hotkeys are wired (T-002) but undiscoverable and unchangeable.
- **What:** a settings tab mapping actions to keys with conflict detection, persisted user overrides, and a "?" overlay listing the live map; the binding registrar stays the single seam so advertised vs real never diverges.
- **Scope:** hotkey service extension, settings tab, overlay view, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** overrides apply without restart; conflicts diagnose at edit time.
- **Tests:** conflict golden, rebind live-reload, the overlay mirror.
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.85 (feat `199e2a0` after the board claim `1f1fac8`), developed supervisor-solo over a wall-killed session whose six service/ViewModel drafts were reviewed and adopted, not reblanched; the takeover fixed two real draft gaps (`HotkeyConflicts.Normalize` let the parser's `FormatException` escape — the catch makes the null verdict real — and `HotkeyService.BuildRoutes` now also excludes `KeyModifiers.None` routes, so a hand-edited settings file cannot bind a bare key that would fire while typing; the map keeps such an entry reusable for the editor's diagnosis, the routes keep it OUT). The single seam is the registry (four actions: open settings, new order, toggle assistant, symbol palette; stable ids = the overrides map's keys, resx name keys, shipped canonical gestures); the window's static `Window.KeyBindings` retired for a global code-behind route (`OnGlobalKeyDown` on the window, Bubble + `handledEventsToo: true`, attached on `OnOpened`, removed on `Closed`) that translates every press through `TryMatch` into `MainViewModel.RouteHotkey` — an unknown action id faults through the log and dispatches nothing (never a substitute); while the palette is open only its own toggle routes (its navigation stays the overlay's), and Esc closes the cheat sheet first; the T-095 palette source pins were updated to pin the REMOVED static-binding form (the registry defaults the pins used to enforce live with the service facts). The settings window carries the editor through a `Hotkeys` styled property (the settings ViewModel stays untouched by the seam; the mandatory nested binding needed `AncestorType={x:Type views:SettingsWindow}` — the plain `AncestorType=Window` failed to compile with AVLN2000); one editable row per action with edit-time verdicts (unparseable, modifier-less, conflicting — refused, never substituted, all localized), per-row live commit (`WhenAnyValue(X => X.GestureText).Skip(1)` — the seed emission never persists), a no-op write guard (a commit whose text equals the persisted state writes nothing — this both stops a reverted default-row edit from manufacturing an override and settles the write-through echo and the reset round trip so no write loop spins), per-row reset persisting a cleared entry, the read-only cheat-sheet overlay mirroring the same map from the Help menu (a palette sibling; scrim press and Esc close it), and a language switch re-localizing names, verdicts and the sheet. Persistence: the overrides document is one settings key in its own `Hotkeys` section (`UiSettingsKeys`), parsed fail-safe (malformed/non-object/non-string/unknown-action/unparseable entries all drop — the remainder survives; the relaxed-JSON encoder keeps `+` human-legible per the stored-shape contract), persisted cache-leads by `HotkeyService.SetOverrideAsync`, re-applied live through the settings changes stream — binding, menu gestures and sheet rebinding without a restart. The composition registers two typed singletons (`AddHotkeyServices`) with no startup-walk leg, pinned by registration facts per the T-104 smoke lesson. Landings lessons of record: the service's construction-time seed read fires before any subscriber exists, so the ViewModel's constructor carries its own synchronous `ApplyMap(service.Map)` hand seed (without it the cheat sheet stays empty until the first override change — the takeover test caught it); a substitute's `Arg<type>()` arg-lookup with three same-typed parameters matches the first, the captures ride `ArgAt`. The kit shapes 4 conflict/service/VM/registration fact files plus the hotspot-riders: 11 menu facts (the shared manager rides every `MenuViewModel` construction — the ctor churn is honest, the injected instance and the sheet toggle carry their own fact), 2 palette source-pins updated, resx gains the `Hotkeys.*` prefix (12 keys, en+ru) and the lint's prefix list. Full suites green (App.Tests 1319, UI.Tests 289); gate passed in one run with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the only protected file touched).

## T-103 — Detachable panels: multi-window workspaces

- **Branch:** feat/multi-window, worktree zt-multi-window, based on develop@243c50a (claimed 2026-11-10 per standing directive 6; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `f266c06`, the feat `ea57f40` (the alpha bump and the changelog ride the feat); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.90`, developed supervisor-solo. The one-control-instance discipline: a panel's UserControl never duplicates — the detach physically MOVES it cross-window (a control cannot hold two visual parents; the inline Border host swaps to the detached note FIRST, then the window takes the panel; the attach legs release the window's content BEFORE the inline slot takes it back) and the panel's DataContext rides LocalValues the main window pushes on `DataContextChanged` (a markup binding would re-resolve against the detached window's inherited null and unbind the moved control) — the workspace symbol context broadcasts across all panels by construction, since the moved control keeps its main-window ViewModel. The coordinator `DetachedPanelCoordinator` is the runtime authority: the slots register once at the main window's open (panel control, inline host, focus affordance), the per-panel window shells carry the T-066 chrome, the persist writes serialize through one Concat pipeline, and the restore rides `Observable.FromAsync` + `Subscribe` (no bare fire-and-forget); the layout ViewModel mirrors the detached state (`PanelDetached`/`PanelAttached` via `ObserveOnUiThread`) and owns the View menu's `ToggleDetachCommand`; a hidden assistant that still lives detached attaches back BEFORE its column collapses. Geometry: the placement stores FRACTIONS of the display's working area (`PanelDisplaySample`, `PanelWindowPlacement` with `ClampFraction`/`ClampExtent`), the restore composes the exact display sample first, remaps by the stored absolute point when the monitor moved, centers on nothing; the record `UI.DetachedPanels` (default `"[]"`) parses fail-safe — blank, malformed, wrong-root, unknown-panel and unusable-display documents restore nothing, never throw, and the fresher duplicate wins per panel. The geometry facts caught a real implementation bug: `Snapshot`'s int/int division floored every position fraction to zero (192/1920 = 0) and would have pinned every window to the top-left corner — the operands now ride double math. The HUD's chart frame-tap freezes while the chart is detached (the HUD overlay is a main-window citizen); the panel's `HudFrameTapSource` styled gate rides a typed `$parent[panels:ChartPanelView]` compiled binding. App shutdown: `ShutdownMode.OnMainWindowClose` + the `ShutdownRequested` arm mark the coordinator, so closing panel windows return their content but never rewrite the stored record — the detached layout stands exactly as the user left it. Avalonia 12 API notes for the next multi-window task: Border is not a ContentControl (`Child`, not `Content`), `Dictionary.Remove(key, out value)` (no `TryRemove`), `window.Screens.All`, and compiled bindings resolve `$parent[type].Property` across windows. The launch smoke caught one real wiring bug the tests could not: the view lanes must resolve `IDetachedPanelCoordinator` (the interface the DI registers), never the concrete type — `GetRequiredService<DetachedPanelCoordinator>()` throws at startup and the restore leg dies before registering the slots.
- **Origin:** splitters (T-038) resized one window; multi-monitor setups deserve owned windows.
- **What:** chart/book/assistant detach into owned windows (chrome templates compose per the T-066 canon), geometry per display persisted, the workspace symbol context broadcasts across all panels, app shutdown persists both layouts.
- **Scope:** layout service + window shells, symbol context broadcast, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** detach/attach is lossless; restore across DPI shifts stays sane.
- **Tests:** detach/attach roundtrip golden, context broadcast, geometry restore.

## T-104 — Single instance hand-off + CLI symbol open

- **Branch:** feat/single-instance, worktree zt-single-instance, based on develop@80fe7af (claimed 2026-11-09; queued 2026-11-06 (supervisor idea at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut)).
- **Origin:** a second launch spawns a second app; integrations (shortcuts, scripts) want arg-driven opens.
- **What:** second launches forward arguments (`--symbol`, `--exchange`, optional protocol strings) to the running instance and exit; the running instance applies the workspace symbol. Args are validated allowlist-style — nothing can inject orders; nothing writes to stdout outside the MCP protocol rule.
- **Scope:** app bootstrapping, a handoff service, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** a second start on any platform redirects cleanly and exits promptly.
- **Tests:** handoff golden, malformed-arg rejection, exit-parity cases.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `3835e97` (the board claim) and `f2faff9` (the feat), shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.80` (the chain rebased onto 47b6543 after the T-138 stabilization landed first, per the user's stabilization-first choice; landing order 3835e97 -> f2faff9 -> 9d7189a); developed supervisor-solo (the dispatch wall killed every dispatched worker; the wave-3/4 precedent). The start decision layer: an OS-enforced exclusive open on one well-known lock file decides ownership — crash-safe and portable by the framework's own `FileShare.None` semantics (a killed instance never wedges the next launch; the lock file is never deleted — the claim lives in the exclusive open, not in the file's existence), no named-mutex per-OS quirks. A second start never touches Avalonia: its vector walks the allowlist grammar first (the two flags in split and attached-at-the-equals forms, protocol strings on the open route, an empty vector a valid prompt redirect) — every position outside the allowlist, every order-shaped token included, is a hard rejection with its own reason, never a defaulted substitute; a well-shaped vector forwards through the bounded dispatch pipe (framed 64 KB JSON on one wire naming policy, one connected peer at a time, a bounded framing read, the first server instance created synchronously in `Start` so a second start's first connect never races the pump's warm-up) and exits promptly with the mapped exit-parity code (0 forwarded, 1 rejected, 2 the hand-off never served within the bounded window). The receiver re-validates the whole vector semantically: the exchange id resolves against the live adapter catalog and the symbol text parses through the real symbol parsers (the exchange's format when an exchange rides along, the normalized format otherwise) — a malformed value is a refusal receipt, never a substituted symbol; the application routes through the palette precedent (a same-exchange selection writes the chart's workspace symbol, a cross-exchange one writes the workspace symbol preference first and only then switches the active exchange, so the pending bootstrap preselects the pair); all applies serialize behind one gate and UI writes marshal through the Avalonia dispatcher. The dispatch receipts live in a dedicated rolling file log (`zettatrade-handoff-*.log`); nothing in the lane writes to stdout; the task ships ZERO user-facing strings, so no resx keys exist by design (en+ru N/A, stated plainly). Draft fixes adopted at review (the drafts never shipped, so the fixes ride the feat): the attached-form slice drops the `=` separator (the draft ate it into the value — three parser facts exposed it), the forwarder serializes with the receiver's own `HandoffJson.Options` (the PascalCase/camelCase asymmetry decoded every forward to nothing — every handoff would have died `HandoffUnavailable`), the no-selection forward fact pins the short-circuit BEFORE the apply route (the draft awaited an apply call the contract never makes), and the dead `DuplicateFlag` rejection reason left the enum (repeats refuse as `DuplicateSelectionSource`). Tests: 33 Infrastructure facts (the grammar matrix, the claim/gate exit-parity, the golden forward, the dead-pipe window) + 3 App registration facts (the T-122 smoke lesson institutionalized: the two-descriptor shape, the walk screen pinned both ways, the stay-alone fact). THE LANDING VERDICTS: the authorized gate re-run PASSED on its first verdict-bearing invocation (a preceding invocation died on the [PROTECTED] lint - the omitted -AllowProtected flag for the supervisor-only Directory.Build.props bump - with build/tests skipped, so no content verdict was spent); the launch smoke on the gate-built exe (ProductVersion 0.1.0-alpha.80+9d7189a…): the dispatch log records "Single-instance claim acquired; building the application", the Serilog window holds 0 ERR/FTL and exactly the 7 ambient WRN (6 demo-watchlist ticker warnings + 1 Binance quote-asset warning), the app closes gracefully. The smoke canonized two log-slice traps: a second same-day process rolls the day file to `zettatrade-YYYYMMDD_001.log` (the plain day name stays stale), and exception-body continuation lines carry no timestamp - a lexical slice without the timestamp-line filter pulls stale bodies into the window.

## T-105 — Reconnect policy hardening: backoff, jitter, watchdog

- **Branch:** feat/reconnect-policy (base `develop` at `8e6d430`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-07).
- **Origin:** reconnect fixes are per-adapter (T-048, T-052); the policy itself is implicit and untestable.
- **What:** an explicit per-adapter policy object (exponential + jitter bounds + max delay) and a health watchdog that force-cycles a silent socket; a central supervisor exposing state (later to T-098); adapter implementations consume the policy — zero behavior drift on the happy path.
- **Scope:** a policy/supervisor service in Infrastructure, adapter wiring, tests.
- **Acceptance:** a half-open socket heals inside the watchdog window; churn with 2+ symbols stays fixed (T-048 regression pinned).
- **Tests:** watchdog golden, jitter bounds, churn regression.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the explicit reconnect stack (`bf506a0`) — the `ReconnectPolicy` value object (exponential backoff, bounded jitter with the re-clamped cap, a per-session handshake-attempt budget BUG-029 keeps; guarded constructors fail loudly), the central `ReconnectSupervisor` (per-adapter snapshots via `For(id, policy)`, a hot `StateStream` for the T-098 health strip, ONE sequential pump over an unbounded `Channel`, a throwing subscriber never kills the pump) and the per-session `SocketSilenceWatchdog` (the arming rule: only a session that exchanged a frame may be condemned, outbound frames only ever arm — a half-open socket accepts sends into its TCP buffer; inbound frames arm and refresh; `Interlocked`/`Volatile` liveness, no lock); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.55` (see CHANGELOG.md). Scope decisions: the supervisor only SURFACES what the T-048/T-052 fixes already do and never drives the transports (the zero-drift acceptance lives in the report plumbing — direct `WebSocketClient` constructions without a supervisor report nothing); the watchdog's violation action aborts the dying socket so the parked receive throws into the usual policy-computed reconnect path (the heal of a half-open socket); the shipped silence window is 60 s with a probe clamped into a 100 ms–2 s reaction band. Resx untouched (no user-facing surface yet — the T-098 health strip renders later). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file); the pre-existing T-048 resubscribe family stays sensitive to parallel-suite load (the known T-116 flake; solo runs pass).

## T-106 — Exchange rate limiter with per-endpoint budgets

- **Branch:** feat/rate-limiter (base `develop` at `df22054`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-07).
- **Origin:** REST/token budgets are unmanaged today; bursts risk 429s and bans.
- **What:** per-endpoint token buckets with weights, a priority queue (user actions over background fills from T-086), 429/Retry-After aware; limits live adapter-side so the UI never busy-waits; demo/testnet exempt.
- **Scope:** an Infrastructure limiter + adapter hooks, `Settings.*` keys, tests.
- **Acceptance:** background backfill never starves a user submit; Retry-After honored.
- **Tests:** drain/refill timing golden, Retry-After compliance, preemption.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the budget limiter (`1f7fc95`) — the `RestRateLimiter` carrying one token bucket per served endpoint family (`RestEndpointBudget` manifests: budget, refill interval, per-request weights; Binance splits its published 6,000 weight-per-minute per-IP allowance into klines 3,600 + pricing 2,400 with the documented tier weights 2/4/6 by kline slice ≤100/≤500/≤1,000 — an out-of-range slice overestimates to the top tier, the safe metering direction; Bybit carries 10 requests per second per family at one token a call), the two lanes (`RestLane.User` drains the bucket's main pool, `RestLane.Background` borrows only a capped slack — `max(1, ceil(share×budget))` tokens at the background's own flow — so a backfill burst can never starve or delay a user submit), the lane routing (`RestLaneResolver`: a range within the 2-minute `TailRequestGrace` → user, past its end → background, ambiguous → user), the queue discipline (the user rank is served ahead of queued background work, FIFO inside a lane, deliberately not reserve-based — a light later request may slip behind a heavy earlier one's spilled remainder; wakes estimate the fit point with a 2 ms overshoot and every wake re-fits, so truncation can only wake early, never spin), the 429 path (the adapters call `SendRateLimitedGetAsync` — acquire → send → report; the venue's `Retry-After` parks BOTH pools for the endpoint and the expiry refills them to full; missing or non-positive guidance parks for the 2 s `DefaultBlockSpan`; a park or deficit stretching past the 10-minute `MaxParkedWait` fails the waiter with the bounded `RestRateLimitWaitExpiredException`; cancelled waiters leave no spent debt) and the settings seam (the `Limiter.*` keys hydrate once before the first acquire — an absent key leaves the manifest standing, only a PERSISTED `BackgroundShare` reshapes it — and are then followed reactively; `Enabled: false` flips the pass to the pass-through `UnthrottledRestRateLimiter.Instance`, which is also the demo and paper adapters' standing posture by design — synthetic book, no venue or IP budget). Shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.59` on the branch (the develop head had already moved past the `df22054` base to its own alpha.58 cut — the landing note for the supervisor: collisions in `Directory.Build.props`/CHANGELOG resolve at landing, alpha.59 stays top of the branch's CHANGELOG). Resx untouched (no user-facing surface — the limiter is silent plumbing). Facts caught three real product bugs before landing: the `Math.Max(1, …)` seconds floor in the share re-application that silently turned sub-second refill intervals into a tenth of their flow (the original suite hang), a park-expiry double spend (an in-pass scan admitted and spent for the caller, then the caller's own fit spent again — the admission-flag re-check after the advance closes it) and hydration clobbering the manifest share with the 0.35 default on every host carrying a settings service. A testing discipline worth keeping for every future manual-clock fact: injected `TimeProvider` wakes ride `Task.Delay(TimeSpan, TimeProvider, ct)`, which arms its timer at a WHOLE truncated millisecond — so pin boundaries strictly below and above both the fit point and the whole-ms wake due, never at the raw estimate, and cap every pending await (`WaitAsync`) so a regression reads as a timeout, not a hung run. Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).

## T-107 — Adapter conformance test kit (shared battery)

- **Branch:** feat/adapter-kit (base `develop` at `ce580b6`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-07).
- **Origin:** every adapter carries bespoke tests; the shared contract deserves a common gauntlet (the Bybit/demo/Binance variance shows it).
- **What:** an abstract test battery parameterized over canned offline captures: subscribe/reconnect restore, depth resolution (T-072's resolver), the rejected-not-default error contract, guard invariants; new adapters run the kit as part of gate.
- **Scope:** the kit as a test project + gate integration, capture-pack fixtures.
- **Acceptance:** the demo and Bybit adapters pass the kit outright; the kit documents itself as the adapter recipe.
- **Tests:** the kit itself (this is a test deliverable) plus its gate wiring.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the shared battery kit (`bcb0c8b`) — the self-contained `tests/ZettaTrade.AdapterKit.Tests/` project (nothing in `src/` depends on it, no other test project shares a file with it; the adapter recipe lives in its own README.md). The battery: an abstract `AdapterConformanceBattery` of eight legs, a run being leg × pack — identity (the pack's Id/Name/capabilities/construction-time connectedness exactly), the connect flip (a transport-backed rig sees exactly one session), the pack-declared guards (an unknown symbol rejected on every subscribe face and GetBars with the symbol parameter; a non-positive bar limit rejected with the limit parameter; the blank-endpoints constructor probe through the subclass's `RunCtorGuardProbeAsync` override, whose default throws so a missing probe is a wiring error, never a vacuously green leg), the captured universe (the served symbol list equals the pack's in the serve order), the depth-surface profile (an explicit-set adapter carries `ServedBookDepths`; a clamped one carries no set — the pack is honest about that, too), the unsupported-depth contract (BUG-032 as the pack declares it: explicit-set — the rejection rides EACH subscription as an `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` naming the served set and the rejected request, and no stream ever starts; clamped — the deterministic first-snapshot clamp shape), the T-072 resolver arbitration (`ExchangeAdapterBase.ResolveBookDepth`: the resolved depth is what lands on the wire's book topic, the raw unresolvable request never rides it, and a no-declaration profile passes every request through untouched) and the restore story (always-on: ticker/book/trade cadence events land on both sides of one adapter-owned connect cycle; transcript: two recorded sessions replayed through a real reconnect — per-session ticker prices, per-session rebuilt books, a late subscriber riding the shared subject, and the session count pinned, no churn). The capture pack (`Captures/<adapter>/Pack.json` plus the files it names): adapterId/adapterName, capabilities as pipe-separated enum names, connectsAtConstruction, symbols + strangerSymbol, clientSideGuards (unknown-symbol/bars-limit/blank-endpoints only — unknown names rejected at load), depthProfile `explicit-set` (servedBookDepths + the rejectedDepthRequest the rejection leg attaches) or `clamped` (clampedServedDepth, no served set), depthProbeRequests for the resolver leg, bookTopicPrefix + subscribeMarker for the transcript legs, reconnectMode `transcript` (transcriptSessions: a frame-script file plus that session's ticker price, in session order) or `always-on` (with none), cannedRest (REST path fragment → captured response file); loading is failing-fast — a malformed pack is a kit configuration error, never a silently weakened battery — and the first captured symbol is the battery's probe everywhere. The harness: the kit's own in-process Kestrel loopback `KitLoopbackServer` (127.0.0.1 only, bind-retry on contention, sessions accepted by 1-based ordinal so a reconnecting adapter addresses the new session by its number, every received frame captured in arrival order, log providers stripped so nothing writes to stdout) and the `KitCannedRestHandler` in-process disguise (canned bodies by URL fragment, unmapped paths answer 404 so a mis-wired or unplanned REST call fails loudly inside the kit, never silently hits the network). The reference shapes: the demo battery rides no rigs (the inline canned adapter), the Bybit battery rides both (the two recorded sessions replayed through the rig, the catalog through the disguise, the REST base deliberately unresolvable — captures and canned responses only, never real keys, testnet/mocks per the standing rule). Adapters proven to pass outright with zero product-side change: the demo adapter (always-on, clamped, unknown-symbol + bars-limit) and the Bybit adapter (transcript reconnect riding the shipped T-105 policy, explicit-set {1, 50}, blank-endpoints) — 8 legs × 2 packs = 16 conformance verdicts, all green. Gate integration: NO gate.ps1 edit — the gate's solution-wide build/test discovers the kit through its ZettaTrade.slnx entry (the one protected file touched, the granted wiring), and onboarding a new adapter is its capture pack plus one sealed `Packs/<Adapter>AdapterBattery.cs` subclass (`OpenSessionAsync` builds the adapter with its rigs). Hazards: the rig's drop must be `CloseOutputAsync` (the transport exits its receive loop on the received Close frame and lets the reconnect machinery answer; a full `CloseAsync` waits for a reply that never comes and the leg dies on the rig's own socket), the restore budget is the T-105 reconnect policy (5 s initial delay + ≤25% jitter) times three headroom, and the kit pins session counts and data identity, never timing facts (those stay with the T-020 E2E suite). Tests-only deliverable: no VersionSuffix bump, no CHANGELOG section. Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the ZettaTrade.slnx test-project wiring is the only protected file; the solution build/test/format is green, no suite needed a retry).

## T-108 — Performance HUD overlay (fps, memory, WS latency)

- **Branch:** feat/perf-hud, worktree zt-perf-hud, based on develop@32d9457 (claimed 2026-11-09; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut; taken autonomously by the supervisor session right after the T-094 landing, per the user's standing "take the next tasks" directive); rebased onto develop@8482ee4 mid-flight when the parallel T-140 landing moved the base and consumed alpha.83 — the HUD ships as alpha.84.
- **Origin:** the AGENTS targets (60 fps book, <1 ms WS, <500 MB) have no visible instrument; nothing guards them day-to-day.
- **What:** a dev-mode overlay showing chart/book fps, working memory, WS latency histogram and channel backpressure bird gauges; off by default, settings-driven, zero allocation when off (no subscriptions, no timers).
- **Scope:** a HUD service + overlay, `Diagnostics.*` settings keys, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** the HUD's own cost is in the noise when running; off-state leaves zero traces.
- **Tests:** the gauge math golden, the off-state zero-cost assertion.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the zero-off-cost shape — the off state is one property read in the chart's render pass (the `FrameTapEnabled` styled property arms a tap counter the render only touches when shown), no subscriptions, no timers, no allocations; showing the overlay arms three legs at once (the sampler's composite tick, the book-stream tap riding `OrderBookViewModel.Updates`, and the re-formatting snapshots leg), hiding it tears all three down. The measurements: the chart fps from an `Interlocked` reset-and-read frame counter (`TakeFrameTapCount`) sampled over the tick window; the WS latency as the snapshot age at the consumer edge (`UtcNow - book.Timestamp`, negative clock-skew ages clamp into the fastest bucket) through a five-bounded-bucket-plus-overflow histogram (1/5/20/100/500 ms); the backpressure gauge rides `LogTailService`'s bounded channel (the new public `BackpressureBacklog` against the public 8192 capacity); the memory figure is the process working set. The gauge math is pure (`HudMath`: fps, histogram bucketing, gauge bars, memory formatting) and golden-tested; the service lifecycle (the off-state zero-cost, start/stop, the tick drain), the persistence walk (default off, restore-on, the toggle persists immediately) and the localized readouts carry their own facts. Test discipline learned: a `ReactiveCommand`'s execute does not run synchronously under the test pump — the flip only lands when the `Execute` call is awaited (its `IObservable<RxVoid>` awaiter comes from `System.Reactive.Linq`), and the snapshot fact arms the overlay by direct assignment, keeping the command path its own fact. The resx keys ride the `Diagnostics.` module prefix — added to check-resx.ps1's known-prefix list (the script's own documented extension mechanism), keeping the persistence section and the string keys coherent. Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the Directory.Build.props version bump is the only protected file; run 1 failed on the resx module-prefix rule and one doc-comment HTML entity — both fixed, run 2 green).

Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the bump); run 1 tripped only on usings order (`Avalonia.Automation.Peers` sorts before `Avalonia.Controls`), run 2 passed. ## T-109 — Soak harness and budget gates (cold start < 3 s, memory < 500 MB)

- **Branch:** feat/soak, worktree zt-soak, based on develop@f77941e (claimed 2026-11-10 per standing directive 6; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut).
- **Origin:** the perf targets are asserted ad-hoc; multi-hour stability is unmeasured.

- **Scope:** the soak harness, CI integration bits, a docs page on interpreting reports.
- **Acceptance:** a 4-hour soak on the fixtures finishes within memory budget; budget gates run in gate on demand.
- **Tests:** the harness itself plus the budget assertions' goldens.
- **Status:** done. Canon: this branch carries 280e5a4 — the soak machinery: the silent Soak tool (prints nothing; exit 0 pass / 2 input error / 3 breach), 17 goldens pinning the Stats/slope/verdict math and the driver-tool JSON contracts, the driver (process hygiene, cold start spawn→MainWindowHandle with a 60 s cap, fixed-cadence samples), the budget files (the landing lane enforces MemoryMeanMb 400 / MemoryPeakMb 500 / ColdStartMs 3000; the CI lane keeps the cold start null = advisory, memory enforced), the gate `-Budgets` opt-in lane, the weekly CI soak workflow and `docs/SOAK.md` — plus this status-flip commit (the board row + the archive splice). No version bump and no CHANGELOG section: the app itself is untouched (scripts + CI + docs + a repo-internal console tool), per the T-117 CI/docs-only and T-116 tests-only precedents. The acceptance proof — the 4-hour run, 480 samples over 14 400 s: working set mean 235.9 / max 243.5 / P95 242.3 / P99 242.4 MB; private mean 106.1 / max 116.1 / P95 112.7 / P99 114.2 MB; leak slope 0.00 MB/h on both series; memory verdicts OK / OK; process hygiene clean (zero ZettaTrade processes before and after). The honest breach: cold start 3 999.3 ms vs the 3 000 ms budget — the Debug lane's spawn-to-window wall (~1.6 s to the runtime before the first Serilog line, ~1.7 s to the service assembly, the rest to the window build); the landing lane enforces and reports this breach until a dedicated startup-optimization task lands (follow-up candidate noted at this close); the CI lane deliberately keeps the cold start advisory (runner noise). A `-Budgets` gate demo must show exactly this breach and nothing else — expected and disclosed, not a fix-blocker for this task. Recorded lane limits: GC per-generation sampling degrades gracefully (no `.NET CLR Memory` counter instances exist for .NET 10 on this lane — `Sampled: false`, never invented); the soak observes the process from the outside (memory + cold start), fps stays the T-108 HUD's manual read; the lane is Windows-only, CI rides windows-latest. PS 5.1 lessons for the canon: a comma inside a method call splits call arguments (assign the formatted string to a variable first), and `dotnet exec` takes the tool's args directly (no `--` separator).

## T-111 — Strategy contracts MVP: candle-close signals (no auto-trading)

- **Branch:** feat/strategies (base `develop` at `f8d68ca`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-08).
- **Origin:** plugin-first declares strategies as separate assemblies (AGENTS); nothing exists — no host, no contracts, no `ZettaTrade.Strategies` project yet.
- **What:** a minimal signal contract (evaluate on candle close, emit neutral/long/flat events per symbol+interval), the host+loader in Strategies reusing the indicator scanner's canon (T-054's fix included), a demo strategy; the output is a signals channel only — no order placement, no risk bypass; the human decides.
- **Scope:** the new Strategies project, contracts + host, the demo plugin, tests.
- **Acceptance:** a broken/unloadable plugin degrades the scan, never the app (T-054 regression pinned).
- **Tests:** strategy-kit conformance, the demo golden on seeded candles, scan tolerance.
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.67 (feat `4b0b9a5`; the wave map's alpha.68 ceded to the landing order — the supervisor's renumber chore `9317320` rides the branch as its own commit). The new `ZettaTrade.Strategies` leaf module (Core is its only reference) owns the seam: `IStrategy` answers one neutral/long/flat stance on a read-only closed-candle window per (symbol, interval) — pure, cheap, deterministic; `StrategyContext` carries the series identity so an implementation never mistake it, `SeriesWindow` retains the per-series window; `StrategyHost` runs one sequential candle-close evaluation loop from an owned input channel and stamps one `StrategySignal` per (strategy, series) per accepted candle into an owned inert output `Channel<T>` — read-only events for the human and the UI tasks ahead: NOTHING rides a signal to an order, to the risk lane or to any trading adapter surface (the landing review verified the module's reference graph and the absence of the signal-to-order path), and a strategy that throws is quarantined for the session — logged loudly, the rest of the scan keeps working (the T-054 tolerance canon at runtime). `PluginStrategyCatalog` scans the strategies plugins directory once per session reusing the indicator scanner's tolerance shapes wholesale: a malformed, unloadable (missing dependency) or empty-id assembly is skipped with a loud warning while the healthy neighbors keep registering — the T-054 regression pinned by the loader matrix; the documented ctor convention is a public parameterless constructor, the id read off the built instance, whitespace and duplicate ids refused. The wiring registers through `StrategiesDependencyInjection` (`AddStrategiesServices`). ## T-112 — Signal markers on the chart + signal rail

- **Branch:** feat/signal-markers (base `develop` at `a2acdeb`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, blocker T-111, developed 2026-11-08).
- **Origin:** signals without a surface are invisible; markers + a rail personalize the workspace read.
- **What:** markers at candle closes (▲/▼ with tooltips) via the chart overlay layer, plus an N-item signal rail panel streaming live; a click prefills the ticket (T-025's prefill-not-submit lesson), never sends.
- **Scope:** Charting overlay + the rail panel, the T-111 contracts (consumed, unchanged), resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** marker placement matches the signal's candle exactly; bursts do not flood the rail (coalesced).
- **Tests:** placement golden, the rail stream, prefill routing.
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.69 (feat `8e39fcb`). The surfacing leg of the T-111 seam: the chart's compose pass (`ComposeSignalMarkers`, a pure internal static pinned by tests without a VM instance) maps the host's per-series stance registry onto the displayed bars by open time — one marker per matching bar carrying THE bar's own candle, Neutral an honest non-reading that never markers; `ChartSignalLayout.Resolve` (the new pure layout unit under `ZettaTrade.UI/Charting`, the `ChartBarLayout` precedent) is a two-pointer merge anchoring every marker at the slot its own candle rides in the visible slice — placement exact by construction, a marker whose candle left the window resolves to nothing (an absence, never a neighbor's slot), two readings on one bar stack on the same slot; the draw consumes the hits through the same slot/price mapping the candles use. The signal rail panel under the chart (`SignalRailViewModel` + `SignalRailRow` + `ISignalRailPrefillSink`) streams live: a burst inside one coalesce window lands as ONE rows replacement (never a flood), the rows ring stays bounded newest-first, Neutral never enters, and a row press rides the MainWindow PREFILL route — the same transient dialog path the book level presses share — prefilling the ticket with the reading's facts (symbol, side, the reading candle's close) and never sending (a signal never rides an order path; the human decides). The hosting leg feeds the chart's delivered full series to the T-111 host through the composition-owned `SignalSeriesRelay` (the chart and the service never hold each other): candles enter the pump exactly once per series and only when provably closed (the in-flight candle is never judged), a re-covered delivery drops its whole re-visit, and the per-series stance registry prunes to the delivered window's floor. resx `Signal.*` en+ru (title, empty state, stance labels, row tooltip) with the `Signal.` module prefix registered in `check-resx`. Tests: 19 new (4 service facts on the demo host, 6 rail streaming/press facts, 3 compose goldens, 6 resolve goldens) plus the App.Tests VM fixture constructors extended for the new ChartViewModel signature. Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the branch touches `scripts/check-resx.ps1` + the bump); one flaky retry on an App.Tests known timing family (recorded, scope not widened). This board-flip commit closes the section.

## T-113 — Backtest harness over stored candles

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-06 (supervisor idea at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut). **Blocker:** T-111 + T-085.
- **Origin:** strategies need an offline replay to earn trust; live data is the wrong place to discover bad math.
- **What:** a deterministic simulator replaying cached candles through the strategy contract with the paper ledger's fills (T-051-corrected path), an equity summary report (feeds T-083 later), reproducible seeds; entirely offline (no adapters, no limiter).
- **Scope:** a backtest service + report + a run/progress VM, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** the same seed reproduces the same curve byte-for-byte; cancel mid-run is clean.
- **Tests:** determinism golden, ledger integration, the cancel path.
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.70 (feat `9caf433`; branch `feat/backtest`, base `develop` at `18a4fe1`, developed 2026-11-08 supervisor-solo). The replay consumes the real T-111 seam exactly as registered — a genuine `StrategyHost` evaluation loop over `ICandleCache`'s paged walk-back loader (the cache serves at most its page cap per call, the loader walks back by open time, the honest shorter window when the cache runs dry) — and nothing else: no adapter is ever constructed, no rate limiter, no order surface, entirely offline by construction. Determinism canon: the seeded slippage draw is the replay's only stochastic input (xoshiro256** via the explicit-seed `Random` ctor), zero slippage consumes no draw and is seed-independent by construction, fill ids are the run-local `bt-NNNNNN` sequence and every timestamp is the judging candle's time — the same request over the same cache produces a byte-identical canonical report (test golden). Paper-parity ledger (BUG-033/034 + T-051 order): the balance check runs before any mutation, a refusal records a visible `BacktestRefusal` and leaves the book untouched, weighted-average entries over the whole new size, a partial close never re-averages the remainder, a flip stamps its fresh position at its own fill, commissions stamp from the fee registry per the fill's taker role — a missing schedule leaves the commission null (the honest unknown); the paper parity nuance is preserved verbatim: the balance accounting counts only the trade's quote delta, the fee rides the record and the totals. The single-flight run gate refuses a concurrent second start with an explicit error (runs never interleave, events stay coherent); cancel is the stop — a cooperative token check per page and per signal yields the cancelled envelope with NO report and no artifacts, `OperationCanceledException` is the happy path. The events stream (Started → Progress(per-256-candle stride) → terminal) rides a `Subject` as usual; the UI marshals via `ObserveOnUiThread`. The Backtest window (modal, opened via the menu's Interaction route like the stats/balances windows): transient `BacktestRunViewModel` over the singleton harness, a fresh form per open, the attach-handle pattern owns the event + language subscriptions so a dead window never lingers; every input parses explicitly (refuse-not-default: symbol, bars, seed, slippage, fraction each carry their own localized refusal line); the composite task includes a run-local `CancellationTokenSource` and the status lines re-resolve live on language change. resx `Backtest.*` en+ru 26/26 keys, the `Backtest.` module prefix registered in `check-resx` (chore `66cdbf6` rides the branch like every wave-3 resx chore). Tests: 39 new — 29 in the new `ZettaTrade.Backtest.Tests` suite (determinism golden, zero-slip seed independence, unknown-strategy refusal, concurrent-start refusal, mid-load cancel envelope + event order, walk-back paging, empty-cache honest baseline; the ledger's average-price/BUG-034/refusal facts; the request's validation bounds) + 10 `BacktestRunViewModelTests` (request composition, stalled-run cancel, localized surfaces) with a hand-rolled fake `ICandleCache`. Scope note recorded honestly: recorded stance transitions other than long-entry/long-exit (e.g. a Short from a future strategy) land as the honest mark-only hold, never a synthesized fill — the long-side replay is the T-083 feed's determined scope; shorts stay a follow-up. Gate passed on run 2: run 1 hit the known App.Tests load-sensitive family under parallel-suite load (the identical 34-fail tally that re-ran 1020/1020 green manually minutes earlier) and flagged format drift in the two new files (import order, a trailing blank line) plus the VM's synchronous cancel wrapped in `Task.Run`-style `Task.FromResult`; the fix converted CancelCommand to a synchronous `ReactiveCommand.Create` and a scoped `dotnet format --include` settled the style — run 2 green with only interface-stub `Task.FromResult` notes left. Known-flake record: `ZettaTrade.App.Tests` (the parallel-load timing family), never widened scope to fix in-flight.

## T-114 — AI market-context injection into the chat

- **Branch:** feat/ai-context (base `develop` at `f8d68ca`; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut, developed 2026-11-08).
- **Origin:** the assistant is wired (T-032) but blind to the now; MCP tools fetch on demand, yet a compact snapshot as context is the tier everyone reads first.
- **What:** a context provider that compacts the workspace moment (last price, session change, book top N, active indicator values, alert states, paper/live tag) into a token-bounded preamble, injected per message behind an explicit user toggle chip; local models get the same treatment first; the MCP protocol-purity rule stays absolute (nothing writes to stdout but the protocol).
- **Scope:** an MCP tool/context provider in AI, the chat VM toggle, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** the preamble fits a tight token budget and reads deterministically for the same snapshot.
- **Tests:** compaction golden, the consent toggle matrix, the paper/live tag correctness.
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.65 (feat `f27df47`; the wave map's alpha.67 ceded to the landing order — the wave's first landing takes the next free number). The split: ZettaTrade.AI owns the pure pair — `MarketMoment` (the paper/live classification handed over as a decided fact, the venue id, the last price, the 24h change, the top-three book levels per side, the enabled indicator setup) and `MarketContextPreambleComposer` (fixed priority: venue tag, price/change, book, setup; 220 tokens at 4 chars/token; the shave drops whole low-priority parts — indicators, then the bids, then the asks, then the 24h change — and never re-words or invents; an all-absent moment renders the honest "no live market data received yet" line). The moment carries no alert states this ship: the alerts lane does not exist yet (T-096 queued), and the shape never invents the absent fact. The App owns the wiring: the consent chip persists as the `UI.ChatMarketContext` key (default off, the startup walk restores it), the preamble rides `WorkspacePromptComposer` behind the workspace head only behind consent, and `ChatContextService` binds the top of the book per symbol switch (an adapter that serves no book stream degrades the book part, never a crash — the T-054 tolerance shape) and the persisted indicator setup (read once at init, re-applied live; the standing time frame survives a symbol switch while the stale facts blank). MCP protocol purity stands untouched (nothing writes to stdout but the protocol). Tests: 33 new (14 composer golden/shave, 19 context/VM consent matrix including the flip-between-sends and the degrade-to-silence lane) plus the pre-existing `ChatContextServiceTests` adapted (the 24h-change fact rides the tick; the rig carries an empty book stream). Hazard: the [TASK-FROMRESULT] gate warning stays on the interface-stub completion (a warning-only false-positive family shared with the suite's NSubstitute canons).

## T-116 — Adapter WS E2E tests flake under solution-parallel load: the 10 s `EventWait` trips on scheduler starvation (queued from the T-073 verification)

- **Branch:** feat/ws-e2e-budget (base `develop` at `df22054`; queued 2026-11-06 from the T-073 post-fix verification, developed 2026-11-07).
- **Origin:** during the T-073 post-fix full-solution verification, `ZettaTrade.Infrastructure.Tests.Exchange.BinanceAdapterWsE2eTests.Depth_and_trade_frames_publish_order_book_and_trades` failed with `System.TimeoutException` inside `WaitAsync(EventWait)` (captured console: the xUnit `[FAIL]` line, 10 s mark, Infrastructure run at 23 s duration) while every focused/localization run stayed green; the merged-tree capture run (`post5.trx` window) left the mock-server handshake, subscribe and all frames delivered under no extra load — the fact passes solo. Entire E2E family carries `private static readonly TimeSpan EventWait = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10)` (`BinanceAdapterWsE2eTests`, `BybitAdapterWsE2eTests`, `BinanceAdapterResubscribeTests`, `BybitAdapterResubscribeTests`, `WebSocketClientTests`, `PaperTradingAdapterTests`).
- **Capture 2026-11-07:** the same starvation signature reproduced on
  `ZettaTrade.Infrastructure.Tests.Exchange.ExchangeAdapterBaseSubscriptionTests`
  during the T-075/T-077 landing wave (a fail under solution-parallel load; green
  solo and on the gate's built-in flaky retry). Add the test to the family pass.
- **What:** the mock-server collect/subscribe/frame budget is a bare-bones constant with a 10 s cap; under solution-level load (parallel testhosts chewing cores, xUnit parallelism in sibling assemblies) a scheduler stall crosses it and the fact fails spuriously — no product defect is involved (the WS client and adapter behave). Root-cause the real latency budget (collector wait vs scheduling), then either raise the family budget uniformly (60 s) or split tight timing assertions (latency ceilings) from the completion waits; no production code changes expected.
- **Scope:** `tests/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure.Tests/Exchange/*Tests.cs` (the shared `EventWait` constants and `PollUntilAsync` budgets), tests only.
- **Acceptance:** full-solution consecutive runs (≥20, cold and warm) hold zero WS E2E timeout failures; the budget rationale is written into the test files' headers.
- **Tests:** no new logic; the existing E2E battery (mock server + adapters) is the test.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the family budget (`7cc4530`, stiffened `4ba66ac`, `40234fa`; the arming discipline `21d83c2`) - the WS E2E family's `EventWait` constants ride the house load budget of 240 s: the bare 10 s cap starved under the scheduler stalls of parallel testhosts (the whole family shares the one constant, so the lift is uniform), the interim 120 s stiffening still tripped the deepest races, and 240 s keeps the family's healthy walls at ~26-30 s while bounded under a full sibling storm; tests-only surface, no product code touched (the budget rides `WaitAsync(EventWait)` and `PollUntilAsync(EventWait)`). The deepest race was found by the series itself: a test pushing frames before their feed's SUBSCRIBE crossed the serialized send gate starved the collector for three full-budget famine runs (walls 246.3-247.1 s, bare TimeoutException at WaitAsync) - arbitrated by arming the feeds before their wire pushes and settling the wire layout before pinning the counts (`21d83c2`). The shipped proof: 27 attempts, 21 CONSECUTIVE green full-solution runs (dotnet exit code, cold bin/obj wipes at runs 1 and 11, warm `--no-build` otherwise), walls 25.5-66.4 s, ZERO WS E2E timeouts across any attempt; the six interim red attempts all hit NON-WS known families and each passed on its rerun (the App panel in-flight family x4 attempts, one App load-sensitive cluster x1 with 10 failures, one Infrastructure `EveryNewSubscription_RerunsTheIdempotentStart` x1) - the exact split the budget is meant to absorb. Hazards: the family budget is a shared constant - new E2E tests inherit it instead of forking their own; the proof artifacts (TSV + TRX tallies) live outside the repo; no release (tests-only deliverable).

## T-123 — Trading-mode chip: paper/testnet/live always visible

- **Branch:** feat/trading-mode-chip (worktree `zt-trading-mode-chip`, based on develop@e06b198).
- **Origin:** the credibility instant: paper/testnet/live must read at one glance from anywhere. The state currently lives in dialogs and the T-127's per-panel badge plan; the safety posture the docs promise (T-117/T-119) deserves a visible seat.
- **What:** a prominent chip in the shell showing the ACTIVE trading lane of the workspace's traded adapter: PAPER (demo/paper), TESTNET (via the T-015 endpoint identity), LIVE (anything else) with per-state colors (calm/prompt/danger). Level flips ride the reactive settings stream; switching INTO live requires a `IConfirmationService` prompt on the flip itself. When detachable windows arrive (T-103), the chip propagates to every window.
- **Scope:** a `TradingModeService` + `TradingLaneResolver` in App (lane by endpoint identity over the T-015 registry snapshot; unparsable identity is an error, never a default), the chip view in the shell's status bar + the gated switch surface (flip-to-live prompts via `IConfirmationService` on the flip itself; decline aborts cleanly), the runtime flip call sites ride the gate, `Trading.*` resx keys in BOTH languages (+ the resx-gate prefix registration, house precedent) and tests. The alpha.76 bump + the CHANGELOG section ride the feat commit; no `Core/Interfaces` changes; detachable windows stay out (T-103, YAGNI) — the service exposes a reusable `IObservable` stream so a per-window chip attaches later.
- **Acceptance:** there is no state where a real-money lane is indistinguishable from paper; the flip-to-live is always a confirmed event; the chip is present in every window context (today the single shell).
- **Tests:** the mode-mapping matrix (demo/testnet/live by endpoint identity, minus the rejection path), the flip confirmation gate, multi-window propagation.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `96701d2` (the board claim), `6a2b4ab` (the feat: `TradingLane` + `TradingModeService` — the resolver maps the paper identity to the paper lane and classifies venue surfaces by their configured record's `IsTestnet`; an identity outside the registry snapshot throws, never resolves by default; the standing `BehaviorSubject` stream bootstraps late subscribers; `ConfirmLaneFlipAsync` gates the live target behind the `Trading.ConfirmGoLive` prompt and passes paper/testnet through), `eca19c6` (the settings-port fix the feat surfaced: the `UrlPairs` shape allowed only string members and rejected the records' own exported `isTestnet` — every store holding an endpoint record refused its own re-import; the gate keeps the one optional camel-case identity member, two-string exports stay valid, functions pin all three sides) and `c6ae166` (the launch-smoke process-hygiene rule the user dutied after night-long leftover exes). The chip rides the status bar between the active exchange name and the language field with clean-theme tint bindings over the candle palette keys; 40 new facts (the mapping matrix + flip gate + the posted-beat chip renders) green. Gate history is thick: the first run tripped on the load-sensitive pump family across a box carrying two stuck verify-loop runs (killed, the box swept empty); the second run surfaced the REAL settings-port regression above and was resolved by the fix; the third run printed `GATE PASSED` with `-AllowProtected` (the bump). The pre-merge launch smoke stayed clean: the walk completed in its run segment with zero ERR/FTL/exception entries (`ProductVersion=0.1.0-alpha.76+eca19c62`, six demo watchlist WRN ticks are the ambient stream fallbacks, not failures), and the process hygiene rule ran both sweeps clean. ## T-124 — Symbol compare: a second series overlaid on the chart

- **Branch:** feat/chart-compare, worktree zt-chart-compare, based on develop@391d7af (claimed 2026-11-09; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074…T-115 backlog cut).
- **Origin:** compare is a demo-standard flex — today's chart is strictly single-symbol and the workspace context is one symbol (T-017); a relative overlay is the missing depth-of-analysis moment.
- **What:** a compare action adding a second series of the same interval from another symbol on the same adapter, rebased to percent-change over the visible window (the T-090 percent mode semantics), with removable legend chips per series and distinct colors; the input flows via the global symbol palette (T-095 synergy, not a hard dependency); pan/zoom/interval switches keep both series coherent.
- **Scope:** the multi-series renderer in Charting, chart VM wiring, resx en+ru, tests.
- **Acceptance:** the overlay holds under churn; removal restores the single-series rendering bit-exact; axis invariants (T-035/T-037 canon) never break with two series.
- **Tests:** multi-series render golden, percent-rebasing goldens, the removal-restore identity, the axis invariants.
- **Status:** done. Canon: shipped as 0.1.0-alpha.86 (feat `23bd107` after the board claim `70b0eae`), supervisor-solo on day 2 — both T-124 workers died at the free-usage wall (the second mid-implementation, leaving ~895 uncommitted lines); the takeover reviewed every hunk, adopted the design wholesale and completed the missing legs (tests, canon), discarding nothing. The compare layer family: `ChartCompareLayer` (label = the normalized symbol, its own fetched bars) plus the pure `ChartCompareSeries` helpers — exact open-time alignment (an unshared slot is an honest null, never a nearest-neighbor substitute), each layer's own percent anchor (ITS first aligned close) and the axis-domain candidates the shared scale unions; the renderer rides additive-only INTO `CandlestickChart` (`CompareLayers`/`MainSeriesLabel` StyledProperties, a private `CompareFrame` record, the snapshot's two added members, the union refactor that IS the historical two-bound call bit-for-bit at empty lists, `DrawCompareLayers` painting over the volume profile and under the indicator/VWAP overlays, the legend chip strip pinned top-right whose cached detach zones feed the `CompareRemovals` stream the pointer press routes handled BEFORE pan arming can capture). Percent semantics picked and documented: PERCENT mode maps each series through its own anchor (the (100→110) ≡ (1000→1100) equivalence); the linear mode shares raw prices — a far-priced neighbor honestly stretches the one padded scale (squashing, not clipping); the log mode shares the floor; pan/zoom keep both series coherent, an INTERVAL or EXCHANGE change drops the layers, a same-interval symbol switch keeps them. The VM refuses, never substitutes: no loaded series / self / duplicate / the fifth pick (cap 4) / a fetch fault with the carried detail / an empty fetch, all localized (`Chart.Compare*` family + `Palette.CompareHint`, 9 keys en+ru — the lint's existing `Chart.` module prefix already covers them, so no check-resx edit was needed), a fetch that races a rebase drops its own result quietly (the frame-stamp + re-check shape the repair chain keeps). The palette leg: `IsPickingCompare` rides `BeginComparePickAsync` (a newer pick resolves the older with null), every close ends the pick null, compare rows rank the CURRENT exchange only (a foreign-exchange row could never serve the standing adapter's fetch). Removal-restore identity proven END TO END through the T-094 export harness: a layer on, then off, renders the plain frame BYTE FOR BYTE; removals subtract only the named layer in the VM. Tests: 23 new — 9 pure goldens (`ChartCompareSeriesTests`, UI.Tests), 3 render facts (`CandlestickChartCompareTests`: the byte identity, the far-priced layer's in-plot legend ink pinning the axis union, the honest-gap/wholesale-drop render), 11 VM facts riding the `ChartViewModelTests` kit; full suites green (App.Tests 1333, UI.Tests 298). Honest simplifications: the stale-fetch drop is pinned by construction (the fake's bars gate cannot hold a fetch mid-world), and the "multi-series golden" rides byte-determinism + in-plot-ink instead of a full bitmap golden. Gate: run 1 = GATE PASSED with `-AllowProtected` (the bump; standing warnings: no agent-scope, gitleaks-skip).

## T-125 — Delivery pipeline: CI builds + GitHub Releases (Win/macOS/Linux)

- **Branch:** feat/release-pipeline, worktree zt-release-pipeline, based on develop@0823719 (claimed 2026-11-09; queued 2026-11-06 (presentation-track gap, supervisor idea at the monetization review; not urgent)).
- **Origin:** the repo has no CI at all (no `.github`); binaries exist only inside the builds; public users and partners need a real download door — the alpha program (T-129), the landing (T-126) and every demo link all stand on Releases.
- **What:** `.github/workflows/release.yml` — on a version tag (`v*`), publishes self-contained per-OS artifacts (win-x64 zip, osx-x64/osx-arm64 zip, linux-x64 tar.gz) built straight from the tag's `Directory.Build.props` version, with the release notes taken straight from the matching CHANGELOG section; artifact naming `zettatrade-<version>-<rid>.<ext>`. Code signing and notarization are deliberately out of scope until beta (the docs say when that flips). No product code changes.
- **Scope:** `.github/workflows/release.yml` + a small packaging helper in `scripts/` (a local dry-run publish), docs note in `docs/SECURITY.md` (what the artifacts contain, hash handling).
- **Acceptance:** a tagged release candidate produces three OS artifacts on GitHub Releases with the correct version strings and the CHANGELOG excerpt attached; a broken build fails the workflow loudly.
- **Tests:** workflow lint; a smoke script asserting artifact presence + embedded version match (CI-side); the local dry-run publish command is verifiable by the acceptance agent.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `2f93316` (the board claim) and `7e4db54` (the feat: `.github/workflows/release.yml` runs on a `v*` tag push only, a three-OS fail-fast-false matrix of self-contained publishes - win-x64, osx-x64 and osx-arm64 zip legs, linux-x64 tar.gz - archiving `zettatrade-<version>-<rid>.<ext>` flat-rooted; the version is parsed at workflow level from the tag checkout's committed `Directory.Build.props` and cross-guarded against the tag name (a mismatched tag throws instead of shipping); the release notes ride the verbatim `## [0.1.0-alpha.NN]` CHANGELOG.md section of the same tree with a missing section throwing; the release-creating job asserts every archive present and non-empty, ships `zettatrade-<version>-sha256.txt` in the `sha256sum -c` two-space convention and creates the GitHub Release through the preinstalled `gh` CLI; each matrix leg smoke-asserts the app host plus the embedded informational version against the props version; permissions minimal (contents read house-wide, write only on the release-creating job), no secrets beyond the automatic `GITHUB_TOKEN`), `scripts/release-dry-run.ps1` (the local one-RID mirror: the version from the committed props, the CHANGELOG section presence, the same self-contained publish command and the same app-host plus embedded-version smoke; `-OutDir` is caller-given by design and mandatory, the publish tree lands in `<OutDir>/publish-<rid>`, the script prints what it `would archive` without building the zip and exits 0 only on a fully passing run) and the `docs/SECURITY.md` release-artifacts note (what the artifacts contain - the app plus the bundled self-contained runtime and the four built-in theme JSON files, never user data or credentials, built in CI from the tag's tree; hash handling with the per-OS verify commands; nothing signed or notarized until beta, with the beta-flip checklist of Authenticode signing and hardened-runtime notarization over `ISecretStore`-class credential storage). No version bump and no CHANGELOG section: no product code changed. Local proof (this box, Windows PowerShell 5.1, dotnet 10.0.403): the win-x64 dry run passed with the props version `0.1.0-alpha.77` matched by the embedded informational version, the app host present, 289 files published, `Would archive: zettatrade-0.1.0-alpha.77-win-x64.zip`, exit 0 in 3.6 s warm; two draft defects were caught by the smoke itself on the first run and fixed inside this branch: the numeric file-version string `0.1.0.0` is not the embedded version (the informational string `0.1.0-alpha.77+<commit sha>` is) and a cosmetic publish-count wrap on the helper's own printout. The bundled indicator plugins do not ride the archive by design: the loader reads the user-data plugins directory (`ConfigPathResolver.PluginsDirectory`), so the publish tree carrying the app plus its satellite assets is complete. Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `.github/*` and `scripts/*` protected-area edits are exactly this task's sanctioned scope); the workflow could not be linted by actionlint/yamllint - both absent from this box, recorded as absent. Pending CI-side acceptance (stated plainly, never faked): the first real `v*` tag on the public repository - the tag pushing, the four archives plus the sums file landing on GitHub Releases with correct version strings and the CHANGELOG excerpt attached, and a broken build failing its leg loudly - cannot be exercised from this session; the runner semantics (`gh` release creation, the artifact download merge, the per-OS pwsh runtimes loading a net10.0 managed dll for the metadata-only version probe, `Compress-Archive` on the macOS legs) certify only on that first push. This board-flip commit closes the section.
