## T-129 — Alpha program: testers onboarding, feedback triage, public metrics

- **Branch:** feat/alpha-program, worktree zt-alpha-program, based on develop@b38f7c1 (claimed 2026-11-10 per standing directive 6; queued 2026-11-06 at the monetization review). **Blocker:** T-126 (done — the landing is shipped).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `ae8e744`, the artifacts `ec2e9a9`; a docs-only task — no version bump, no changelog entry. The artifacts: `docs/ALPHA-PROGRAM.md` is the program canon (the no-telemetry absolute; the tester route download → run → report → board; the triage pipeline table with the lean 6-label set — `bug`, `feedback`, `triage`, `board` + the GitHub defaults `good first issue`, `help wanted`; the public-metrics gate: only numbers re-derivable from a cited public link, the local-first promise untouched); `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml` disables blank issues and routes security reports to `docs/SECURITY.md`, `bug-report.yml` asks the build version (the status bar shows it), OS, adapter/mode, numbered repro and the log tail (T-101's log viewer), `feedback.yml` is deliberately free-form with a kind picker — both forms apply `triage` automatically. `scripts/labels-sync.ps1` syncs exactly the canonical label set idempotently through the gh CLI (create-or-update, never deletes). `scripts/generate-metrics.ps1` renders `docs-site/public/metrics/index.html` from the public GitHub REST API only (releases + per-asset download counts, stars/forks/watchers, contributors, the cited resolved-issues search) with every number carrying its re-derivation link; refused-not-defaulted: any API failure fails the run loud and the previous page stands — the one legitimate non-failure state is the explicit PENDING page while the repository is not visible to the public API (a page with no numbers, never a fabricated zero), and the generator stays pure ASCII because Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a BOM-less ps1 as CP1252 — a UTF-8 em-dash byte inside a here-string decodes into a smart quote and breaks the tokenizer (the repo convention stays no-BOM, so scripts stay ASCII-only; both new scripts verified parse-clean). `.github/workflows/metrics.yml` rebuilds the page weekly (schedule + dispatch) and commits it into the docs-site deploy. The landing's alpha section (en and ru) now links the issue chooser, the program doc and the metrics page — the "program is queued" copy is retired. Environment notes: the gh CLI is not on the dev machine (like gitleaks), so the label sync and the dry-run issue walk happen GitHub-side once the artifacts land — the verification record at the end of ALPHA-PROGRAM.md describes the exact procedure.
- **Origin:** the traction gap: features ship, but there is no channel between them and real humans, and any investor conversation needs honest, verifiable numbers — not private telemetry.
- **What:** a lightweight program over the public surface: a call-for-testers section on the landing (T-126), feedback intake via GitHub (issue templates + a label set), a triage pipeline feeding findings into the board (TASKS.md rows in the usual lifecycle), and a public metrics page built ONLY from public GitHub data (Release download counts, contributor/star counts, issues resolved) with sources cited — the local-first/no-telemetry promise of the product stays absolute (nothing in the app phones home). The gate: numbers must be re-derivable by anyone clicking the cited links.
- **Scope:** docs-site section(s), `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/*` + labels, `docs/ALPHA-PROGRAM.md`, the metrics page generator (static, CI-able); no product code.
- **Acceptance:** a tester route reads download → report via template → labeled issue → a board row; the metrics page shows only public-source numbers with their links visible.
- **Tests:** none (docs/ops); the template routing verified by a dry-run issue.

## T-130 — Extract Livery into a standalone private repo (MIT kept), consume as a package

- **Branch:** feat/livery-split, worktree zt-livery-split, based on develop@1f9475c (claimed 2026-11-10; the split half already lives outside this repo — the Livery repo bootstrapped from the subtree-split history, package 0.1.0 published to the private GitHub Packages feed, CI green — so this task lands the consumption switch). Queued 2026-11-06 (open-core structure at the licensing review: Livery goes to its own PRIVATE repo, the license stays MIT — the sources stay hidden and the package is the public surface, flipping the repo public later is one click; this is the pilot of the multi-repo mechanics. Sequenced between alpha releases, coordinated across sessions — Livery paths are touched by UI work).
- **Origin:** Livery is domain-neutral by design (appearance styles/controls plus the extensions like `ObserveOnUiThread`), has no tie to trading logic, and the paid plugin plan will ride the same multi-repo mechanics this split rehearses first (a versioned package, the two-PR cross-repo workflow).
- **What:** a one-shot history-preserving split (`git filter-repo --subdirectory-filter src/Livery`) into the new private repo `Livery` on the same account, the LICENSE file stays MIT; a publish workflow shipping the NuGet package (a public binary on nuget.org, sources stay private); ZettaTrade removes the in-tree `src/Livery` and switches to `PackageReference` pinned to the package version; Livery gains its own README, its in-repo test suite and semver+changelog discipline from day one; AGENTS.md and ZettaTrade_SKILL.md updated (the target-layout note loses `src/Livery`, plus a two-repo ownership paragraph: appearance-only changes go through the Livery repo, platform-specific and trading code never move there).
- **Scope:** the bootstrap and split commits in the new Livery repo, the ZettaTrade consumption switch, the docs notes; no product behavior changes.
- **Acceptance:** the new repo's history reaches back through the split for Livery files (nothing lost at the splice); a clean ZettaTrade clone builds with zero in-tree Livery sources and the full test matrix stays green; the two-PR cross-repo path lands once as a rehearsal (a style tweak: the version-bumped PR in Livery, then the bump PR here).
- **Tests:** the matrix over the consumption switch; the Livery suite in the new repo; the rehearsal change verified on both sides.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the consumption switch `b19ace1`, the compiled-surface pins `3869654`, riding Livery 0.1.1 `ae6ee9d`; shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.93`. The Livery half (the standalone repo): bootstrapped from the subtree-split history — nothing lost at the splice, Livery file history resolves straight through the filter-repo; package 0.1.0 published to the private GitHub Packages feed (MIT stays, sources stay hidden, the package is the public surface); the bump rehearsal 0.1.1 (`4cfe1aa`) published and consumed one-line — the two-repo path landed in the rehearsal order (version-bumped release there, consuming bump here); the style pins home (`82581c9`, the suite rides build CI on every develop commit; the step-path miss fixed in `2c7dcdc`). The consumption switch: `src/Livery` gone (−1969 lines), five consumers ride `PackageReference Include="Livery" Version="$(LiveryVersion)"` with ONE version fact in `Directory.Build.props` — a future package bump is a one-line PR; the root `nuget.config` pins nuget.org plus the `zettatrade` GitHub Packages source with no committed credentials, and the empirically verified nuget quirk carries the auth: user-level credentials keyed by the SAME source name apply across config levels, so CI replicates the developer setup with a same-name `dotnet nuget add source` + GITHUB_TOKEN (release and soak gained `packages: read` + the feed-auth step; the Livery package grants this repository Actions access once). The chrome pins walk the COMPILED package surface — the object graph the `StyleInclude` serves — instead of reading removed sources: Avalonia 12 hides `AvaloniaLocator.CurrentMutable` behind `[PrivateApi]` (stripped from the ref assembly) and refuses user-implemented resolvers, so the headless platform is the only supported route, and it binds its compositor to the dispatcher-owner thread — the pin stands it up ON the T-138 pump thread through the pump's own drain path (one posted job: setup + load, executed only during a requested drain, never spontaneously — the frozen dispatcher world stays frozen); the source-text pins (title-bar automation names, focus indicators, template content bindings) assert the Livery repo's own sources now. GATE PASSED; ff-merge to develop.

## T-131 — Plugin SDK seam: versioned plugin abstractions for out-of-tree modules

- **Branch:** feat/plugin-sdk, worktree zt-plugin-sdk, based on develop@2bf507b (claimed 2026-11-10 per standing directive 6; queued 2026-11-06 at the T-074:T-115 backlog cut). Open-core prerequisite; Core contracts are the supervisor's lane per AGENTS — additive interfaces only.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `2ce9590`, the feat `accf0b9` (the alpha bump, the charter pin, docs and fixtures ride the feat); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.89`. The contract assembly `ZettaTrade.Plugins.Abstractions` is net10.0 and references ONLY `ZettaTrade.Core` (no Livery, no UI stack — any layer may consume the contract without dragging Avalonia along); its version is pinned at 1.0.0 and the governor rule lives in `docs/PLUGINS.md`: the pinned public surface IS the SemVer promise — a break on any public member = a major bump, and the golden fails an accidental one. Every plugin class manifests itself through `ZettaTradePluginAttribute` (plugin id, display name, SemVer 2 version, dependency ids); the loader constructs it once through the parameterless-ctor fact; the lifecycle follows the `IAsyncInitializable` canon — `InitializeAsync(context, token)` completes before the loader collects what the plugin registered; the extension points are concrete records — `PluginPanelDescriptor(id, titleResxKey, widgetFactory)` (the localization canon survives the seam: the panel title is a resx key, nothing user-facing string-bakes), `PluginToolbarItemDescriptor`, `PluginSettingsSectionDescriptor`. The DI entry `AddPluginSdk(scanDirectory)` (`ZettaTrade.Infrastructure.Plugins`) registers the loader, the mutable `PluginExtensionRegistry` and the read-only `IPluginExtensionRegistry` with snapshot semantics; the loader joins the startup walk automatically (a `IAsyncInitializable` registration of the CompositionRoot). The loader `PluginSdkHost` walks the plugins directory the catalogs share (top level only; a missing directory is an empty world, not a fault): unreadable assemblies warn-and-continue, a partly-unreadable assembly's readable types are scanned, an `IZettaTradePlugin` class without the manifest refuses, the manifest gate refuses blank ids/names, non-SemVer-2 versions and blank dependency entries — one lie refuses the plugin; duplicate ids refuse EVERY carrier deterministically (never scan-order first-wins); a declared dependency no scan produces refuses the dependent; a cycle refuses every member (the self-edge included); the survivors start through a deterministic ordinal-sorted Kahn walk. One plugin's load leg arms its own child DI layer — the plugin's registrations resolve FIRST (a host registration can never shadow a plugin service), the `IPluginContext` seat joins that layer at the loader's construction (a plugin registration can never steal it), and the context resolves the plugin layer before the host's root provider, answering NULL for an absent capability (rejected-not-defaulted, the plugin decides; the trading-safety obligations ride the same window: risk/confirmation arrive as host capabilities, never fabricated). The whole pass never throws — a hostile plugins directory cannot take the startup walk down; whatever survived the gates lands in the registry and every verdict pins in the `PluginLoadReport` (loaded/refused/errors, ordinal-sorted). The cold-start charter pin (`PluginCatalogPackRegistrationTests.ColdStart_TheProductionGraph_ReferencesNoPluginAssembly`) now allowlists exactly ONE plugin contract reference — `ZettaTrade.Plugins.Abstractions`; every other `ZettaTrade.Plugins.*` assembly stays forbidden in the production graph. Fixture split: `ZettaTrade.Plugins.Fixtures` (the valid shapes: `SamplePanelPlugin` full-surface and the `AlphaPlugin` + `BetaPlugin` chain proving the dependency order) and `ZettaTrade.Plugins.Fixtures.Hazards` (duplicate id, missing dependency, bad version, manifest-less class, self-cycle, an A-B ring). Tests shipped: 5 loader goldens (sample+chain load with extension publication; hazards refuse loudly sparing the survivors; garbage assembly warns and the scan continues; a partly-unreadable assembly does not break the scan; the missing directory), `PluginSemVerTests`, and the reflection-surface golden `PluginContractBaselineTests` — 26/26 plugin facts, 417/417 Infrastructure tests, the App charter suites green on rerun (one LogTail timing flake retried green alone — the known family). Docs: `docs/PLUGINS.md` author guide + the README pointer. No resx keys shipped: the seam carries no user-facing strings — loader verdicts are file logs. The registry's shell-side rendering (panels opening in the shell) is the consumers' work downstream — the seam ships the registration, not the rendering.
- **Origin:** plugin-first is the architecture since day one, yet no formally VERSIONED contract exists for modules compiled outside the solution; without one every core refactor breaks out-of-tree builds — the paid modules first of all — and the marketplace shape stays theoretical.
- **What:** a contract surface `ZettaTrade.Plugins.Abstractions` — the plugin identity/manifest, the lifecycle via the `IAsyncInitializable` canon, DI registration entry points, the UI extension points (panel/toolbar/settings section registration), market-data and services access, and the trading-safety obligations (any order path inside a plugin passes `IRiskManager`/`IConfirmationService`, no exceptions); SemVer discipline declared with the governor note (a breaking release = a major bump + a pinned contract baseline test); the author guide `docs/PLUGINS.md` documents the contract, the rejected-not-defaulted error semantics and the platform rules (nothing writes to stdout except MCP protocol messages; logs go to files only).
- **Scope:** the contract assembly, the loader wiring (one blessed path to register external assemblies), docs; NO proprietary code lands in the public repo — the surface stays MIT.
- **Acceptance:** a sample MIT fixture plugin built out of the solution compiles against the published contract and loads through the loader with its panels registering; the matrix stays green with the sample present and absent.
- **Tests:** loader contract tests (manifest parsing, duplicate ids, missing dependencies fail loudly), the end-to-end sample load golden, a public-API surface test pinning the contract baseline (accidental breaks fail CI).

## T-132 — Closed-source plugin packaging: signed artifacts + local license-key activation

- **Branch:** feat/plugin-packaging, worktree zt-plugin-packaging, based on develop@69badc4 (claimed 2026-11-10 per standing directive 6; queued 2026-11-06 at the licensing review). **Blocker:** T-131 (done — the SDK seam shipped as alpha.89).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `415890c`, the feat `072fe02` (the alpha.91 bump, the CHANGELOG and both doc legs ride the feat). Recovered from the dead session's uncommitted draft adopted hunk-by-hunk per the recovery protocol — no restart. The licensing shelf: the `zt1.<payload>.<signature>` token (unpadded base64url, RSA-SHA256 over the EXACT payload bytes), the claims record (`product`=ZettaTrade binding, pluginId, subject, validity window) serialized with the Web defaults, an explicit 7-status `LicenseValidationStatus` where every non-valid value refuses (unconfigured/missing/malformed/tampered/expired/wrong-product) — refused-not-defaulted everywhere: activation stores nothing on refusal and the loader gate keeps the module out; a malformed protected-plugins list protects EVERYTHING via the `"*"` sentinel (an unparsable operator list must open no gate). The gate `LicenseGate` rides `PluginSdkHost` before EVERY load; the keys and the protect list live on the settings document (`Licensing/VerificationKey`, `ProtectedPlugins`, `Key.{pluginId}`). The EULA leg: `LicenseEulaService` reads `<pluginId>.EULA.txt` next to the artifact in the plugins scan directory (a non-filename id or an unreadable file degrades to no text); the first activation of a module carrying it surfaces the agreement and waits for the accept tick, the acceptance persists per plugin id (`EulaAccepted.{pluginId}`), a persisted acceptance goes straight to validation, and nothing stores until the tick. The dialog canon decision: the VM exposes `AttachVerdictRendering()` returning `IDisposable` — the window owns subscriptions via WhenActivated + DisposeWith; the VM is NOT IDisposable (proven by experiment: `DisposeWith` binds only to IDisposable receivers). Cannon notes struck in the test matrix: static shared NSubstitute fakes accumulate calls across facts — fresh substitutes per fact; an unconfigured `Task<string?>` substitute does NOT default to null — configure explicitly; `CanExecute` reads via `await Observable.FirstAsync(command.CanExecute)` (the plain `.FirstAsync()` is ambiguous under `using ReactiveUI.Primitives`); C# named arguments are case-sensitive; `(x % 4) switch` needs the parens. resx en+ru balanced 17/17 license keys in both files; the mojibake byte audit over every touched file passed. 58 new test cases: 45 licensing (token, validator goldens, gate, activation, EULA), 10 dialog-VM facts under the serialized dispatcher collection, 1 menu-route fact, 2 host gate-stage facts; the full-suite run tripped the known WS-E2E flake family twice, the re-run green 464/464. The doc legs: `docs/LICENSES.md` (the open-core split at a glance, the never-lands-here punish list) and `docs/PLUGIN-PACKAGING.md` (the private-repo contract: repo naming, EULA packaging, the signed-artifact CI recipe, the key-issuance shapes).
- **Origin:** in the open-core split the closed modules live in private repos and ship as SIGNED artifacts; customer machines activate them with a license key validated locally — the entitlement flow must exist and behave identically everywhere before real money moves.
- **What:** the private-repo convention fixed in docs: the name `zettatrade-plugin-<feature>`, a proprietary LICENSE + EULA file, CI building a signed NuGet/zip artifact whose version tracks the SDK version it builds against; the consumer-side activation: the license key entered once (a dialog or a file), LOCAL validation (an offline-signed key check — no phone-home required; an optional online revalidation may arrive later without touching the no-telemetry promise — validation logs to files only), and refused-not-defaulted semantics: invalid, expired, tampered or wrong-product keys REFUSE activation loudly and the plugin stays off; the EULA surfaces at first activation.
- **Scope:** the key validation lib + the activation UI (resx en+ru), the packaging docs/templates for the private CI, `docs/LICENSES.md` describing the open-core split (MIT core vs proprietary modules) in a glance-readable form; the private repos themselves appear only per actual module.
- **Acceptance:** a signed closed fixture activates with a valid key and is refused with an explicit error on invalid/expired/tampered/wrong-product keys; no network is required for validation; no proprietary artifact or wording lands in the public repo.
- **Tests:** key crypto goldens (valid/expired/tampered/wrong-product/missing), the activation gate in UI, the refused-not-defaulted contract on every failure path, file-only logging for the validator.

## T-133 - MCP paper fills enter the journal: tool-placed fills ride the ledger the panels and PnL read

- **Branch:** queued 2026-11-06 (supervisor note raised at the T-082 landing: the PnL core's report flagged the blind spot; queued immediately so the seam does not rot).
- **Origin:** fills placed through the `TradingToolsRegistrar` MCP tools bypass the App-side SQLite order journal - the canonical local ledger that order history, positions, CSV export, trade statistics and the T-082 PnL engine all read. An MCP-placed paper fill is a real (paper-lane) money flow invisible to every accounting surface.
- **What:** the MCP trading tools' execution path writes through the same journal ingestion the panels use (or the journal seam gains the missing notifications - the lightest cut that makes MCP-placed fills appear in order history, positions, CSV exports, the T-083 statistics and the T-082 PnL realized history); no scope creep into real-adapter behavior; the paper/demo lane is the acceptance surface.
- **Scope:** Infrastructure/App wiring under the MCP trading tools (Infra-only; no Core entity or interface changes expected), tests.
- **Acceptance:** a fill executed via an MCP tool becomes visible in every journal-backed surface and is accounted in PnL deterministically across restarts, exactly like a fill placed from the UI.
- **Tests:** MCP-placed fill golden (a journal row and a PnL realized entry appear), mixed UI+MCP fills keep the expected ordering, restart determinism holds.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the journaling wrapper seam (`58f3296`), the MCP fill goldens (`c25c872`) and shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.50` (see CHANGELOG.md). Chosen cut: the tools' registrar resolves a `McpJournalingTradingAdapter` decorator around the paper adapter, recording every accepted placement into the same session-journal + SQLite-ledger pair the dialog and position actions write, and every accepted cancellation via the same `MarkCancelledAsync` under the wrapped adapter's exchange identity - the lightest cut, no duplicated ingestion. PnL parity rides the engine's 5-second ledger sync and its boot replay (the same surface the UI lane uses), each fill ingested exactly once with the T-080 commission passthrough; restart determinism pinned by a recomputed-engine golden. Both persistence arms best effort: a journal fault is logged and never fails an accepted tool action. Risk-check order untouched; Core untouched (0 diff). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).

## T-134 — Supervisor-mode skill (OpenCode): the standing directives on explicit invocation

- **Branch:** feat/supervisor-skill (base `develop` at `086579b`; queued and developed 2026-11-07, supervisor-owned task).
- **Origin:** the standing directives (waves of up to four subagents on non-conflicting tasks, supervisor review + self-merge into `develop`, handoff-state persistence, crash and zombie recovery) lived only in live session context — a fresh session must be able to pick them up from the repo itself.
- **What:** an OpenCode skill at `.opencode/skills/zettatrade-supervisor/SKILL.md` encoding the full supervisor protocol: wave planning (max four subagents, conflict-sliced, one Charting task per wave), worker briefs + fences, version-bump assignment per wave, the landing/rebase protocol (sync check, bump/CHANGELOG/board conflict map, gates with or without `-AllowProtected`, ff-merge + worktree cleanup), the handoff-state-file convention, crash recovery including subagent re-spawn by sessionID and zombie stand-down arbitration, the standing self-merge directive (user-issued in the session; the skill ships the English statement only), and the explicit-invocation discipline with `disable-model-invocation: true` (never auto-fires; default mode = human review).
- **Scope:** `.opencode/skills/zettatrade-supervisor/SKILL.md` + the `docs/TASKS*.md` rows. Docs-only: no code, no version bump, no CHANGELOG section.
- **Acceptance:** a fresh session invoked explicitly runs the next wave per the standing directives without further prompting; without the invocation the skill stays undiscoverable to the model and nothing changes in default behavior.
- **Tests:** none (docs-only); the gate covers the mechanical checks. Manual proof: skill discovery from the project scope + explicit `@zettatrade-supervisor` load.

## T-136 — Repo hygiene: baked mojibake repair, machine-path purge, prevention lints

- **Branch:** `chore/repo-hygiene` (worktree `zt-presentation-backlog`, based on develop@cd62fcb)
- **Origin:** the user-directed split: the work first rode `feat/presentation-backlog` alongside the presentation-track board claim; the supervisor separated the backlog landing (T-120..T-129 rows only) from the repo fixes, which now stand as this dedicated task.
- **What:** the whole-repo repair of the double-encoding wave (UTF-8 bytes re-read as Windows-1251, up to three generations deep) - `CHANGELOG.md` (130 sites), `Directory.Build.props` (the version-policy comment rewritten ASCII-safe by design), four chart/test comment sites; plus the ten arrow sites the first inversion pass lost (the Windows-1251 encoder best-fits U+2192 to the byte 0x3E, so the deepest arrow sites inverted to a plain greater-than instead of the arrow). Forward-recovery proof: 144/144 sites reproduce the observed junk byte-for-byte at their corruption depths; 89 of 127 anchorable `CHANGELOG.md` sites also match their clean historical ancestors byte-identically. The machine-local absolute worktree paths on nine archived Branch rows were dropped to the archive's own majority name-only form, keeping branches, worktree names and base hashes.
- **Prevention:** the gate scans the same 652-file repo text set for both hazards - `[MOJIBAKE]` (the corruption-only marker set of the double-encoding bug, with files read as UTF-8 explicitly so Windows PowerShell 5.1 cannot fabricate false positives out of legit dashes) and `[MACHINE-PATH]` (drive-letter paths rooted in Users/Projects; URL schemes excluded by a lookbehind so https:// and avares:// stay legal). The matching canon line lives on the board header, in `AGENTS.md` rule 5 (the registered skill copy kept in sync) and in the multi-agent sync section of `ZettaTrade_SKILL.md`.
- **Acceptance (achieved):** both lints scan clean across 652 text files; `GATE PASSED` with `-BaseBranch develop -AllowProtected` (the gate edits are protected-area work). No version bump: repo hygiene only, no app change.
- **Status:** done. Merged into develop as `a9c66dc`.

## T-137 — Fix the app startup crash: the concrete settings store resolves in DI

- **Branch:** `fix/startup-di` (worktree `zt-startup-di`, based on develop@986a728; reported 2026-11-08 — the first app launch since the alpha.68…72 stretch landed with no smoke run between waves).
- **Origin:** the app builds but dies at launch with the CLR unhandled-exception exit (0xE0434352) before the main window exists: `InvalidOperationException: Unable to resolve service for type 'ZettaTrade.Infrastructure.Configuration.SettingsService' while attempting to activate 'ZettaTrade.App.Services.SettingsPortService'`. T-100 registered the port singleton while its design (the doc comment: the batch/snapshot primitives are the concrete store's members) requires the CONCRETE `SettingsService` resolution — and `AddSingleton<SettingsService>` never existed in any commit, so startup has been broken since T-100 and the log gap (the last successful run 2026-11-06) hid it through two landing waves.
- **What:** extract the settings-store registrations into their own module extension per the house root rule (`AddSettingsStoreServices`): one singleton instance registered by the concrete type and forwarded as `ISettingsService`, so interface consumers (chat, layout, the app walk) and concrete consumers (the settings port) share the one store. The settings ViewModel's `SettingsPortService` dependency then activates and the startup walk completes.
- **Scope:** `CompositionRoot.cs`'s settings-store block (hunk-local), a new `SettingsStoreDependencyInjection.cs`, the registration regression facts, the alpha bump + CHANGELOG; no Core interface change, no port or store logic change.
- **Acceptance:** the built app launches and stays alive through the composition walk (the Serilog walk shows no startup-failure entry), and the targeted registration facts pin the shared instance.
- **Tests:** the registration matrix (the concrete-first shape, the singleton identity across the concrete and the interface, the port activation over the real graph shell).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch claims at develop@986a728 and lands the fix as `6fdb94c` (single feat commit with the alpha.73 bump + the CHANGELOG section). Root cause: the T-100 port's concrete-over-interface dependency by design was never backed by a registration of the concrete type in any commit, and the crash survived two landing waves because no launch happened between alpha.68 and the user's report - follow-up candidate: a startup smoke in the landing acceptance routines. Proof: the 0.1.0-alpha.73 build launched and stayed alive with zero failure entries in its own log segment (the composition walk walked through to the live terminal: workspace selection, charts, the Bybit WebSocket); the registration facts pin the shared one-store identity; App.Tests 1125/1125 in one run (the known flake family did not trip), the gate passed on the first run. Merged into develop as `6fdb94c`. Follow-up encoded the same day by user direction: the pre-merge launch smoke became standing directive 5 in the supervisor skill (Landing protocol step 5 carries the procedure - the log-slice discipline exists because the day file accumulates stale crash records, and the whole-file grep of this task's own smoke initially miscounted against them).

## T-138 — Stabilize the App.Tests dispatcher tally flake: one serialized dispatcher world

- **Branch:** feat/flaky-pump-tally, worktree zt-flaky-pump-tally, based on develop@db66924 (claimed 2026-11-09; the T-104 gate run 2 cascade — 53 fails, every one in the documented dispatcher-VM family — made the standing flake family the T-104 landing blocker, and the user picked the stabilization-first route).
- **Origin:** the T-135 sibling shape: T-101 recorded the tally flake (37 fails, then all-green, then 38 fails within minutes on an identical binary), T-127's gate run 1 and T-104's gate run 2 both cascaded (54/53 fails; the pump's own `DrainAsync` fact the recorded casualty). The TRX evidence pins the mechanism: a dispatcher job failing `AvaloniaSynchronizationContext.Ensure` thread-ownership inside `RunJobs` surfaces through the pump's drain path and poisons every subsequent drain of the shared `Dispatcher.UIThread` — a cross-collection interference, not a semantic bug in these facts.
- **What:** win the dispatcher first-touch race BY CONSTRUCTION, tests-only (NO version bump, NO CHANGELOG section): Avalonia binds `Dispatcher.UIThread` to its FIRST-touching thread, and the pump's static-constructor install only won the class-load lottery (the recorded 37-fails/all-green/38-fails flip-flop) — the hang dump pinned the missing piece: DISCOVERY itself touches the test assembly (xUnit `MemberData` reflection invokes property getters) before any test class's static ctor, so a foreign thread can claim the dispatcher while the pump is not even asked; every pump drain then fails "The calling thread cannot access this object" and the serial block cascades. The fix installs the pump from a custom xUnit test framework (`[assembly: TestFramework]`, instantiated before ANY discovery reflection — the earliest executable hook xUnit v2 offers). A `[ModuleInitializer]` was tried first and REFUTED by the hang dump: the module initializer blocked on the pump thread's ready signal while the runtime refuses to run same-assembly code (the pump's RunPump) until the module initializer completes — a guaranteed self-deadlock (2/2 cert runs hung at discovery, zero tests started). `OrderBookViewModelTests` (a real drainer riding the parallel default) plus `FirstRunWizardViewModelTests` and `PositionsPanelViewModelTests` (VM-internal posters) join `AvaloniaDispatcherSerial` explicitly, and the collection's contract doc records the poison shape.
- **Scope:** `tests/ZettaTrade.App.Tests` only (the xUnit test-framework hook file, the three `[Collection]` attributes, the collection's contract doc); NOTHING in src/*.
- **Acceptance:** three consecutive plain full App.Tests runs green on the quiet box, then GATE PASSED (max 2 runs; a known flake-family trip of the gate's flaky retry is recorded, not iterated).
- **Tests:** the fix IS the test; the certification is the 3x-green streak.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch lands the fix as `0bce039` (a single feat commit; tests-only — no version bump, no CHANGELOG section). The refuted path is canon too: the `[ModuleInitializer]` installer self-deadlocked by construction (the hang dump: the module ctor blocked on the pump thread's ready signal while the runtime refuses to start same-assembly code — RunPump frozen at its prestub; 2/2 cert runs hung at discovery with zero tests started), and the same dump pinned the flake's missing piece — discovery-time `MemberData` reflection touches the test assembly before any test-class static ctor, which is how a foreign thread won the dispatcher first-touch lottery (the T-101 37→0→38 flip-flops). Certification: App.Tests 1207/1207 green in three consecutive runs (16 s / 16 s / 17 s — faster than the 42–48 s serial-runner attempts: collection parallelism is back and the pump stays deterministic), then GATE PASSED on run 1 (no protected flag needed). Discipline notes for the canon: one test run per shell invocation with `--blame-hang` (a wedge self-diagnoses into a dump + the hung test's name instead of eating the budget), the TRX file as the authoritative record, and sub-5-minute background-shell witnessing (the supervisor skill carries the standing rule). Merged into develop by fast-forward; the worktree and the branch were removed after the landing.

Canon: the claim `4bbfc5e`, the work commit `1e5c48a`, the flip `4310514`, the reviewer-base fix `065d784` and the gate-flake diagnosis `43d4c56` — AGENTS.md 71 -> 41 lines (7.4 KB -> 5.2 KB) carrying identity, the ten workflow rules, shared files, trading safety, performance targets and the canon map; the zettatrade skill 87 -> 55 lines (8.9 KB -> 4.1 KB) holding architecture, MVVM and UI, localization and the read-on-demand index; eleven references (315 lines) extracted verbatim into `.opencode/skills/zettatrade/references/` with three point fixes (the dead scaffold item dropped and the list renumbered, the overbroad "NEVER System.Reactive" wording tightened to RxVoid/System.Reactive.Unit); the root file is an 11-line stub index; zt-feature lost the dead Known-debt clause and gained the real gate command, zt-supervisor lost the wedge-watching duplication (a pointer to the supervisor skill remains) and gained `develop` plus `-Base develop`; zt-reviewer rides `develop...<branch>` for its review diffs. Greps verify zero machine-local paths, zero stale markers, UTF-8 without BOM across all seventeen touched files. This branch carried the shared file-tail anchor and landed last of the open batch, rebased per the sync rules. The gate flaked once on this branch (the PluginIndicatorCatalogTests pair failed twice within one run, then passed on standalone reruns): diagnosed as the T-054 fixture leak (BrokenBase riding the copy-local flow into the test output) being loaded and locked by the parallel SDK host test's LoadFrom probe — a pre-existing develop phenomenon, not a docs regression; the fix rides T-148 (feat/fixture-leak, landed first in this batch), after whose landing this gate path is deterministic. Merged into develop by fast-forward. GATE PASSED on the rebased tip (`-AllowProtected` for the AGENTS.md shield). No version bump (docs-only, rule 6).

## T-148 — Kill the BrokenBase fixture leak: the T-054 base assembly never reaches the output directories

- **Branch:** feat/fixture-leak, worktree zt-fixture-leak, based on develop@afb4d52 (claimed 2026-11-10 during the T-147 gate diagnosis on develop@243c50a; re-based as T-103/T-129/T-132 landed and the T-149 claim landed first).
- **Origin:** the T-147 gate flaked twice within one run (PluginIndicatorCatalogTests.Scan_APluginWithMissingDependencies_*, then green on manual reruns); the diagnosis chained three facts: the Broken fixture references BrokenBase without ExcludeAssets="runtime", so the base dll lands in Broken/bin and flows transitively into the Infrastructure.Tests output past the test project's direct exclusion; both plugin scanners load through Assembly.LoadFrom, whose context resolves a missing dependency by probing AppBase — the SDK host test then loads and locks the leaked copy; the two test classes run in parallel (no [Collection] grouping), so the catalog fixture's leak-delete races the lock and dies with an IOException, failing both tests at the Stage step.
- **What:** the BrokenBase reference becomes compile-only in BOTH consumers: the Broken fixture and the Infrastructure.Tests project gain `Private="false"` on their BrokenBase ProjectReference (ExcludeAssets="runtime" alone was verified NOT to stop the copy-local flow on a clean rebuild — Private kills it on both the direct and the transitive path), so the base assembly never reaches any test output while the ProjectReference build-order guarantee stays; the catalog fixture's leak-delete remains as belt-and-suspenders. Two lines of build metadata plus honest comments; no product code, no test code.
- **Scope:** tests/ZettaTrade.Plugins.Broken/ZettaTrade.Plugins.Broken.csproj plus the board. Test-fixture build hygiene: the shipped app does not change, so no version bump per rule 6.
- **Acceptance:** after a clean rebuild ZettaTrade.Plugins.BrokenBase.dll is absent from both Broken/bin and the Infrastructure.Tests output; the full Infrastructure.Tests suite runs green with the lock-race ingredient gone; the T-054 premise (the base is never staged) holds on disk, not only by deletion.
- **Tests:** the targeted rebuild assertions plus the gate over the branch; the gate becomes deterministic on this path.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `c763adf`, the work commit `a89b1fa` and the flip `09ad56e` — two csproj metadata lines (`Private="false"` on both BrokenBase ProjectReferences, `ExcludeAssets="runtime"` kept as the intent marker) kill the deterministic copy of the base assembly: after a clean solution rebuild the leak counts are 0 in Broken/bin and 0 in the Infrastructure.Tests output while Broken.dll still ships (the SDK host test's File.Exists premise), and the full Infrastructure.Tests suite ran 417/417 green pre-commit. The landing re-based onto afb4d52 (the T-103/T-129/T-132 batch and the T-149 claim landed first; T-132's PluginSdkHost changes did not touch the fix targets). Merged into develop by fast-forward. GATE PASSED on the rebased tip. No version bump (test-fixture build hygiene, rule 6).

## T-149 - Stabilize the App.Tests heatmap stale-epoch flake: a completion-driven settle pin

- **Branch:** feat/app-test-flak, worktree zt-app-test-flak, based on develop@cb5e3f9 (first claimed 2026-11-10 on develop@243c50a and re-based twice as T-103/T-129/T-132 and then the T-130..T-164 wave landed mid-flight; the stabilization work starts 2026-11-11 on the user's go).
- **Origin:** the gate's flaky retry keeps absorbing an App.Tests failure: the feat/fixture-leak gate log (2026-11-10) records ZettaTrade.App.Tests.Services.HeatmapFeedServiceTests.AStaleEpochComposition_PublishesNothing failing the first run and passing the retry ("passed on flaky retry; consider stabilizing the timing-sensitive test"); an earlier gate run showed the same rescue, but gate-last.log keeps only the last run, so that occurrence is no longer reproducible from logs. The retry is the safety net, not a fix - the flake is pre-existing on develop and surfaces on unlucky scheduling. Third member of the stabilization family after T-135 and T-138.
- **What:** the fixture's settle pin is a bounded Task.Yield() spin-poll whose budget is calibrated for an idle machine (the while/SpinAbortBudget loop after parkedGate.SetResult()): under the gate's solution-parallel load the pool-starved pipeline outlives the budget and the poll aborts loudly - the recorded FAIL. Make the pin completion-driven instead of spin-driven: await the outcome's own TCS (pumpConnect) behind a timeout guard, so the wait completes exactly when the swap's composition arms the demo pump, a never-landing composition still fails loudly on the timeout, and no spin budget can be exhausted by scheduling noise; the coverage intent stays and the gate's retry remains belt-and-suspenders.
- **Scope:** tests/ZettaTrade.App.Tests only (the HeatmapFeedServiceTests fixture); no product code, no version bump (test stabilization, matching the T-135/T-138 precedent).
- **Acceptance:** a streak of consecutive first-run green runs of the full App.Tests suite (target: ten, zero retry absorptions), the stabilized test keeps its original coverage intent (the stale-epoch drop still asserted through the real pump connection), the gate stops printing the flaky-retry warning on this path.
- **Tests:** the stabilized fixture plus the repeated first-run streak; the gate over the branch.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `a6df1af`, the feat `6e26103` (one feat, no bump - test stabilization, the T-135/T-138 precedent). The pin went completion-driven: `Task.WhenAny(pumpConnect.Task, Task.Delay(SettleTimeout))` with a named `SettleTimeout` (5 s) and the loud diagnostic on a guard miss; the `SpinAbortBudget` constant deleted (dead after the rewrite). The acceptance streak verified by exit codes: TEN consecutive full App.Tests first-run greens, zero failures, zero retry absorptions (the streak script's cosmetic print glitched mid-loop; the verdict ledger is the `dotnet test` exit code and none of the ten failed). Gate PASSED on the first attempt - no flaky-retry warning on the heatmap path; the stale-epoch coverage intent stands (the settle is still the REAL pump connection: the fake's connect TCS completes only after the wrapper's inner subscription is fully formed).

## T-150 — Design tokens: Fluent system brushes out of the views, palette keys only

- **Branch:** feat/design-tokens, worktree zt-design-tokens, based on develop@8395e6f (claimed 2026-11-11 as the first of the T-150…T-162 design-review batch — the two color systems converge first).
- **Origin:** two parallel color systems run side by side: ThemeManager publishes only the palette
  keys, while the views lean on stock Fluent strokes — the status bar paints
  `SystemAccentColorLight2` (the MainWindow status Border), the panel separators and the settings
  cue glyphs ride `SystemControlForegroundBase*`. A user JSON theme recolors none of them: the
  status bar stays the Fluent tint under any palette, and a light custom theme keeps dark-tuned
  separators.
- **What:** every view color rides the palette: the status bar moves to Surface + a top Border
  stroke, the separators and cue glyph colors pick Border/TextSecondary families; ThemeManager
  additionally publishes the derived Fluent system keys from the palette so stock control paths
  converge. No layout or behavior change.
- **Scope:** the App views' brushes, the Livery Themes publish step; no palette values (T-151 owns
  values), no new dependencies.
- **Acceptance:** a fresh theme JSON touching only Background/Surface/Border recolors the status
  bar, the separators and the settings glyphs live; no view keeps a Fluent `System*` brush key;
  hot-reload retints everything without a restart.
- **Tests:** source pins over the views against the forbidden `SystemAccentColor*`/`SystemControl*`
  keys; a ThemeManager test asserting the derived keys ride the palette; the existing contrast
  pins stay green.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `ba62bcb`, the feat `36a1d4e`, the ship `2850c59`
  (0.1.0-alpha.95, the satellite consumption line at Livery 0.1.3). Two-repo choreography: the
  satellite Livery carries the derived-key publish (`2ac3e23`, the tag `v0.1.2`, superseded the
  same day by `d132077`, the tag `v0.1.3` — a re-publish was impossible, the tag existed and the
  feed dedups) and the consumer rides `LiveryVersion` 0.1.3; the satellite's derived facts
  (26 keys, arguably exact blend bytes) live in the consumer's UI.Tests. The hard discovery pin:
  Avalonia 12's property store enforces value-type identity per effectively bound entry — a raw
  Color published over a stock brush key throws
  `InvalidCastException` («Unable to cast object of type 'Avalonia.Media.Color' to type
  'Avalonia.Media.SolidColorBrush'») the moment a `DynamicResource` binding already resolved a
  brush (0.1.3 publishes the `*Brush` family as `SolidColorBrush`, the accent family as Colors).
  Views: the status bar Surface + a top Border stroke, 12 separators/light-queue strokes ride
  Border, the settings cue glyphs ride Border/TextSecondary (`PaletteTokenPinsTests` forbids the
  `SystemAccent`/`SystemControl` substrings across 25 app axaml files and regex-pins the status
  bar). Suite infra: WindowChromeTests' style loader gained the app-world reuse guard — Avalonia
  12 refuses a second AppBuilder setup, and the new publish-pins class stands the world up first.
  Gate triage: attempt 1 failed the two chrome style pins (the unguarded second setup, fixed in
  the feat), attempt 2 tripped `WorkspaceLayoutViewModelTests.TheRuntimeEmits_DriveTheMirror`
  (green 5/5 in isolated Release replay — the known App.Tests load-sensitive timing family,
  absorbed by the gate's flaky retry), attempt 3 PASSED.

## T-151 — Palette contract lint: WCAG pins over every theme file + the Accent role fix

- **Branch:** feat/palette-lint, worktree zt-palette-lint, based on develop@9c2cb0b (claimed 2026-11-11 as the second of the T-150…T-162 design-review batch — the Accent role contract lands right after the token convergence).
- **Origin:** the Accent key lost its role: the dark palette uses it as a barely-visible hover
  fill (contrast vs Surface ~1.4:1), the light one makes the TextBox focus border invisible
  (~1.2:1 vs white), while the auto-created sample theme reads Accent as a loud brand red. A
  palette file can silently ship an unusable hover/focus state — `Livery.Themes.Contrast` already
  has the math, the shipped themes have no battery walking their role pairs.
- **What:** a role contract documented once (surface / interactive hover / focus stroke / text /
  status) and a Livery test battery walking every shipped + user-scanned theme file through
  Contrast.Ratio pins: hover-vs-Surface, TextPrimary-on-hover, focus-vs-Background, the state
  colors as chip fills; dark.json/light.json values re-tuned (Accent becomes an
  interactive-strength token, or the restyling moves hover/focus onto a dedicated key).
- **Scope:** Livery.Themes (docs + tests + Contrast consumers), the themes/*.json values, the
  Controls.axaml selectors if the hover token moves; no control markup.
- **Acceptance:** every shipped theme passes the pin battery; breaking a value (e.g. an invisible
  focus border) fails the gate naming the pair; custom user themes hit the same pins on load.
- **Tests:** the pin battery over the real files (the shipped goldens recorded), plus a negative
  fixture that a broken palette fails loudly.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `13c1b75`, the feat `f715449`, the ship `e264976`
  (0.1.0-alpha.96, the satellite consumption line at Livery 0.1.4). Two-repo choreography: the
  satellite Livery carries the contract itself (`72b6622`, the tag `v0.1.4` — `PaletteContract`
  with the role-shape doc, the battery, and the sample theme dropping the brand-red accent for
  the interactive blue) and the consumer rides `LiveryVersion` 0.1.4; the satellite's own facts
  (healthy baseline, named-pair diagnostics, the missing/unparsable-role loudness) live in
  `Livery.Tests`. The choice inside the task's fork ((a) re-tune vs (b) a dedicated
  hover/focus key): (a) — the Accent becomes the interactive-strength token, no markup moves,
  no new role rolls out; dark #0f3460 → #2563eb (hover 1.36 → 3.27, focus 1.58 → 3.24), light
  #dee2e6 → #3b82f6 (1.24 → 3.56 hover, 1.30 → 3.68 focus, и чёрный TextPrimary на заливке
  держит 4.5), oled #1a1a1a → #2563eb (1.14 → 3.27); midnight and trading-pro already
  answered the contract untouched. The battery pins the five shipped files + the generated
  sample through the real `ThemeManager.InitializeAsync` first-run path (a pre-existing empty
  user directory never triggers the sample branch — the fact creates no directory before the
  manager), and the accent goldens are literal pins, so a rollback fails the gate by name and
  golden. Gate PASSED with one flaky-retry absorption in App.Tests (the known load-sensitive
  timing family); the satellite release overcame one lesson of the T-150 choreography — the
  first-serving check after the feed poll ran while the consumer still pinned the older
  version, so the honest restore check re-ran AFTER the `LiveryVersion` bump.

## T-152 — Trading mode chips readable: dark foreground on the state colors

- **Branch:** fix/lane-chip-contrast, worktree zt-chip-contrast, based on develop@0bdc21f (claimed 2026-11-11 as the third of the T-150…T-162 design-review batch — the readable-chip treatment lands on the converged token system).
- **Origin:** safety-critical polish: the lane chips, the testnet badges and the health dots fill
  with CandleUp/Highlight/Error while their text inherits the default foreground — white over
  #22c55e/#ffd700/#ef4444 lands near ~2:1, far under WCAG for pill-sized type; T-123's
  always-visible lane chip is exactly the element the user must never squint at.
- **What:** a shared readable-chip treatment: a theme-aware dark foreground over the state fills
  (or tinted fills with strong borders), applied to the status-bar lane chip, the ticket's testnet
  badge and the panels' badges — every consumer of the state colors as chip fills.
- **Scope:** the chip/badge templates in MainWindow, NewOrderDialogWindow, PositionsPanelWindow
  and the OrderHistoryWindow copy; the Livery Chip class when T-154 lands; resx untouched.
- **Acceptance:** every state-colored chip's text contrast measures at 4.5:1 or better in all
  shipped themes (the T-151 pins may guard it); the lane stays legible in dark, light, OLED and
  trading-pro at 100% zoom.
- **Tests:** contrast pins per chip state per theme; source pins that no chip template overrides
  the readable foreground.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `b7cd639`, the feat `a29100c`, the ship `45a2533`
  (0.1.0-alpha.97; the satellite consumption line stays at Livery 0.1.4 — the treatment rides
  the package's public `Contrast.Ratio`, no satellite change again). App-local design: the new
  `ChipForegroundPublisher` (an `IAsyncInitializable` the startup walk owns, mirroring the
  application-lifetime ThemeManager) re-derives on every theme apply — it reads the applied
  CandleUp/Highlight/Error fills from the resource world (the source palette keys publish as
  `Color` structs per the T-150 shape), scores the two achromatic candidates against all three
  fills through `Livery.Themes.Contrast.Ratio` and publishes the better worst-case as the
  `ChipForeground` brush (a brush over a new key — the T-150 publishes-as-brush canon). The
  math puts at least one achromatic candidate above 4.5:1 against any single fill; a user
  palette pairing bright and dark chip fills can deny both — best-effort keeps the better
  worst-case, and the shipped set passes everywhere: black worst 5.58:1 (over Error #ef4444)
  on the dark family, white worst 4.83:1 (over light Error #dc2626), against the pre-fix
  ~2.28:1 (white over #22c55e) and 3.79:1 (black over the light Highlight #8a6100). Surfaces:
  the T-123 lane chip's three state classes, the three T-127 testnet-badge copies
  (NewOrderDialog, PositionsPanel, OrderHistory) and the notification count badge — the badge
  dropped its Background-as-text walk-around for the honest equal. The derivation runs inline
  on UI-thread applies and posts from the hot-reload pipeline's background thread; it stays
  silent while the world carries no fills (a no-apply startup keeps the ambient foreground
  rather than a wrong pairing). Tests: `ChipForegroundPublishTests` (pure dark/light worlds,
  the mixed best-effort edge, and the acceptance walk applying all five shipped themes through
  the real manager measuring every chip pair ≥ 4.5:1) plus three `PaletteTokenPinsTests`
  source pins (the marker counts per window, the state fill keys, the dropped walk-around).
  Gate PASSED on attempt 1; smoke 58 log entries, 0 ERR/FTL, ProductVersion
  `0.1.0-alpha.97+45a25332…`.

## T-153 — Status bar declutter: the message channel + the summary, the settings noise out

- **Branch:** feat/status-bar, worktree zt-status-bar, based on develop@b96dee7 (claimed 2026-11-11 as the fourth of the T-150…T-162 design-review batch — the strip sheds the once-a-month noise right after the chips-readability pass).
- **Origin:** eight groups ride the bottom strip (the Ready text, the exchange picker, the active
  exchange, the lane chip, the connection state, the theme name, the language name, the version
  and the health dots); the theme/language/version facts change once a month and eat the reading
  space needed for the actual status messages.
- **What:** the strip keeps: a real message channel (transient event messages over the Ready idle
  text), the exchange picker, the active exchange + lane chip, the connection state, the health
  dots. The theme/language labels move to the settings (both already switch there); the version
  moves to Help/About.
- **Scope:** the MainWindow status Border, the StatusBar ViewModel rows, resx en+ru for the moved
  captions; no exchange/provider contracts.
- **Acceptance:** the strip carries at most the five named groups; an event still surfaces on the
  strip before it vanishes; every removed label stays reachable in one action (settings/About).
- **Tests:** StatusBar VM pins for the message channel's replace/coalesce behavior; a view pin
  asserting the removed bindings are gone.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the claim `b4012d6`, the feat `5462111` (0.1.0-alpha.98). Takeover
  fact: the claim landed from a supervisor session that died right after committing it (no
  drafts on disk) — adopted as-is and executed supervisor-solo (the subagent lane stays closed
  per the 2026-11-09 quota deaths). Design decisions: the channel reads the notification
  center's RAW `Events` lane, not the `Toasts` display lane — the strip is a status surface; the
  per-source mutes and Do-Not-Disturb gate the toasts, never the status facts. Each event
  surfaces its summary through `NotificationRow.ForDisplay` (the row's compose-at-arrival
  discipline: a displayed message never re-renders on a language switch; only the Ready idle
  text re-derives, and the idle re-derivation skips while a message holds via the
  `_messageHeldUntil` guard — the fallback-notice pattern). Replace semantics ride a Switch-ed
  per-event expiry timer: a later event unsubscribes the message it replaces together with that
  message's pending expiry, so a burst coalesces into the displayed latest and one vanishing
  ride covers it (five-second display, test-overridable). The version's one-action home is the
  About/Disclaimer dialog — the existing Help menu item, no new window (the task scope kept the
  surface set): the dialog's view model carries `VersionText` (`AppVersion.Current` through a
  factory DI registration) rendered as a caption+value line; the strip itself went
  five-column (`*,Auto,Auto,Auto,Auto`), health dots at column 4. Removed: the `IThemeManager`
  and `appVersion` constructor dependencies plus the `CurrentThemeName`/`CurrentLanguage`/
  `AppVersion` properties. Tests: the message-channel pin battery in `StatusBarViewModelTests`
  (idle Ready text, surface-then-vanish with the BUG-018 posted-write discipline, the second
  event's replace, the language-switch guard with the re-derived idle text),
  `StatusBarStripPinsTests` view pins (exactly five columns, the removed bindings gone, the
  version reachable in the About dialog), the disclaimer VM's version-line pin, and the
  lane-chip call-count pin narrowed to the lane keys (the ctor's idle-text read is out of that
  pin's scope). Gate PASSED on attempt 1 (`-AllowProtected` for the bump); smoke: alive 15 s,
  ProductVersion `0.1.0-alpha.98+5462111826…`, the run's 88-entry log slice carries 0 ERR/FTL
  (the six WRN entries are the demo-adapter unsupported-watchlist-symbol family plus the
  Binance quote-asset note — logged-by-design degradation, not startup faults).

- **Branch:** feat/type-scale, worktree zt-type-scale, based on develop@6cce2ea (claimed 2026-11-11 as the sixth of the T-150…T-162 design-review batch — the tokens publish as Livery resources, so the satellite release 0.1.6 rides the same task).

  scale (4/8/12/16/24) published as resources from Livery; the monospaced numeric stack (the

  ViewModel changes.
